Who receives itThe addressee, together with the letter.
Differs fromAnnexure: attached at the end of a main document (report, order, form), numbered (Annexure I, II) and referred to in the text; an enclosure is any separate paper sent with a letter. Attachment: the e-mail form of an enclosure.
Example
Yours faithfully, R. Karthik Encl.: 1. Copy of the transfer certificate 2. Copy of the community certificate

6.22 Annexure HIGH-FREQUENCY

DefinitionA document attached at the end of a main document (report, G.O., circular, agreement, application form), numbered Annexure I, Annexure II ... and referred to in the text.
PurposeTo give supporting details (lists, tables, statements, forms) without breaking the flow of the main text.
Who issues itThe author or office that prepares the main document.
Who receives itEveryone who receives the main document; the annexure is a part of it.
Differs fromEnclosure: a separate document sent with a letter, shown as "Encl."; an annexure forms part of the main document and is quoted by number in the text ("vide Annexure II").
Example"The list of selected candidates is given in Annexure I to this order."

6.23 Forwarding letter

DefinitionA letter by which an intermediate officer passes an application or document (received from someone else) to a higher authority, usually with remarks such as "forwarded and recommended".
PurposeTo send an employee's application, representation or proposal up the proper channel, after checking it and adding the forwarding officer's remarks.
Who issues itThe intermediate officer or Head of Office (e.g. a Headmaster forwarding a teacher's application to the District Educational Officer).
Who receives itThe higher or competent authority that will decide.
Differs fromCovering letter: written by the sender of the documents to explain them; a forwarding letter is written by an intermediate officer passing on another person's papers with remarks. Endorsement: a short forwarding note on the paper itself, not a separate letter.
Example
Subject: Request of Thiru S. Vignesh, Junior Assistant, for No Objection Certificate - forwarded Sir/Madam, The application of Thiru S. Vignesh, Junior Assistant of this office, for a No Objection Certificate to appear for a TNPSC examination is sent for your orders. His work and conduct are satisfactory. Forwarded and recommended. Yours faithfully, S. Anitha Tahsildar, Salem

6.24 Covering letter

DefinitionA letter that accompanies enclosed documents and explains them: what is sent, why, and what action is expected.
PurposeTo introduce the enclosures so that they are understood and dealt with correctly (e.g. a job application sent with a resume; an office sending returns, bills or a report).
Who issues itThe sender (owner) of the documents.
Who receives itThe person or office to whom the documents are sent.
Differs fromForwarding letter: written by an intermediate officer passing on someone else's papers with remarks; a covering letter is written by the sender of his or her own documents. (American English: "cover letter".)
Example"I enclose the audited accounts of the Society for 2025-26 for your approval."

6.25 Model formats

Short model formats in full-block style (names and file numbers are illustrative). In each one, note where the salutation and the complimentary close appear - and where they do not. For blank templates and full exam answers, see Sections 9 and 10.

(a) Official letter

Key features: file number, office and date at the top; addressee by designation; Subject and Reference before the salutation; "Sir/Madam,"; first person "I am directed to ..."; "Yours faithfully,"; signed with designation ("for" = signing on behalf of the head of office).

No. 1234/A2/2026 Office of the District Collector, Salem Dated 12 October 2026 To The Commissioner, Salem City Municipal Corporation, Salem - 636 001 Subject: Installation of street lights on Anna Nagar Main Road - approval Reference: Your letter No. 567/H1/2026 dated 30 September 2026 Sir/Madam, I am directed to refer to your letter cited and to inform you that the proposal to install street lights on Anna Nagar Main Road has been approved. You are requested to complete the work within one month and to send a completion report to this office. Yours faithfully, R. Divya for District Collector, Salem

(b) D.O. letter

Key features: the sender's name and designation at the top; "D.O. No." and date; addressed by name ("Dear Thiru Senthil,"; Central Government: "Dear Shri ...,"); personal, first-person tone; "Yours sincerely,"; the addressee's name and address at the bottom left.

S. Arun, I.A.S. District Collector, Salem D.O. No. 1234/A2/2026 Dated 12 October 2026 Dear Thiru Senthil, The repair of the Omalur Main Road has been pending for some months for want of technical clearance from your office. The road is used every day by a large number of school students, and its poor condition has already caused minor accidents. I shall be grateful if you could look into the matter personally and arrange to issue the clearance at an early date. Yours sincerely, S. Arun Thiru P. Senthil, Superintending Engineer, Highways Department, Salem - 636 001

(c) Office Memorandum

Key features: file number, Ministry, place and date at the top; the words "OFFICE MEMORANDUM"; subject; third person "The undersigned is directed to ..."; paragraphs after the first are numbered; no salutation, no close; name and designation; "To" at the bottom left.

No. 1234/A2/2026 Government of India Ministry of [MINISTRY] New Delhi, dated 12 October 2026 OFFICE MEMORANDUM Subject: Nomination of officers for training in office procedure The undersigned is directed to state that a five-day training programme in office procedure will be held in New Delhi from 16 to 20 November 2026. 2. All Ministries/Departments are requested to nominate two Section Officers each for the programme and to send their names to this Ministry by 30 October 2026. S. Lakshmi Under Secretary to the Government of India To All Ministries/Departments of the Government of India

(d) Office Order

Key features: file number, office and date; the words "OFFICE ORDER"; an impersonal order in the third person; no salutation, no close; signed by the Head of Office or "for" him or her; "To" and "Copy to" at the bottom.

No. 1234/A2/2026 Office of the District Collector, Madurai Dated 12 October 2026 OFFICE ORDER Thiru R. Arun, Junior Assistant, Establishment Section, is posted to the Accounts Section with immediate effect. He will hand over charge of his present work to Tmt. K. Meena, Junior Assistant, Establishment Section, before 15 October 2026. V. Suresh for District Collector, Madurai To Thiru R. Arun, Junior Assistant Tmt. K. Meena, Junior Assistant Copy to: Superintendents, Establishment and Accounts Sections; personal files

(e) Circular

Key features: file number, office and date; the word "CIRCULAR"; subject; impersonal wording ("It has been decided ...", "All ... are requested to ..."); no salutation, no close; "To" names the whole group; receipt to be acknowledged.

No. 1234/A2/2026 Office of the District Collector, Madurai Dated 12 October 2026 CIRCULAR Subject: Time-bound disposal of petitions from the public It has been decided that every petition received from the public should be disposed of within 30 days of its receipt. All district-level officers are requested to follow this instruction strictly. 2. A monthly report on the petitions received and disposed of should be sent to this office by the 5th of every month. The receipt of this circular should be acknowledged. K. Priya District Collector, Madurai To All District-level Officers, Madurai District

(f) Notice

Key features: name of the office; the word "NOTICE"; date; a short title in capitals; the message in the third person; name and designation of the issuing authority; no salutation, no close.

OFFICE OF THE DISTRICT COLLECTOR, MADURAI NOTICE 12 October 2026 PUBLIC GRIEVANCE REDRESSAL MEETING Notice is hereby given that a public grievance redressal meeting will be held at the Collectorate, Madurai, on 19 October 2026 at 10 a.m. Members of the public may submit their petitions in person at the meeting. K. Priya District Collector, Madurai

(g) Tamil Nadu Proceedings format

Key features: the heading "PROCEEDINGS OF THE ..."; "Present:" (the officer passing the order); Rc. No. and date; Sub: and Ref:; the word "ORDER:"; the order in the third person; signature and designation; "To" and "Copy to"; no salutation, no close.

PROCEEDINGS OF THE DISTRICT COLLECTOR, MADURAI Present: Tmt. K. Priya, I.A.S. Rc. No. 1234/A2/2026 Dated 12 October 2026 Sub: Establishment - Revenue Department - Transfer of Junior Assistant on request - Orders issued Ref: Request of Thiru R. Karthik, Junior Assistant, Taluk Office, Melur, dated 5 October 2026 ORDER: In the reference cited, Thiru R. Karthik, Junior Assistant, Taluk Office, Melur, has requested a transfer to Usilampatti on family grounds. His request is accepted, and he is transferred and posted as Junior Assistant, Taluk Office, Usilampatti, with immediate effect. K. Priya District Collector, Madurai To Thiru R. Karthik, Junior Assistant, Taluk Office, Melur Copy to: The Tahsildar, Melur; the Tahsildar, Usilampatti

(h) Endorsement at the foot of a letter

Key features: only the end of the letter is shown. The endorsement comes below the signature of the main letter; it repeats the file number and date, says "Copy forwarded to ... for information and necessary action." and is signed again.

I am directed to request you to complete the repairs to the Panchayat Union Middle School building, Pudur, before 30 November 2026. Yours faithfully, V. Suresh for District Collector, Madurai No. 1234/A2/2026, dated 12 October 2026 Copy forwarded to: 1. The Block Development Officer, Madurai East, for information and necessary action. 2. The Headmaster, Panchayat Union Middle School, Pudur, for information. V. Suresh for District Collector, Madurai

The same form is used to return a paper to its sender, e.g. "Returned in original to Thiru S. Vignesh, Junior Assistant, as the application has not been sent through proper channel." On copies of a signed communication, "Sd/-" (= signed) is written before the officer's name to show that the original was signed.

6.26 Quick comparison table

FormSalutation?Complimentary close?Person (1st/3rd)Typical opening wordsSigned as
Official letterYes: "Sir/Madam,"Yes: "Yours faithfully,"1st"I am directed to ..."Name + designation ("for" the head of office when signed on his or her behalf)
D.O. letterYes: "Dear Thiru X," (Central: "Dear Shri X,")Yes: "Yours sincerely,"1st (personal)"Please refer to ..." / "I would like to draw your personal attention to ..."Officer's own name (name and designation printed at the top)
Office MemorandumNoNo3rd"The undersigned is directed to ..."Name + designation
Office OrderNoNo3rd"Thiru X, Assistant, is posted to ..." / "It is hereby ordered that ..."Head of Office, or "for" him or her (name + designation)
OrderNoNo3rd"Sanction is hereby accorded to ..."Competent authority (name + designation)
NotificationNoNo3rd"It is hereby notified that ..."Authorised officer (e.g. Secretary to Government), in the name of the President / Governor
ResolutionNoNo3rd"It has been decided to constitute a Committee ..."Secretary / Joint Secretary to Government (name + designation)
CircularNoNo3rd"It has been decided ..." / "All Heads of Offices are requested to ..."Issuing officer (name + designation)
NoticeNoNo3rd"Notice is hereby given that ..."Issuing authority (name + designation)
EndorsementNoNo3rd (impersonal)"Copy forwarded to ..."Signature + designation, below the endorsement
Memo (internal)NoNo1st or 3rd (direct)"Please refer to ..." / "Please send ..."Sender's name in the "From" line (may be initialled)

Note: a resolution passed at a meeting (recorded in the minutes) is not the same as a Government Resolution published in the Gazette.

6.27 Do not confuse and must remember

DO NOT CONFUSE: Petition = request to an authority for redress or action, often signed by many people (also an application to a court); representation = usually one individual's grievance or request for reconsideration, sent through proper channel.

DO NOT CONFUSE: Memorandum (memo) = short internal note in any organisation (To / From / Date / Subject); Office Memorandum = Government form between Ministries/Departments, in the third person ("The undersigned is directed to ...").

DO NOT CONFUSE: Circular = sent to many offices or persons; notice = displayed or published for people to read; notification = published in the Official Gazette (rules, orders, appointments).

DO NOT CONFUSE: Enclosure = separate paper sent with a letter ("Encl."); annexure = numbered attachment at the end of a main document (Annexure I, II), referred to in its text.

DO NOT CONFUSE: Forwarding letter = written by an intermediate officer passing someone else's application to a higher authority with remarks ("forwarded and recommended"); covering letter = written by the sender to explain his or her own enclosed documents.

DO NOT CONFUSE: Minutes = record of discussions and decisions, written after the meeting and confirmed at the next; agenda = list of items circulated before the meeting with the notice.

DO NOT CONFUSE: D.O. letter = personal, officer to officer by name, "Dear Thiru X," ... "Yours sincerely,"; official letter = impersonal, addressed by designation, "Sir/Madam," ... "Yours faithfully,".

MUST REMEMBER: No salutation and no complimentary close in an Office Memorandum, Office Order, Order, Notification, Resolution, government circular, notice or internal memo.

MUST REMEMBER: "I am directed to ..." = official letter (first person); "The undersigned is directed to ..." = Office Memorandum (third person); "It is hereby notified that ..." = Notification.

MUST REMEMBER: Official letter: "Sir/Madam," + "Yours faithfully,". D.O. letter: "Dear Thiru X," (Central: "Dear Shri X,") + "Yours sincerely,", with a D.O. No. at the top.

MUST REMEMBER: Notifications and Resolutions are published in the Official Gazette; circulars and notices usually are not.

MUST REMEMBER: Order = financial sanctions and disciplinary matters; Office Order = internal administration (distribution of work, leave, postings within the office).

MUST REMEMBER: Tamil Nadu: a G.O. is issued by a Secretariat department; Proceedings (Present: / Rc. No. / Dated / Sub: / Ref: / ORDER:) are issued by Heads of Departments and District officers.

MUST REMEMBER: An employee's application or representation to a higher authority goes through proper channel - the immediate superior forwards it with remarks.

MUST REMEMBER: Endorsement (government) = note at the foot forwarding copies or returning a paper; endorsement (banking) = signature on the back of a cheque to transfer it.

EXAM TIP: Article 350 of the Constitution: every person may submit a representation for the redress of any grievance to any officer or authority of the Union or a State in any of the languages used in the Union or in the State.

EXAM TIP: All executive action of the Union Government is expressed to be taken in the name of the President (Article 77), and that of a State Government in the name of the Governor (Article 166); so Government orders and notifications are issued in their name.

MEMORY TRICK: A comes before M: the Agenda comes before the meeting, the Minutes after it.

MEMORY TRICK: "Demi" means half: a demi-official letter is half official (it is about office work) and half personal (friendly, addressed by name).

7 Letter-Writing Terminology

Key terms in five groups. Learn the meaning AND the correct use; revise the HIGH-FREQUENCY terms first. "(Avoid: ...)" marks outdated or wrong usage; "(Note: ...)" marks a common confusion.

7.1 Parts and layout terms

The parts of a letter in order, then the layout styles. Full anatomy: Section 1; templates: Section 9.

TermSimple meaningExample sentence
A. Top of the letter
LetterheadThe printed heading on an organisation's stationery: name, logo, address, phone, email (and GSTIN for firms).All replies to customers must be printed on the company letterhead.
Sender's addressThe writer's address at the top of the letter. In formal letters write only the address here; your name goes below the complimentary close.If the question gives no address, write "Examination Hall" as the sender's address.
Date lineThe date of writing, placed below the sender's address. Book style: 12 October 2026. (Avoid: all-numeric dates such as 12/10/2026 - they are ambiguous.)The date line reads "12 October 2026", not "12/10/26".
Inside addressThe receiver's designation or name and full address, written after "To" below the date.The inside address must match the address on the envelope.
Reference (Ref.)(1) A line quoting the number and date of an earlier letter, order or notice that this letter follows up; (2) the file number printed at the top of business letters ("Our Ref." / "Your Ref.").Reference: Your letter No. ACC/112/2026 dated 2 October 2026.
Attention lineA line such as "Kind Attention: The Purchase Manager" that directs a letter addressed to a firm to one person or department.Add "Kind Attention: The Purchase Manager" so that the letter reaches the right desk quickly.
Subject (Sub:) HIGH-FREQUENCYA one-line summary of the purpose: a short noun phrase after "Subject:", with no full stop. This book places it before the salutation (after it in the traditional British layout - accepted variant).Subject: Request for a duplicate fee receipt
B. Greeting, body and close
Salutation HIGH-FREQUENCYThe greeting that opens the letter, followed by a comma, e.g. "Dear Sir/Madam,". It must match the complimentary close. (Note: never put "Dear" and "Respected" together - see 8.15.)Use "Dear Sir/Madam," when you do not know the name of the officer.
BodyThe main part of the letter: opening paragraph (why you are writing), main paragraph(s) (facts and details) and closing paragraph (the action you want).Keep the body to three or four short paragraphs.
Complimentary close HIGH-FREQUENCYThe polite phrase before the signature, e.g. "Yours faithfully,": capital Y, small f or s, no apostrophe, comma after. It must match the salutation. Older grammar books call it the "subscription" or "leave-taking"."Dear Sir," takes "Yours faithfully,"; "Dear Mr. Raman," takes "Yours sincerely,".
SignatureThe writer's own handwritten sign, placed below the complimentary close, with the name written clearly under it.In an exam, sign as "ABC" unless the question gives a name.
Signature blockEverything below the close: signature, full name, designation and, in business letters, "For FIRM NAME" above the signature.For Kaveri Paper Mills, the signature, S. Senthil, Sales Manager.
DesignationThe official post of a person (e.g. Branch Manager, Tahsildar). Official letters are usually addressed to the designation, not the name.Address the letter to "The District Collector", not to the officer by name.
C. Below the signature
Enclosure (Encl.) HIGH-FREQUENCYA paper sent in the same envelope as the letter; all enclosures are listed at the foot after "Encl.:".List every enclosure at the foot, e.g. "Encl.: 1. Copy of the fee receipt".
AnnexureA numbered supporting document attached to a letter, report or application and referred to in the text (Annexure I, Annexure II); common in government, legal and tender papers. (Note: books usually say "appendix".)The list of beneficiaries is given in Annexure II.
AttachmentA file sent with an email. (Note: in emails write "attached"; in paper letters write "enclosed".)Please see the attached PDF of the purchase order.
CC (Copy to) HIGH-FREQUENCY"Carbon copy" (now often read as "courtesy copy"): lists the other people who receive a copy; every receiver can see these names. Paper letters usually write "Copy to:".The complaint to the Commissioner ended with "Copy to: The Zonal Officer, Ward 12".
BCC"Blind carbon copy": a copy sent without the other receivers knowing; used mainly in email.The school sent the circular to all parents in BCC to keep their email addresses private.
P.S. (postscript)Latin post scriptum, "written after": a short note added below the signature. (Avoid in formal letters - put the point in the body; fine in informal letters.)P.S. Do bring the photo album when you come for Pongal.
D. Layout styles
Full-block formatA layout in which every line (addresses, date, paragraphs, close) starts at the left margin, with no indentation. All model letters in this book use it.In full-block format, even the date and the complimentary close begin at the left margin.
Semi-block formatAn older layout: the first line of each paragraph is indented, and the date, close and signature are placed towards the right. (Note: accepted variant.)Some older textbooks print their model letters in semi-block format.
From-To formatThe school format widely taught in Tamil Nadu: "From" (sender's name and address) and "To" (receiver's address) at the top, "Thanking you," before the close, "Place:" and "Date:" at the bottom left. (Note: accepted variant - see Section 9.)In the From-To format, the sender's name is written at the top after "From".
Envelope addressThe receiver's address written on the envelope; school exams often ask for it. Older grammar books call it the "superscription".Copy the envelope address exactly from the inside address, with the pin code on the last line.

DO NOT CONFUSE: Enclosure = a paper in the same envelope (listed after "Encl."); Annexure = a numbered supporting document referred to in the text ("see Annexure I"); Attachment = a file sent with an email. CC = copy with names visible to all receivers; BCC = hidden copy (see Section 15).

7.2 Office and procedure words

Words for receiving, recording, checking and deciding letters in government offices, banks and firms. For the forms of official correspondence see Section 6.

TermSimple meaningExample sentence
A. Receiving, recording and sending
CorrespondenceLetters and messages exchanged between persons or offices. (Note: uncountable - not "correspondences" for letters.)Please quote the file number in all future correspondence.
ForwardedSent on to another person or office, usually with a short note; a "forwarding letter" passes on another person's papers with brief remarks (see 6.23).The application is forwarded to the District Educational Officer for necessary action.
Covering letterA short letter sent with documents or goods, stating what is enclosed and why.Send a covering letter with your CV when you apply for a job.
Acknowledgement HIGH-FREQUENCYA written confirmation that a letter, application, payment or goods have been received; also the receipt itself. (Note: "acknowledgment" without the e is also correct, mainly American.)The office issued an acknowledgement with a receipt number for my application.
Dispatch (despatch)Sending letters or goods; also the office section that sends them. (Note: both spellings are correct; "dispatch" is more common.)The reply was dispatched by registered post on 5 October 2026.
DocketA short summary or record entry of a document's details (number, date, sender, subject), written on the paper or its cover or in a register; "docketing" means making this entry.The clerk docketed each letter with its inward number and date before filing it.
Inward registerThe register in which every letter received by an office is entered with a serial (inward) number, date and sender's name.Your letter was entered in the inward register as No. 1452 on 6 October 2026.
Outward registerThe register (also called the dispatch register) that records every letter sent out: number, date, addressee and mode of sending.The outward register shows that the reply was posted on 8 October 2026.
File numberThe number given to the file on a subject; every letter on that subject quotes it so that replies reach the right file.Please quote File No. EST/245/2026 in your reply.
DraftThe first version of a letter, prepared for checking and approval before the final copy is signed.The assistant put up a draft reply for the officer's approval.
Fair copyThe clean final version made after the draft is corrected and approved; this copy is signed and sent.After the officer approved the draft, a fair copy was typed and signed.
B. Requests, complaints and follow-up
RequisitionA formal written request for supplies, staff, services or information, often on a fixed form.The laboratory assistant sent a requisition for chemicals for the next term.
RepresentationA formal written statement of a grievance or claim sent to an authority, asking it to reconsider or act (common in service matters).The teacher submitted a representation to the Director seeking correction of her seniority.
PetitionA formal written appeal to an authority (government, court) for a specific action, often signed by many people.The residents sent a petition signed by 300 families to the Collector for a new bus stop.
GrievanceA complaint about unfair treatment or a problem that affects you.She registered her grievance online and received a reference number.
RedressalSetting right a grievance; the remedy given. (Note: mainly Indian usage; international English uses "redress".)Write to the grievance redressal officer if the branch does not reply.
ReminderA follow-up letter sent when an earlier letter, order or payment has received no response.This is our second reminder; our letter dated 1 September 2026 is still unanswered.
IntimationFormal information or notice about an event or decision. (Note: common in Indian official letters; plain English: "information", "notice".)Please give prior intimation of your visit to the branch.
ClarificationAn explanation that removes a doubt or confusion.The candidate sought clarification on the age limit given in the notification.
ConfirmationA statement that something is true, correct or definitely arranged.Please send written confirmation of the hall booking by email.
Through proper channel HIGH-FREQUENCYSending an employee's application to a higher authority through his or her immediate superior; a "Through: ..." line is written above or below the addressee.Teachers must send transfer requests through proper channel, that is, through the Headmaster.
C. Permission and proof
Authorization (authorisation)Official permission for a person to act, or the document that gives it (an authority letter).The account holder gave written authorization to her son to collect the cheque book.
VerificationChecking that facts or documents are true and correct.Original certificates will be returned after verification.
CertificationAn official written statement that something is true or meets a standard; also the certificate itself.The form needs certification by the head of the institution.
Attested copyA photocopy signed as a true copy of the original by an authorised person (e.g. a Gazetted Officer or notary). (Note: many offices now accept self-attested copies.)Enclose attested copies of your mark sheets.
AffidavitA written statement made on oath or affirmation before a notary or oath commissioner.The applicant filed an affidavit stating that the original certificate was lost.
UndertakingA written promise to do or not to do something (not made on oath).Every student gave an undertaking to follow the hostel rules.
TestimonialA written statement about a person's character, conduct or ability, given by a teacher or employer. (Note: in business it also means a customer's praise of a product.)The Principal gave her a testimonial for the scholarship application.
D. Decisions and orders
RecommendationA suggestion that something be done; in offices, a lower officer's written support for a request, sent to the authority that decides.The Headmaster forwarded the leave letter with his recommendation.
Endorsement HIGH-FREQUENCY(Government) a note at the foot of a communication forwarding copies ("Copy forwarded to ... for information / necessary action") or returning a paper to its sender. (Banking) a signature on the back of a cheque or other negotiable instrument to transfer it.The endorsement at the foot read: "Copy forwarded to the Tahsildar for necessary action."
ApprovalFormal agreement that a plan, request or proposal may go ahead (the general word).The proposal was sent to the Director for approval.
Sanction HIGH-FREQUENCYFormal authorisation by the competent authority, especially for spending money (financial sanction); also for leave, loans or posts. (Note: in the news, "sanctions" also means penalties on a country - a different meaning.)The Government has accorded sanction for Rs. 25 lakh for the new school building.
Ratification HIGH-FREQUENCYFormal approval of an action ALREADY taken, e.g. an urgent decision confirmed later by the council or board.The purchase made during the flood emergency was placed before the council for ratification.
ComplianceActing as a rule, order or request requires; a "compliance report" states the action taken.Please send a compliance report on the audit objections within 15 days.
Non-complianceFailure to follow a rule, order or request; it may lead to a penalty.Non-compliance with this order will lead to disciplinary action.

MEMORY TRICK: Sanction comes BEFORE (permission to spend or act); Ratification comes AFTER (approval of an action already taken). Recommendation = a lower officer supports a request; approval or sanction = the higher authority says yes.

7.3 Formal linking words

Connectors used in official, legal and business letters. Use the common ones freely; use the "here-/there-" words and Latin forms sparingly - plain English is clearer.

TermSimple meaningExample sentence
A. Linking to a topic or an earlier letter
RegardingAbout; on the subject of. (Note: "regarding" already means "about" - never write "regarding about".)I am writing regarding the delay in issuing my community certificate.
ConcerningAbout; relating to (slightly more formal than "about").We have received several complaints concerning the new billing system.
With regard toAbout; in connection with. (Note: "with regard to" or "as regards" - NOT "with regards to".)With regard to your request for a refund, we have credited Rs. 1,200 to your account.
With reference toLinks your letter to an earlier letter, advertisement or notice.With reference to your advertisement in The Hindu dated 5 October 2026, I wish to apply for the post of Accountant.
In response toAs a reply to (a letter, request, inquiry or advertisement).In response to your inquiry dated 2 October 2026, we enclose our latest price list.
Further toFollowing on from (an earlier letter, call or meeting).Further to our telephone conversation this morning, I confirm our order for 50 chairs.
B. Rules, conditions and reasons
Pursuant toIn accordance with; as required by (a rule, order or agreement).Pursuant to clause 7 of the agreement, we are returning the damaged goods.
In accordance withFollowing; in the way required by (rules, instructions, a contract).The goods have been packed in accordance with your instructions.
As perAccording to. (Note: common in Indian business letters; "according to" or "as stated in" is plainer.)As per your order dated 3 October 2026, we have dispatched 100 cartons.
Subject toDepending on a condition; only if something else happens.The appointment is subject to verification of original certificates.
In view ofBecause of; considering.In view of the heavy rain, the camp has been postponed.
NotwithstandingIn spite of; even though.Notwithstanding the holiday, the fee counter will remain open.
In lieu ofInstead of; in place of.Staff who worked on the holiday will get a day off in lieu of it.
C. Acting for others and dates
On behalf ofAs the representative of; for (another person or organisation).I am writing on behalf of the Residents' Welfare Association, K.K. Nagar.
Per pro (p.p.) HIGH-FREQUENCYLatin per procurationem, "by agency": used when a person signs a letter on behalf of another person who has authorised it. (Note: p.p. is now usually written before the name of the person being signed for.)In the manager's absence, his assistant signed the letter "p.p. R. Suresh, Manager".
With effect from (w.e.f.) HIGH-FREQUENCYFrom the stated date, when an order or change starts to apply. (Note: never "w.e.f. from" - "from" is already inside.)Thiru R. Arun is transferred to Salem with effect from 1 November 2026.
HenceforthFrom now on.Henceforth, all leave applications must be submitted online.
inst. / ult. / prox.Old commercial short forms: instant = this month, ultimo = last month, proximo = next month. (Avoid: write the month name.)Write "your letter of 5 October", not "your letter of the 5th inst."
D. "Here-" and "there-" words
HerewithWith this letter. (Note: "Please find enclosed herewith" is redundant - write "Please find enclosed" or "I enclose".)I send herewith a copy of the lease deed.
HerebyBy this (statement or document); used when the words themselves perform the act.I hereby declare that the information given above is true.
HeretoTo this (document).The terms of supply are given in the schedule attached hereto.
HereinafterFrom this point on in this document; used to introduce a short name.Kaveri Paper Mills (hereinafter called "the Supplier") shall deliver the paper by 30 November 2026.
ThereofOf that; of it (the thing just mentioned).The contract, or any part thereof, shall not be transferred.
ThereinIn that (place, document or matter).Read the notification and follow the instructions given therein.
AforesaidMentioned earlier in the same document.The aforesaid amount of Rs. 5,000 will be refunded within 30 days.
AforementionedMentioned before; same meaning as aforesaid. (Note: "the above" or simply "this" is often plainer.)The aforementioned candidates should report for certificate verification on Monday.
E. Latin short forms
Vide HIGH-FREQUENCYLatin for "see / refer to": points the reader to an order, letter, page or annexure. (Note: officialese; Indian letters often use it to cite an order - "vide G.O. No. 45 dated ..." means "see / under G.O. No. 45".)The revised rates are given in the table (vide Annexure II).
Re:"In the matter of; about" (from Latin); email programs add "Re:" to the subject of a reply. (Note: in formal letters write "Subject:".)Re: Your application for a new electricity connection
Inter aliaLatin for "among other things".The report recommends, inter alia, a new water tank for the school.
Viz.Latin videlicet, "namely": introduces the exact items just mentioned; read it aloud as "namely".Two documents, viz. the invoice and the delivery challan, are missing.

EXAM TIP: Latin in letters: vide = see; viz. = namely; inter alia = among other things; re = in the matter of; p.p. (per pro) = signed on behalf of; P.S. (post scriptum) = written after. The "here-" words point to THIS document (herewith, hereby, hereto); the "there-" words point to THAT thing just mentioned (thereof, therein).

7.4 Polite formula expressions

Fixed polite expressions for requesting, informing, enclosing and closing. Section 8 gives many more phrases, arranged by purpose.

TermSimple meaningExample sentence
A. Requesting
KindlyA polite word for "please" in requests. (Note: use it once; do not add "please" as well, and never write "kindly be informed".)Kindly issue me a duplicate hall ticket before 20 October 2026.
I request you toA formal way to ask for an action. (Note: not "request you to please"; the verb takes no "for": "I request leave", not "I request for leave".)I request you to grant me leave for three days from 14 October 2026.
I shall be grateful ifA very polite formal request, usually followed by "you would" or "you could".I shall be grateful if you would send me the prospectus for 2027.
I shall be obliged ifSame as "I shall be grateful if"; very formal.I shall be obliged if you could refund the excess amount of Rs. 450.
At your earliest convenienceAs soon as you can (polite). (Note: vague - give a date when the matter is urgent.)Please let me know at your earliest convenience whether the hall is available.
Please acknowledge receiptA request to confirm that the letter, payment or goods have reached the receiver.Please acknowledge receipt of this letter and the enclosed cheque.
B. Informing
We wish to inform you thatA neutral formal way to give information.We wish to inform you that our office has moved to Anna Nagar, Chennai.
We are pleased to inform you thatThe formal way to give good news.We are pleased to inform you that you have been selected for the post of Clerk.
We regret to inform you thatThe formal way to give bad news.We regret to inform you that the book you ordered is out of print.
This is to bring to your notice thatDraws an authority's attention to a problem; used in complaints and reports.This is to bring to your notice that the street lights on Gandhi Road have not worked for two weeks.
I am directed toThe opening of an official Government letter, written on the orders of a higher authority.I am directed to inform you that your request for transfer has been accepted.
This is to certify thatThe standard opening of a certificate or testimonial.This is to certify that Selvi R. Kavya studied in this school from 2020 to 2026.
C. Enclosing and closing
Please find enclosed HIGH-FREQUENCYThe standard phrase for sending papers with a letter. (Note: do not add "herewith"; a modern alternative is "I enclose".)Please find enclosed a copy of my mark sheet.
I look forward to hearing from youThe standard polite closing sentence. (Note: "look forward to" + -ing: "hearing", not "hear".)I look forward to hearing from you soon.
D. Handle with care
Please be informedAn old formula for giving information. (Avoid: write "This is to inform you that" or "I wish to inform you that".)Better: "This is to inform you that the office will remain closed on 2 October 2026."
Do the needful HIGH-FREQUENCYIndian officialese for "take the necessary action". (Avoid: vague and dated - say exactly what you want done.)Better: "Please take the necessary action to repair the pipeline."
Thanking youA stand-alone line before the close. (Note: accepted Indian usage; better modern style is "Thank you for your time." inside the last paragraph.)Thank you for your prompt attention to this matter.

7.5 Commercial and banking words

Words used in business and bank letters; the meanings match Sections 3 to 5. For cheque dishonour, stop-payment and transport documents see Sections 4 and 5.

TermSimple meaningExample sentence
A. Buying and selling
Quotation HIGH-FREQUENCYThe seller's statement of prices, terms and conditions, sent in reply to an inquiry before any order; it is valid for a stated period (a firm offer).Our quotation is valid for 30 days from the date of this letter.
EstimateAn approximate statement of cost; the final bill may differ.The contractor gave an estimate of Rs. 2 lakh for repairing the roof.
TenderA formal, often sealed, offer to supply goods or do work, made in response to an invitation, usually from a public body.The Corporation has invited sealed tenders for road repair work.
Purchase Order (PO)A formal numbered document issued by the buyer to order goods; binding once the seller accepts it.Please quote our PO No. 2026/118 on the invoice and the delivery challan.
Proforma invoiceA preliminary bill sent before supply, for advance payment or import formalities.Kindly send a proforma invoice so that we can arrange advance payment.
Invoice (tax invoice)The bill issued by the seller for goods supplied, showing quantity, price, taxes, total and due date.Payment is due within 30 days of the invoice date.
E&OE"Errors and Omissions Excepted": printed on invoices and quotations so that the seller may correct mistakes later.The words "E&OE" at the foot of the invoice allow the seller to correct clerical errors.
ConsignmentA batch of goods sent to a buyer or agent. (Note: consignor = sender; consignee = receiver.)The consignment of 200 cartons was dispatched by lorry today.
Credit noteIssued by the SELLER to the buyer; it reduces the amount the buyer owes (goods returned, overcharge, allowance).We have issued a credit note for Rs. 600 for the damaged items.
Debit noteIssued by the BUYER to the seller when returning goods or claiming a deduction; it tells the seller that his account has been debited. (Note: under GST law both notes are issued by the supplier.)We are returning 10 defective fans with a debit note for Rs. 12,000.
B. Payments and dues
RemittanceMoney sent to someone, usually by bank transfer, draft or cheque; also the act of sending it.We acknowledge with thanks your remittance of Rs. 15,000 by NEFT.
DuesMoney that must be paid (fees, charges, bills).Please clear your hostel dues before the examination.
ArrearsPayments that are overdue and still unpaid; also pay due from an earlier date (e.g. salary arrears after a pay revision).The tenant is two months in arrears with his rent.
Outstanding(Of an amount) still unpaid; not yet settled. (Note: it also means "excellent" - the context decides.)An amount of Rs. 8,400 is still outstanding against your account.
InstalmentOne part of a debt or price paid at regular intervals. (Note: EMI = Equated Monthly Instalment; American spelling "installment".)The loan is repayable in 24 monthly instalments.
DefaultFailure to repay a loan or pay a bill on time.If the borrower defaults, the bank may recover the amount from the guarantor.
C. Banking and loans
OverdraftA bank facility that lets an account holder (usually of a current account) withdraw more than the balance, up to an agreed limit; interest is charged on the amount overdrawn.The firm applied for an overdraft of Rs. 5 lakh to meet festival-season expenses.
CollateralAn asset (land, gold, deposits) pledged to a lender as security for a loan; the lender may sell it if the loan is not repaid.The bank asked for the house documents as collateral for the loan.
HypothecationA charge on movable property (such as a vehicle or stock) given as security for a loan while the borrower keeps using it.The registration certificate shows the car as hypothecated to the bank until the loan is repaid.
GuarantorA person who promises to repay a loan if the borrower fails to. (Note: in law the guarantor is called the "surety".)My uncle signed the loan papers as guarantor.
NOC (No Objection Certificate)A certificate stating that the issuing authority has no objection to a proposed action.After the car loan was closed, the bank issued an NOC to remove the hypothecation.
KYC (Know Your Customer)The process by which a bank verifies a customer's identity and address with documents.The bank asked me to update my KYC by submitting a copy of my Aadhaar card.
NomineeThe person named by an account holder to receive the money in the account after the holder's death.Please register my wife as the nominee for my savings account.
Demand draft (DD)A prepaid instrument issued by a bank, payable to a named payee; it cannot bounce for lack of funds.The application fee of Rs. 500 must be paid by demand draft.
Letter of Credit (L/C)A bank's written undertaking, issued at the buyer's (importer's) request, to pay the seller (exporter) when specified documents are presented; used mainly in foreign trade.The importer opened a letter of credit in favour of the exporter in Singapore.

DO NOT CONFUSE: Quotation (firm prices) / Estimate (approximate cost) / Tender (formal, often sealed, offer); Proforma invoice (before supply) / Invoice (after supply); Credit note (from the seller) / Debit note (from the buyer). See Section 15.

8 High-Frequency Formal Phrases

Ready-made phrases for each job a letter does. Learn the Formal ones for exams; Semi-formal and Informal ones are shown only for contrast (full comparison: Section 12).

8.1 How to judge register

Register means the level of formality. Decide it from WHO will read the letter and WHY you are writing. Officials, organisations, editors and strangers: formal. People you know but must respect (a neighbour, landlord, colleague or former teacher): semi-formal. Family and friends: informal. When unsure in an exam, choose formal.

SignalFormalSemi-formalInformal
ReaderOfficials, firms, editors, unknown personsKnown persons you respectFamily and friends
SalutationDear Sir/Madam, / Sir, / Respected Sir, (named person: Dear Mr. Raman,)Dear Mr. Raman,Dear Priya, / Dear Appa,
CloseYours faithfully, (named person: Yours sincerely,)Yours sincerely,Your loving friend, / With love,
Subject lineAlwaysOptionalNever
Contractions (I'm, don't)NeverUsually avoidedAllowed
Tone and wordsImpersonal, precise: request, inform, regret, enclosePolite, personal: I would like, please let me knowChatty: can you, thanks a lot, see you soon
Exclamation marksNeverRarelyAllowed

8.14 Same message in three registers

Each row says the same thing three ways. In exam letters, use only the first column.

FormalSemi-formalInformal
Thank you for your letter dated 3 October 2026.Thank you for your letter of 3 October.Thanks for your letter. It was lovely to hear from you.
I shall be grateful if you would send me details of the course.Could you please send me details of the course?Can you send me the course details?
We are pleased to inform you that your application has been approved.I am happy to tell you that your application has been approved.Great news: your application's been approved!
We regret to inform you that the post has already been filled.I am sorry to tell you that the post has already been filled.Sorry, but the job's already gone.
Please accept our apologies for the delay in replying.I am sorry for the delay in replying.Sorry I took so long to write back.
Please find enclosed a copy of the receipt.I am enclosing a copy of the receipt.I've put a copy of the receipt in with this letter.
I wish to complain about the poor quality of the service.I was disappointed with the service.The service was really bad.
You are cordially invited to the annual day celebrations on 10 January 2027.We would be delighted if you could join us for our annual day on 10 January.Do come for our annual day on 10 January. It'll be fun!
I regret that I shall not be able to attend the function owing to a prior engagement.I am sorry that I will not be able to attend the function because of a prior commitment.Sorry, I can't come. I've already got plans that day.
I request you to grant me an appointment at your convenience.Could we meet sometime next week?Let's meet up next week.
This is to remind you that the payment of Rs. 5,000 is overdue.I would like to remind you that the payment of Rs. 5,000 is still due.Don't forget you still owe me Rs. 5,000!
I am extremely grateful for your assistance.Thank you very much for your help.Thanks a lot for helping me out.
Please do not hesitate to contact me if you require any further information.Please let me know if you need anything else.Give me a call if you need anything.

8.15 Outdated or wrong phrases to avoid

Letter questions and error-spotting questions often test these. The left column shows the wrong or outdated form - never copy it.

AvoidUse insteadWhy
Please do the needful.Please take the necessary action. / Say exactly what you want: "Please issue the certificate."Vague, dated Indian officialese; the reader has to guess what "needful" means.
Kindly revert back. / I will revert back.Please reply. / I will reply by Friday."Revert" means "go back to an earlier state", so "back" repeats it; using "revert" for "reply" is Indian usage that other readers may not follow.
Kindly be informed that ... / Please be informed that ...This is to inform you that ... / I wish to inform you that ...A stiff, old-fashioned passive formula.
Please find enclosed herewith ...Please find enclosed ... / I enclose ..."Enclosed" and "herewith" say the same thing twice.
We have received your letter and will reply to the same shortly.We have received your letter and will reply to it shortly."The same" is not a pronoun in modern English; use it, them or this.
Thanking you in anticipation,Thank you for considering my request.Dated, and it presumes that the reader will agree. (The plain "Thanking you," line is accepted Indian usage.)
The meeting has been preponed to Monday.The meeting has been brought forward to Monday."Prepone" is Indian English: understood in India, not internationally.
My cousin brother / cousin sister will collect the documents.My cousin Arun / My cousin Meena will collect the documents."Cousin" already covers both male and female; add the name if you need to show who.
I am having two years of experience.I have two years of experience."Have" meaning possession is a state verb; it is not used in the -ing form.
We discussed about the fee hike.We discussed the fee hike."Discuss" takes a direct object - no "about" (but "a discussion about" is correct).
I request you to please grant me leave.I request you to grant me leave. / Please grant me leave.Double politeness: "request" is already polite; use one polite word only.
Respected Sir, ... Yours sincerely,Respected Sir, ... Yours faithfully,Salutation and close must match: Sir/Madam forms take "Yours faithfully"; "Yours sincerely" goes with "Dear Mr./Ms. + name".
Your's faithfully, / Yours Faithfully,Yours faithfully,"Yours" is a possessive pronoun and never takes an apostrophe; capital Y, small f, comma after.
Dear Respected Sir,Respected Sir, / Dear Sir,"Dear" and "Respected" do the same job; use only one of them.
I beg to state that ...I wish to state that ... / I am writing to ...Old-fashioned and over-humble; modern formal English is polite but direct.
with regards to your letterwith regard to your letter / regarding your letterThe fixed phrase is "with regard to"; "regards" means good wishes ("Kind regards").
Please reply at your earliest.Please reply at your earliest convenience. / Please reply by 20 October 2026.Incomplete: "at your earliest" needs the noun "convenience". ("At the earliest" alone, meaning "as soon as possible", is accepted Indian usage; in standard English it usually follows a time: "on Monday at the earliest".)
I request for two days' leave.I request two days' leave. / I request you to grant me two days' leave.The verb "request" takes no "for"; the noun does ("a request for leave").

MUST REMEMBER: Match the close to the salutation: "Dear Sir/Madam,", "Sir," or "Respected Sir," → "Yours faithfully,"; "Dear Mr. Raman," → "Yours sincerely,". Write "Yours" with no apostrophe, and never put "Dear" and "Respected" together.

MUST REMEMBER: Formal letters use full, polite and precise phrases - "I request you to ...", "I shall be grateful if ...", "Please find enclosed ...", "on or before (date)", "I look forward to hearing from you." Never write "do the needful", "revert back", "please be informed" or "enclosed herewith", and use no contractions, slang or exclamation marks.

9 Letter Format Templates

This section gives 17 fill-in templates, one for each main letter type. Each template is a complete skeleton: the layout, ready-made opening, main and closing sentences, and the correct salutation and close. Learn the skeleton; in the exam, replace every placeholder with the facts given in the question. Fully worked answers are in Section 10, phrases for the body in Section 8, and errors to avoid in Section 11.

9.0 How to Use These Templates

  1. Read the situation: who writes, to whom, and why. This decides the register - formal, semi-formal or informal (see Section 12).
  2. Pick the template from the Template finder below.
  3. Replace every placeholder with facts from the question. A slash inside the brackets means "choose one"; words after a dash are hints. Never leave a bracket in your answer.
  4. Keep the ready-made sentences of the opening and closing paragraphs; build the main paragraph from the points given in the question.
  5. Delete the optional lines you do not need (see the Optional lines table).
  6. Final check: date style, "To" block, Subject before the salutation, matching salutation and close, your name below the close.

Placeholder legend

PlaceholderWhat to write
A. Addresses and date
[SENDER'S ADDRESS]Your door number, street and area - one item per line, each line ending with a comma. Do not write your name here. Exam: "Examination Hall" if the question gives no address.
[CITY - PIN CODE]The last line of any address, with no comma and no full stop: Madurai - 625 020
[DATE]Day, month in words, year: 12 October 2026. ("12th October, 2026" is the traditional Indian style; avoid 12/10/2026 - all-numeric dates are ambiguous.)
[RECEIVER'S DESIGNATION]The post, not the person's name, written after "The": The Commissioner, / The Branch Manager, / The Principal,
[ORGANISATION]The office, company, bank or institution: Madurai City Municipal Corporation, / M/s. Kaveri Traders, (M/s. = Messrs, written before a firm's name)
[RECEIVER'S ADDRESS]The receiver's street and area, ending with a comma; the next line is [CITY - PIN CODE]. For a newspaper, write only the city.
B. Subject, reference and body
[SUBJECT]A short noun phrase that shows the whole purpose, with no full stop: Complaint regarding irregular garbage collection in K.K. Nagar
[REFERENCE NUMBER]The number of the earlier letter, notice, order or advertisement you are answering ("Reference: Your notice No. ... dated ..."), or your own file number in an office or business letter ("Ref. No.: ...")
[PURPOSE]Why you are writing, in one clause: request a duplicate mark sheet
[PROBLEM]The problem in one clause: the street lights on our road have not worked for two weeks
[REASON]Why you need it: my father has been admitted to hospital
[DETAILS]Facts only - who, what, where, when, how much. Short sentences, one idea per paragraph.
[ACTION REQUIRED]The exact action you want: replace the defective fan / issue a new passbook
C. Money and numbers
[ACCOUNT NUMBER]As given in the question; mask it where sensible (XXXXXXXX4521). Never write a PIN, OTP, CVV or password.
[AMOUNT]In figures, and in money letters in words as well: Rs. 12,500 (Rupees twelve thousand five hundred only)
[PO NUMBER] / [INVOICE NUMBER]Exactly as printed on the Purchase Order or the invoice
[GSTIN]The firm's 15-character GST Identification Number (Tamil Nadu numbers begin with 33); model letters show it as 33XXXXXXXXXX1Z5
D. Signature block
[SIGNATURE]Real letter: your handwritten signature above your name. Exam: never sign your real name (see the identity rule below).
[NAME]Your full name below the close (informal letters: first name only). Exam: the name given in the question, otherwise "ABC" / "XYZ".
[DESIGNATION]Your post, when you write for an office or a firm: Purchase Manager / Junior Assistant
[ENCLOSURES]Documents sent with the letter, numbered, one per line: 1. Copy of the invoice / 2. Photographs of the defect. Leave out the Encl. line if nothing is enclosed.
[COPY RECEIVER]Designation and office of each person who gets a copy, with the purpose: The Assistant Engineer, Ward 12 - for information and necessary action
E. Personal and employment letters
[SURNAME]The name written after the title in semi-formal letters: Dear Mr. Raman, / Dear Dr. Meena, (for a name written with an initial, like R. Karthik: Dear Mr. Karthik,)
[FIRST NAME]Informal letters only: Dear Priya, - and your own first name at the end
[POST]The post applied for, held or appointed to: Junior Assistant / Sales Executive
[FROM DATE] / [TO DATE]Start and end of a leave or service period, both written in full (12 October 2026)
[NOTICE PERIOD]As fixed in the terms of appointment: one month / three months

Optional lines - keep them only when needed

LineKeep it only whenWhere it goes
Reference: Your ... No. ... dated ...You reply to, or refer to, an earlier letter, notice, order or advertisementAfter the Subject, before the salutation
Ref. No.: ...An office or a firm writes (its own file number)Below the letterhead, above the date
Kind Attention: ...A letter to a firm must reach one particular officerAfter the receiver's address, before the Subject
Through: ...An employee writes to a higher authority "through proper channel" (via the immediate superior)Above or below the addressee
Subject: ... (semi-formal)Optional in semi-formal letters; never used in informal lettersBefore the salutation
For [FIRM NAME]You sign on behalf of a firmJust below the complimentary close
[DESIGNATION]You write in an official capacityBelow your name
Encl.: ...Something is actually enclosedBelow the signature block
Copy to: ... / CC: ...Copies go to other persons or officesLast line, below Encl.

MUST REMEMBER: EXAM IDENTITY RULE - if the question does not give a name or address, write "Examination Hall" as the address and sign as "ABC" or "XYZ". Never reveal your real identity in an exam answer. If the question gives a name and address, use exactly those.

9.1 Formal Letter

Use for: Any letter to an official, office or organisation you do not know personally - the master pattern for the formal templates that follow.

[SENDER'S ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE] [DATE] To The [RECEIVER'S DESIGNATION], [ORGANISATION], [RECEIVER'S ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE] Subject: [SUBJECT] Reference: Your [LETTER / NOTICE] No. [REFERENCE NUMBER] dated [DATE] Dear Sir/Madam, I am writing to [PURPOSE]. I am a [RESIDENT / CUSTOMER / STUDENT] of [PLACE / ORGANISATION]. [DETAILS - WHAT HAPPENED, WHERE AND WHEN]. [SUPPORTING FACTS - DATES, NUMBERS, ACTION ALREADY TAKEN]. This has caused [EFFECT ON YOU OR THE PUBLIC]. I therefore request you to [ACTION REQUIRED] at the earliest. Thank you for your time and attention. Yours faithfully, [SIGNATURE] [NAME] Encl.: [ENCLOSURES] Copy to: [COPY RECEIVER]

EXAM TIP: Learn this skeleton first - the other formal templates change only the Subject, the body sentences and a few optional lines. Your name goes below "Yours faithfully,", never above the address.

Accepted variant - the "From-To" format (widely taught in Tamil Nadu schools). This book's model letters use the full-block format above, but the From-To layout below is also correct.

From [NAME], [SENDER'S ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE] To The [RECEIVER'S DESIGNATION], [ORGANISATION], [RECEIVER'S ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE] Respected Sir/Madam, Sub: [SUBJECT] [BODY - OPENING, MAIN AND CLOSING PARAGRAPHS] Thanking you, Yours faithfully, [NAME] Place: [PLACE] Date: [DATE] Address on the envelope: To The [RECEIVER'S DESIGNATION], [ORGANISATION], [RECEIVER'S ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE]

DO NOT CONFUSE: From-To format = your name at the top, salutation BEFORE "Sub:", a "Thanking you," line, Place and Date at the bottom-left. Full-block format (this book) = date at the top, Subject BEFORE the salutation, name only below the close, thanks inside the last paragraph. Both are accepted - never mix the two in one letter.

9.2 Semi-formal Letter

Use for: A person you know but must address with respect - a teacher, neighbour, landlord, a friend's parent or a senior colleague (thanks, request, apology, invitation).

[SENDER'S ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE] [DATE] To Mr./Mrs. [RECEIVER'S FULL NAME], [RECEIVER'S ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE] Subject: [SUBJECT] Dear Mr./Mrs. [SURNAME], I hope you and your family are doing well. I am writing to [PURPOSE - THANK YOU / ASK FOR YOUR HELP / APOLOGISE / INVITE YOU]. [DETAILS - WHAT HAPPENED OR WHAT YOU NEED, WHEN AND WHY]. [PERSONAL TOUCH - WHY THEIR HELP OR PRESENCE MATTERS TO YOU]. I would be grateful if you could [ACTION REQUIRED]. Thank you for your [KINDNESS / HELP / UNDERSTANDING]. Please convey my regards to [FAMILY MEMBERS]. Yours sincerely, [SIGNATURE] [FULL NAME]

EXAM TIP: Semi-formal = title + name ("Dear Mr. Raman,") with "Yours sincerely,"; a bare first name would make it informal. Be polite and warm, but use no slang or contractions; the Subject line is optional.

9.3 Informal Letter

Use for: Family members and close friends - sharing news, congratulating, advising, inviting, thanking or consoling.

[SENDER'S SHORT ADDRESS], [CITY] [DATE] Dear [FIRST NAME / RELATION], I hope you and everyone at home are fine. I was very happy to get your letter and to hear that [THEIR NEWS]. I'm sorry I couldn't reply earlier - [SHORT REASON]. I'm writing to tell you about [MAIN NEWS / EVENT]. [DETAILS - WHAT HAPPENED, WHO WAS THERE, HOW YOU FELT]. [ADVICE / INVITATION / REQUEST]. Why don't you [SUGGESTION - COME AND STAY WITH US THIS VACATION]? Please give my regards to [ELDERS] and my love to [YOUNGER ONES]. Do write back soon. Your loving [FRIEND / SON / DAUGHTER], [FIRST NAME]

EXAM TIP: No "To", no receiver's address and no Subject; sign with your first name only. Contractions are fine here, but avoid slang and SMS language in an exam answer.

9.4 Business Letter

Use for: Letters written on behalf of a firm to another firm or to a customer - inquiries, quotations, orders, complaints, adjustments, credit and collection.

[FIRM NAME IN CAPITALS] [FIRM ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE] Phone: [PHONE NUMBER] | Email: [EMAIL ADDRESS] GSTIN: [GSTIN] Ref. No.: [REFERENCE NUMBER] [DATE] To M/s. [RECEIVER FIRM NAME], [RECEIVER'S ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE] Kind Attention: [OFFICER'S NAME AND DESIGNATION] Subject: [SUBJECT] Dear Sir/Madam, We are writing to [PURPOSE], with reference to [YOUR LETTER / YOUR QUOTATION / OUR ORDER] No. [NUMBER] dated [DATE]. [DETAILS - GOODS OR SERVICE, QUANTITIES, PRICES, DATES, TERMS]. [WHAT YOU OFFER, NEED OR PROPOSE]. We shall be glad if you could [ACTION REQUIRED] by [DATE]. For any clarification, please contact [CONTACT PERSON] at [PHONE NUMBER]. We look forward to your early reply. Yours faithfully, For [FIRM NAME] [SIGNATURE] [NAME] [DESIGNATION] Encl.: [ENCLOSURES] CC: [COPY RECEIVER]

EXAM TIP: The firm speaks, so write "we", not "I". Ref. No. at the top is your own file number; the number of the letter, quotation or order you are answering goes into the first sentence.

9.5 Complaint Letter

Use for: Reporting a defective product, poor service, delay, shortage or wrong goods to a company, shop or service provider, and asking for a remedy.

[SENDER'S ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE] [DATE] To The [RECEIVER'S DESIGNATION], [ORGANISATION], [RECEIVER'S ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE] Subject: Complaint regarding [DEFECTIVE PRODUCT / POOR SERVICE] - Invoice No. [INVOICE NUMBER] Dear Sir/Madam, I am writing to bring to your notice that [PROBLEM]. I purchased the [PRODUCT / SERVICE] from [SHOP / WEBSITE] on [DATE] (Invoice No. [INVOICE NUMBER], Rs. [AMOUNT]). [DETAILS - WHAT IS WRONG AND SINCE WHEN]. I reported the problem to [SHOP / CUSTOMER CARE] on [DATE] (Complaint No. [COMPLAINT NUMBER]), but it has not been solved so far. This has caused me [INCONVENIENCE / LOSS]. I therefore request you to [REMEDY - REPLACE THE PRODUCT / REFUND THE AMOUNT / REPAIR IT FREE OF COST] within [NUMBER OF DAYS] days. If the matter is not settled by then, I shall be compelled to approach [HIGHER AUTHORITY / THE CONSUMER COMMISSION]. Yours faithfully, [SIGNATURE] [NAME] Encl.: 1. Copy of Invoice No. [INVOICE NUMBER] 2. [PHOTOGRAPHS / WARRANTY CARD]

EXAM TIP: Give proof (invoice number, date, amount) and ask for ONE clear remedy with a time limit - firm but polite, no angry words. Civic problems (roads, water, garbage) go to an authority: use 9.9.

9.6 Inquiry Letter

Use for: Asking for information - prices, terms, course details, admission, or the availability of a product or service ("enquiry letter" is also accepted).

[SENDER'S ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE] [DATE] To The [RECEIVER'S DESIGNATION], [ORGANISATION], [RECEIVER'S ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE] Subject: Inquiry about [PRODUCT / SERVICE / COURSE] Dear Sir/Madam, I saw your advertisement for [PRODUCT / SERVICE / COURSE] in [NEWSPAPER / WEBSITE] dated [DATE]. I am interested in [WHAT YOU WANT] and would like some information before I decide. I would be grateful if you could send me the following details: 1. [FIRST QUESTION - PRICE / FEES] 2. [SECOND QUESTION - TERMS / DURATION / ELIGIBILITY] 3. [THIRD QUESTION - DELIVERY / DATES / DISCOUNTS] Kindly send these details, along with your [BROCHURE / PRICE LIST / APPLICATION FORM], to the above address or by email to [EMAIL ADDRESS]. I look forward to your early reply. Yours faithfully, [SIGNATURE] [NAME]

EXAM TIP: Number your questions so that the reply can answer point by point, and end by asking for a brochure, price list or form. A firm's inquiry uses the business layout (9.4) and "we".

9.7 Request Letter

Use for: Asking an authority or organisation for permission, a document, a facility, a concession or a change (a duplicate certificate, a bus stop, the use of a hall).

[SENDER'S ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE] [DATE] To The [RECEIVER'S DESIGNATION], [ORGANISATION], [RECEIVER'S ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE] Subject: Request for [WHAT YOU NEED] Dear Sir/Madam, I am writing to request you to [REQUEST IN ONE CLAUSE]. I am a [STUDENT / RESIDENT / MEMBER] of [INSTITUTION / AREA / ORGANISATION]. I need this because [REASON]. [DETAILS - DATES, NUMBERS, WHO WILL BENEFIT]. I am ready to [CONDITION - PAY THE PRESCRIBED FEE / PRODUCE THE DOCUMENTS / FOLLOW THE RULES]. I shall be grateful if you could [ACTION REQUIRED] by [DATE]. Thank you for considering my request. Yours faithfully, [SIGNATURE] [NAME] Encl.: [ENCLOSURES]

EXAM TIP: One letter, one request: state it in the Subject ("Request for ...") and again in the first sentence. Give the reason and offer to meet any condition (fee, documents, rules).

9.8 Application Letter (Job Application)

Use for: Applying for an advertised job - the covering letter sent with your resume and certificates.

[SENDER'S ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE] [DATE] To The [RECEIVER'S DESIGNATION], [ORGANISATION], [RECEIVER'S ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE] Subject: Application for the post of [POST] Reference: Your advertisement No. [REFERENCE NUMBER] in [NEWSPAPER / WEBSITE] dated [DATE] Dear Sir/Madam, In response to your advertisement cited above, I wish to apply for the post of [POST] in your [ORGANISATION / DEPARTMENT]. I believe that my qualifications and experience match your requirements. I have completed [QUALIFICATION] from [INSTITUTION] in [YEAR] with [MARKS / GRADE]. I have [NUMBER OF YEARS] years of experience as [PREVIOUS POST] at [PREVIOUS EMPLOYER], where I [MAIN DUTIES / ACHIEVEMENT]. I am skilled in [SKILLS - COMPUTER, ACCOUNTS, LANGUAGES]. I enclose my resume and copies of my certificates for your consideration. I shall be glad to attend an interview on any date convenient to you. If selected, I shall discharge my duties with sincerity and dedication. Thank you for considering my application. Yours faithfully, [SIGNATURE] [NAME] Encl.: 1. Resume (bio-data) 2. Copies of [EDUCATIONAL AND EXPERIENCE CERTIFICATES] 3. [OTHER DOCUMENTS - PHOTOGRAPH, IDENTITY PROOF]

EXAM TIP: Quote the advertisement and list every enclosure. Freshers replace the experience sentence with a project or internship; a serving employee applies "through proper channel" (add a "Through:" line).

9.9 Letter to a Government Authority

Use for: A citizen writing to an official (District Collector, Corporation Commissioner, Tahsildar, Inspector of Police, head of a department) with a civic complaint, petition or request.

[SENDER'S ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE] [DATE] To The [RECEIVER'S DESIGNATION], [OFFICE / DEPARTMENT], [RECEIVER'S ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE] Subject: [COMPLAINT REGARDING / REQUEST FOR] [ISSUE] in [AREA] Reference: [YOUR NOTICE / MY PETITION] No. [REFERENCE NUMBER] dated [DATE] Sir/Madam, I, [NAME], a resident of [AREA / VILLAGE], wish to bring to your kind notice that [PROBLEM OR NEED, IN ONE CLAUSE]. [DETAILS - SINCE WHEN, HOW MANY PEOPLE ARE AFFECTED, DANGER OR LOSS]. I gave a petition to [LOWER AUTHORITY] on [DATE], but no action has been taken so far. I therefore request you to [ACTION REQUIRED] at the earliest. I shall be grateful for your prompt action in this matter. Yours faithfully, [SIGNATURE] [NAME] Encl.: 1. Copy of the petition dated [DATE] 2. [PHOTOGRAPHS / SIGNATURES OF RESIDENTS] Copy to: The [LOWER AUTHORITY] - for information and necessary action

EXAM TIP: Name the exact office (not just "the Government"), quote earlier petitions with dates, and add "Copy to:" when a lower office must also act. "Sir/Madam," and "Dear Sir/Madam," both pair with "Yours faithfully,".

9.10 Letter to the Editor

Use for: Raising a public issue (a civic problem, a social evil, safety, the environment) in a newspaper so that the public and the authorities take notice.

[SENDER'S ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE] [DATE] To The Editor, [NEWSPAPER NAME], [CITY] Subject: [PUBLIC ISSUE - SHORT NOUN PHRASE] Dear Sir/Madam, Through the columns of your esteemed newspaper, I wish to draw the attention of [THE PUBLIC / THE AUTHORITIES CONCERNED] to [ISSUE]. [DETAILS - WHAT IS HAPPENING, WHERE, SINCE WHEN]. [EFFECTS ON THE PUBLIC - HEALTH, SAFETY, MONEY, TIME]. [ONE EXAMPLE OR INCIDENT]. [SUGGESTIONS - WHAT THE AUTHORITIES SHOULD DO AND WHAT CITIZENS CAN DO]. I hope the authorities concerned will take note of this problem and act without delay. I request you to publish this letter in your newspaper. Yours faithfully, [SIGNATURE] [NAME]

EXAM TIP: Write about a PUBLIC issue, not a private grievance. The letter is addressed to the Editor, not to the officials, and the newspaper's address is only its name and city.

9.11 Bank Letter

Use for: Requests and complaints to your bank branch - cheque book, change of address or mobile number, stop payment, account statement, wrong debit, lost passbook or card.

[SENDER'S ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE] [DATE] To The Branch Manager, [BANK NAME], [BRANCH NAME] Branch, [CITY - PIN CODE] Subject: Request for [SERVICE] - Account No. [ACCOUNT NUMBER] Dear Sir/Madam, I hold a [SAVINGS / CURRENT] account in your branch. I request you to [ACTION REQUIRED] because [REASON]. The details are given below: Name of the account holder: [NAME] Account number: [ACCOUNT NUMBER] Registered mobile number: [MOBILE NUMBER] [OTHER DETAILS - CHEQUE NO., DATE, AMOUNT AND PAYEE / NEW ADDRESS / TRANSACTION DATE AND AMOUNT] I have enclosed [SUPPORTING DOCUMENTS] for your reference. Kindly process my request at the earliest and confirm it by SMS or email. Applicable charges, if any, may be debited to my account. Yours faithfully, [SIGNATURE] [NAME] Encl.: [SUPPORTING DOCUMENTS]

EXAM TIP: Put the account number in the Subject and sign exactly as in the bank's records. Never write a PIN, OTP, CVV or password in any letter or email (more bank letters: Section 4).

9.12 Business Transaction Letter (Order Letter with PO Details)

Use for: A buyer firm placing an order with a supplier, usually after receiving a quotation; the numbered Purchase Order (PO) is enclosed.

[BUYER FIRM NAME IN CAPITALS] [FIRM ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE] Phone: [PHONE NUMBER] | Email: [EMAIL ADDRESS] GSTIN: [GSTIN] Ref. No.: [REFERENCE NUMBER] [DATE] To M/s. [SUPPLIER FIRM NAME], [SUPPLIER'S ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE] Kind Attention: [OFFICER'S NAME], Sales Manager Subject: Order for [GOODS] - Purchase Order No. [PO NUMBER] Dear Sir/Madam, With reference to your quotation No. [QUOTATION NUMBER] dated [DATE], we are pleased to place an order for the following goods under our Purchase Order No. [PO NUMBER] dated [DATE]: 1. [ITEM AND SPECIFICATION] - [QUANTITY] [UNIT] @ Rs. [RATE] per [UNIT] = Rs. [AMOUNT] 2. [ITEM AND SPECIFICATION] - [QUANTITY] [UNIT] @ Rs. [RATE] per [UNIT] = Rs. [AMOUNT] Total value: Rs. [AMOUNT] (GST extra, as applicable) Terms of the order: Delivery: at [DELIVERY ADDRESS] on or before [DATE] Delivery terms: [EX-WORKS / FOR / DOOR DELIVERY] Mode of transport: [ROAD / RAIL / COURIER] Payment: [PAYMENT TERMS] Packing: [PACKING INSTRUCTIONS] Please supply the goods strictly as per the specifications in your quotation. Goods found damaged or not as ordered will be returned at your cost. Kindly acknowledge this order and confirm the date of dispatch. Please quote our PO No. [PO NUMBER] on your invoice, delivery challan and all correspondence. Yours faithfully, For [BUYER FIRM NAME] [SIGNATURE] [NAME] [DESIGNATION] Encl.: Purchase Order No. [PO NUMBER] dated [DATE]

EXAM TIP: Every order states the PO number, item and specification, quantity, rate, total, delivery date, delivery terms and payment terms. The PO becomes binding once the seller accepts it (see Section 5).

9.13 Recommendation Letter

Use for: A teacher, head of an institution or employer recommending a candidate to a specific organisation for a job, admission or scholarship.

[INSTITUTION / COMPANY NAME] [INSTITUTION ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE] Phone: [PHONE NUMBER] | Email: [EMAIL ADDRESS] [DATE] To The [RECEIVER'S DESIGNATION], [ORGANISATION], [RECEIVER'S ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE] Subject: Recommendation for [CANDIDATE'S NAME] - [POST / COURSE / SCHOLARSHIP] Dear Sir/Madam, I am pleased to recommend Mr./Ms. [CANDIDATE'S NAME] for [POST / COURSE / SCHOLARSHIP] in your [ORGANISATION]. I have known [HIM / HER] for [NUMBER OF YEARS] years as [HIS / HER] [TEACHER / HEAD OF DEPARTMENT / MANAGER] at [INSTITUTION / COMPANY]. [HE / SHE] [MAIN ACHIEVEMENTS - MARKS, PROJECTS, AWARDS, RESULTS]. [HE / SHE] is [QUALITY 1], [QUALITY 2] and [QUALITY 3]. For example, [ONE INCIDENT THAT PROVES A QUALITY]. I am confident that [HE / SHE] will be an asset to your [ORGANISATION], and I recommend [HIM / HER] without reservation. Please feel free to contact me at [PHONE NUMBER] or [EMAIL ADDRESS] for any further information. Yours faithfully, [SIGNATURE] [NAME] [DESIGNATION]

EXAM TIP: A recommendation goes to a specific receiver for a specific purpose; a reference letter is general ("To Whom It May Concern", 9.14). If you know the receiver's name, write "Dear Dr. [SURNAME]," and close with "Yours sincerely,".

9.14 Reference Letter ("To Whom It May Concern")

Use for: A general letter about a person's service, conduct or character that he or she can show to any employer or institution.

[ORGANISATION NAME IN CAPITALS] [ORGANISATION ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE] Phone: [PHONE NUMBER] | Email: [EMAIL ADDRESS] Ref. No.: [REFERENCE NUMBER] [DATE] TO WHOM IT MAY CONCERN This is to certify that Mr./Ms. [FULL NAME] worked in our [ORGANISATION / DEPARTMENT] as [POST] from [FROM DATE] to [TO DATE]. During this period, [HIS / HER] duties included [MAIN DUTIES]. [HE / SHE] was [QUALITY 1], [QUALITY 2] and [QUALITY 3], and [ONE SPECIFIC ACHIEVEMENT]. [HIS / HER] conduct and character were [GOOD / EXCELLENT]. We have no hesitation in recommending [HIM / HER] for [ANY SUITABLE POST / FURTHER STUDIES], and we wish [HIM / HER] every success in [HIS / HER] future career. Yours faithfully, For [ORGANISATION NAME] [SIGNATURE] [NAME] [DESIGNATION]

EXAM TIP: No "To" block and no salutation - the heading TO WHOM IT MAY CONCERN replaces both, but the close is still "Yours faithfully,". State only facts you can confirm: dates, post, duties, conduct.

9.15 Resignation Letter

Use for: An employee formally telling the employer that he or she is leaving the job, with the notice period and the last working day.

[SENDER'S ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE] [DATE] To The [RECEIVER'S DESIGNATION], [ORGANISATION], [RECEIVER'S ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE] Subject: Resignation from the post of [POST] Dear Sir/Madam, Please accept this letter as formal notice of my resignation from the post of [POST] in the [DEPARTMENT] department. As required by my terms of appointment, I am giving [NOTICE PERIOD] notice; my last working day will be [LAST WORKING DATE]. I have decided to resign because [REASON - SHORT AND POSITIVE]. I am grateful for the guidance and support I have received during my [NUMBER OF YEARS] years with [ORGANISATION], especially [ONE SPECIFIC THING - TRAINING / PROJECT / MENTORING]. Before I leave, I shall complete all pending work and hand over charge of [FILES / DUTIES] to [SUCCESSOR'S NAME / THE PERSON YOU NOMINATE]. I request you to accept my resignation, relieve me on [LAST WORKING DATE] and issue my relieving letter and experience certificate. Yours faithfully, [SIGNATURE] [NAME] [DESIGNATION] Employee ID: [EMPLOYEE ID]

EXAM TIP: State the post, the notice period and the exact last working day; keep the reason short and positive - never criticise the employer. If you address your manager by name ("Dear Mr. Raman,"), close with "Yours sincerely,".

9.16 Leave Letter

Use for: An employee asking the immediate superior for casual, earned or medical leave for a stated period.

[SENDER'S ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE] [DATE] To The [SUPERIOR'S DESIGNATION], [DEPARTMENT / SECTION], [ORGANISATION], [CITY - PIN CODE] Subject: Application for [TYPE OF LEAVE] leave from [FROM DATE] to [TO DATE] Respected Sir/Madam, I request you to grant me [TYPE OF LEAVE - CASUAL / EARNED / MEDICAL] leave for [NUMBER OF DAYS] days, from [FROM DATE] to [TO DATE], because [REASON]. [DETAILS - DOCTOR'S ADVICE / DATE OF THE FAMILY FUNCTION]. I have completed [URGENT WORK], and [COLLEAGUE'S NAME AND DESIGNATION] has agreed to look after my duties during my absence. I shall rejoin duty on [REJOINING DATE]. I can be contacted on [PHONE NUMBER] if any urgent matter arises. I shall be grateful if you could sanction the leave. Yours faithfully, [SIGNATURE] [NAME] [DESIGNATION] Employee ID: [EMPLOYEE ID] Encl.: [MEDICAL CERTIFICATE / INVITATION]

EXAM TIP: Give the type of leave, the exact dates, the number of days, the reason and who will handle your work. Students writing to the Principal use the same layout and may close with the traditional "Yours obediently,".

9.17 Appointment / Joining Letter

Use for: (a) An employer appointing a selected candidate; (b) the new employee's joining report to the employer on the day of joining.

(a) Appointment letter - employer to candidate

[COMPANY NAME IN CAPITALS] [COMPANY ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE] Phone: [PHONE NUMBER] | Email: [EMAIL ADDRESS] Ref. No.: [REFERENCE NUMBER] [DATE] To Mr./Ms. [CANDIDATE'S FULL NAME], [CANDIDATE'S ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE] Subject: Appointment as [POST] Reference: Your application dated [DATE] and the interview held on [DATE] Dear Mr./Ms. [SURNAME], We are pleased to inform you that you have been selected for the post of [POST] in our [DEPARTMENT] department at [PLACE OF POSTING]. The main terms of your appointment are: 1. Salary: Rs. [AMOUNT] per month ([PAY DETAILS - BASIC PAY AND ALLOWANCES]) 2. Probation: [PROBATION PERIOD] from the date of joining 3. Working hours: [WORKING HOURS] 4. Notice period: [NOTICE PERIOD] on either side 5. Other conditions: as per the enclosed terms of service and the company rules Please report to [REPORTING OFFICER'S NAME AND DESIGNATION] at [PLACE OF REPORTING] on [DATE OF JOINING] at [TIME] with your original certificates, identity proof and [NUMBER] passport-size photographs. Please sign and return the duplicate copy of this letter as a token of your acceptance by [LAST DATE FOR ACCEPTANCE]. If you do not report by [DATE OF JOINING], this appointment will stand cancelled. We welcome you to [COMPANY NAME] and wish you a successful career with us. Yours sincerely, For [COMPANY NAME] [SIGNATURE] [NAME] [DESIGNATION] Encl.: 1. Terms and conditions of service 2. Duplicate copy of this letter (for acceptance)

(b) Joining report - employee to employer

[SENDER'S ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE] [DATE] To The [RECEIVER'S DESIGNATION], [DEPARTMENT], [COMPANY NAME], [COMPANY ADDRESS], [CITY - PIN CODE] Subject: Joining report - [POST], [DEPARTMENT] department Reference: Your appointment letter Ref. No. [REFERENCE NUMBER] dated [DATE] Dear Sir/Madam, With reference to your appointment letter cited above, I hereby report for duty as [POST] in the [DEPARTMENT] department today, [DATE OF JOINING] ([FORENOON / AFTERNOON]). I have signed and returned the duplicate copy of the appointment letter in acceptance of the terms.
