We look forward to receiving your order and assure you of prompt and careful execution. Yours sincerely, For KAVERI PAPER MILLS R. Meena Sales Manager Encl.: 1. Price list 2026-27 2. Catalogue

Important vocabulary: quotation - the seller's statement of prices, terms and conditions, sent in reply to an inquiry; firm offer - an offer that the seller promises to keep open until a stated date; validity - the period for which the quoted prices hold good; list price - the price in the price list, before any discount; inclusive of GST - the tax is already included in the price; transit insurance - insurance against loss of or damage to goods on the way; prices ruling - the prices in force on a particular date; execution (of an order) - carrying out an order by supplying the goods.

Why this format is correct:

  • A quotation answers the inquiry point by point - prices, discounts, delivery, payment and minimum order - in the same order as the buyer's numbered questions, and it quotes the buyer's reference number and date.
  • The validity date ("valid until 31 October 2026") makes it a firm offer; after that date the seller is free to change the prices.
  • It is addressed to a named person, so "Dear Mr. Senthil," pairs with "Yours sincerely,".
  • The terms are set out line by line, so the buyer can copy them into the order.
  • Do not confuse: a quotation gives firm prices before the order; an estimate gives an approximate cost; a tender is a formal, often sealed, offer made in response to an invitation, usually from a public body.

10.24 Order Letter with a Purchase Order Number Formal

Question: Sri Murugan Stationers, 45, West Masi Street, Madurai - 625 001, accepts the quotation of Kaveri Paper Mills, 8, Industrial Estate, Sivakasi - 626 123 (see 10.23). As S. Senthil, Proprietor, place an order under Purchase Order No. SMS/PO/2026-27/058, addressed to the firm for the kind attention of Ms. R. Meena, Sales Manager. State the quantities, delivery date and payment terms.

Situation: Writer: S. Senthil, Proprietor, Sri Murugan Stationers (buyer) | Receiver: M/s. Kaveri Paper Mills, for the attention of Ms. R. Meena, Sales Manager (seller) | Relationship: buyer accepting the seller's quotation | Purpose: to place a clear, complete order | Register: Formal

Correct format: Letterhead → Ref. No. (PO number) → Date → To + inside address (M/s. + firm) → Kind Attention line → Subject → Reference → Dear Sir/Madam, → Body (items and amounts, delivery, payment, acknowledgement) → Yours faithfully, → For firm, name, designation

SRI MURUGAN STATIONERS 45, WEST MASI STREET, MADURAI - 625 001 PHONE: 0452 2XXXXXX | EMAIL: murugan@example.com GSTIN: 33XXXXXXXXXX1Z5 Ref. No.: SMS/PO/2026-27/058 12 October 2026 To M/s. Kaveri Paper Mills, 8, Industrial Estate, Sivakasi - 626 123 Kind Attention: Ms. R. Meena, Sales Manager Subject: Purchase Order No. SMS/PO/2026-27/058 for school notebooks Reference: Your quotation Ref. No. KPM/SAL/2026-27/204 dated 8 October 2026 Dear Sir/Madam, Thank you for your quotation. We are pleased to place the following order at the prices and on the terms stated in it: 1. Long-size notebook, 200 pages (Code LN-200): 100 dozen @ Rs. 540 = Rs. 54,000 2. King-size notebook, 160 pages (Code KS-160): 80 dozen @ Rs. 420 = Rs. 33,600 3. Four-line notebook, 80 pages (Code FL-80): 50 dozen @ Rs. 216 = Rs. 10,800 Total at list prices: Rs. 98,400 Less trade discount at 10%: Rs. 9,840 Net amount payable (inclusive of GST): Rs. 88,560 Please deliver the goods at our shop, at the address given above, on or before 20 October 2026, packed in waterproof cartons as mentioned in your quotation. Timely delivery is essential, as our customers are waiting for these notebooks. We shall pay the invoice amount by NEFT within 30 days of the date of your invoice. Please quote our Purchase Order number on your invoice, delivery challan and all letters about this order. Please acknowledge receipt of this order and inform us of the date of dispatch. Yours faithfully, For SRI MURUGAN STATIONERS S. Senthil Proprietor

Important vocabulary: Purchase Order (PO) - a formal numbered document issued by the buyer to order goods; it becomes binding once the seller accepts it; M/s. - Messrs, written before the name of a firm; Kind Attention - a line that directs a letter addressed to a firm to a particular person; @ - at the rate of; net amount - the amount payable after deducting discounts; delivery challan - a document that travels with the goods and lists what is sent; dispatch - sending off the goods.

Why this format is correct:

  • The PO number appears in the Ref. No. and in the Subject, and the Reference line quotes the quotation, so both firms can trace the deal from inquiry to payment.
  • Each item is listed with code, description, quantity, rate and amount, followed by the discount and the net total. An order must be exact about what, how many, at what price, when and where to deliver, and how payment will be made.
  • The letter is addressed to the firm (M/s. Kaveri Paper Mills); the Kind Attention line only directs it to Ms. Meena. As the letter is still to the firm, the salutation is "Dear Sir/Madam," with "Yours faithfully,".
  • It asks the seller to acknowledge the order and to quote the PO number on the invoice and challan; once accepted, the order binds both sides.

10.25 Complaint and Claim: Damaged and Short Goods Formal

Question: The notebooks ordered by Sri Murugan Stationers, 45, West Masi Street, Madurai - 625 001 (see 10.24) arrived on 19 October 2026 with Invoice No. KPM/INV/2026-27/0412 dated 16 October 2026. Three cartons (15 dozen) of long-size notebooks were soaked with rain water, and 10 dozen king-size notebooks were short. As S. Senthil, Proprietor, write to the Sales Manager, Kaveri Paper Mills, 8, Industrial Estate, Sivakasi - 626 123, complaining and claiming replacement of the damaged goods and supply of the shortage.

Situation: Writer: S. Senthil, Proprietor, Sri Murugan Stationers (buyer) | Receiver: The Sales Manager, Kaveri Paper Mills (seller) | Relationship: customer to supplier after delivery | Purpose: to report the damage and shortage (complaint) and to demand a specific remedy (claim) | Register: Formal

Correct format: Letterhead → Ref. No. → Date → To + inside address → Subject → Reference → Dear Sir/Madam, → Body (facts of delivery, the problem, the claim with a date, evidence) → Yours faithfully, → For firm, name, designation → Encl.

SRI MURUGAN STATIONERS 45, WEST MASI STREET, MADURAI - 625 001 PHONE: 0452 2XXXXXX | EMAIL: murugan@example.com GSTIN: 33XXXXXXXXXX1Z5 Ref. No.: SMS/PUR/2026-27/131 20 October 2026 To The Sales Manager, Kaveri Paper Mills, 8, Industrial Estate, Sivakasi - 626 123 Subject: Damaged and short supply against our Purchase Order No. SMS/PO/2026-27/058 Reference: Your Invoice No. KPM/INV/2026-27/0412 dated 16 October 2026 Dear Sir/Madam, We received the consignment against the above order on 19 October 2026 through Vaigai Road Carriers under Lorry Receipt No. VRC/2291. We regret to inform you that it was not in order. On opening the cartons in the presence of the lorry driver, we found the following: 1. Three cartons of long-size notebooks (Code LN-200), that is 15 dozen, were soaked with rain water. The pages are stuck together, and the notebooks cannot be sold. 2. Only 14 cartons (70 dozen) of king-size notebooks (Code KS-160) were received, although your invoice charges us for 80 dozen. Thus 10 dozen are short. We recorded both facts on the Lorry Receipt and in our Goods Received Note No. SMS/GRN/231. As your quotation provided for delivery at our shop, with transit insurance arranged by you, we request you to replace the 15 dozen damaged notebooks and to supply the 10 dozen short notebooks, free of charge, on or before 28 October 2026. The damaged cartons are kept aside for your inspection or collection. We trust that you will attend to this matter promptly, as our customers need these notebooks for the current school term. Yours faithfully, For SRI MURUGAN STATIONERS S. Senthil Proprietor Encl.: 1. Copy of Lorry Receipt No. VRC/2291 with our remarks 2. Photographs of the damaged cartons (4) 3. Copy of Goods Received Note No. SMS/GRN/231

Important vocabulary: consignment - a batch of goods sent together; Lorry Receipt (LR) - the receipt issued by a road transporter for goods accepted for carriage; Goods Received Note (GRN) - the buyer's own record of the goods received; short supply / shortage - fewer goods received than ordered or invoiced; complaint - a letter reporting a problem (defect, delay, shortage, wrong goods); claim - a demand for a specific remedy or compensation (replacement, refund, price reduction); transit - the journey of goods from the seller to the buyer.

Why this format is correct:

  • The first paragraph gives traceable facts: PO number, invoice number and date (in the Reference line), transporter, LR number and date of receipt.
  • The problem is stated exactly - item codes, cartons and dozens - not vaguely ("some goods were bad").
  • It is both a complaint (it reports the damage and the shortage) and a claim (it demands a specific remedy - replacement and supply of the shortage, free of charge - by a fixed date).
  • The evidence is listed under "Encl." - LR with remarks, photographs and GRN - which makes the claim difficult to refuse.
  • The tone is firm but polite - no threats, no insulting words - and the damaged goods are kept aside for inspection. The seller's reply is an adjustment letter (see 10.26).

10.26 Adjustment Letter: Replacement and Credit Note Formal

Question: As R. Meena, Sales Manager, Kaveri Paper Mills, 8, Industrial Estate, Sivakasi - 626 123, reply to the claim of Mr. S. Senthil, Proprietor, Sri Murugan Stationers, 45, West Masi Street, Madurai - 625 001 (see 10.25). Accept the claim, apologise, arrange replacement of the damaged notebooks and supply of the shortage, and issue a credit note for Rs. 1,500 as a special allowance for the inconvenience.

Situation: Writer: R. Meena, Sales Manager, Kaveri Paper Mills (seller) | Receiver: Mr. S. Senthil, Proprietor, Sri Murugan Stationers (buyer) | Relationship: supplier replying to a customer's claim | Purpose: to settle the claim in full and keep the customer's goodwill | Register: Formal

Correct format: Letterhead → Ref. No. → Date → To + inside address (named person) → Subject → Reference → Dear Mr. Senthil, → Body (thanks and apology, cause, remedy, credit note, prevention) → Yours sincerely, → For firm, name, designation → Encl.

KAVERI PAPER MILLS 8, INDUSTRIAL ESTATE, SIVAKASI - 626 123 PHONE: 04562 2XXXXX | EMAIL: sales.kaveri@example.com GSTIN: 33XXXXXXXXXX1Z5 Ref. No.: KPM/SAL/2026-27/236 23 October 2026 To Mr. S. Senthil, Proprietor, Sri Murugan Stationers, 45, West Masi Street, Madurai - 625 001 Subject: Replacement of damaged notebooks and supply of shortage Reference: Your letter Ref. No. SMS/PUR/2026-27/131 dated 20 October 2026 Dear Mr. Senthil, Thank you for your letter informing us of the damage and shortage in our consignment against your Purchase Order No. SMS/PO/2026-27/058. We are sorry for the inconvenience this has caused you. On inquiry, we found that rain water entered the lorry through a torn cover, and that two cartons of king-size notebooks were left behind at our godown because of a loading error. We accept your claim in full. Today we have dispatched 15 dozen long-size notebooks (Code LN-200) and 10 dozen king-size notebooks (Code KS-160), free of charge, through Vaigai Road Carriers under Lorry Receipt No. VRC/2417. They should reach you by 26 October 2026. The carriers will collect the damaged cartons at the same time, and we request you to hand them over. As a token of our regret, we have also issued Credit Note No. KPM/CN/2026-27/019 for Rs. 1,500 as a special allowance. This reduces the amount due on our Invoice No. KPM/INV/2026-27/0412 from Rs. 88,560 to Rs. 87,060. We have instructed our dispatch staff to check every consignment against the invoice and to use waterproof covers. We value your business and assure you that your future orders will receive our personal attention. Yours sincerely, For KAVERI PAPER MILLS R. Meena Sales Manager Encl.: Credit Note No. KPM/CN/2026-27/019

Important vocabulary: adjustment letter - the seller's reply that settles a complaint or claim (full grant, partial grant, or polite refusal with reasons); credit note - a note issued by the seller to the buyer that reduces the amount the buyer owes; allowance - a reduction in the amount payable, given for damage, delay or inconvenience; free of charge - without any payment; godown - warehouse (Indian English); on inquiry - after investigation; goodwill - the customer's trust in the firm and willingness to deal with it again.

Why this format is correct:

  • It is an adjustment letter that grants the claim in full, in the standard order: thank and apologise → explain the cause briefly → state the remedy with details (quantities, LR number, arrival date) → prevent a repeat → goodwill close.
  • The credit note is issued by the SELLER and reduces what the buyer owes; the letter shows the new balance (Rs. 88,560 less Rs. 1,500 = Rs. 87,060). Do not confuse: a debit note is issued by the buyer when returning goods or claiming a deduction.
  • It explains the cause honestly, without blaming the customer or the transporter, and accepts responsibility in plain words: "We accept your claim in full."
  • It is addressed to a named person, so "Dear Mr. Senthil," pairs with "Yours sincerely,".

10.27 Payment Reminder: First Reminder Formal

Question: Invoice No. KPM/INV/2026-27/0412 dated 16 October 2026, sent to Sri Murugan Stationers, 45, West Masi Street, Madurai - 625 001, was due for payment on 15 November 2026. After adjusting Credit Note No. KPM/CN/2026-27/019 for Rs. 1,500 (see 10.26), Rs. 87,060 is still unpaid. As K. Suresh, Accounts Manager, Kaveri Paper Mills, 8, Industrial Estate, Sivakasi - 626 123, write a polite first reminder to the firm for the kind attention of Mr. S. Senthil, Proprietor.

Situation: Writer: K. Suresh, Accounts Manager, Kaveri Paper Mills (seller) | Receiver: M/s. Sri Murugan Stationers, for the attention of Mr. S. Senthil, Proprietor (buyer) | Relationship: supplier to a valued customer whose payment is overdue | Purpose: to obtain payment while keeping the customer's goodwill (collection series, stage 1) | Register: Formal

Correct format: Letterhead → Ref. No. → Date → To + inside address (M/s. + firm) → Kind Attention line → Subject → Dear Sir/Madam, → Body (goodwill opening, exact dues, polite request, "ignore if paid", offer to clarify) → Yours faithfully, → For firm, name, designation → Encl.

KAVERI PAPER MILLS 8, INDUSTRIAL ESTATE, SIVAKASI - 626 123 PHONE: 04562 2XXXXX | EMAIL: accounts.kaveri@example.com GSTIN: 33XXXXXXXXXX1Z5 Ref. No.: KPM/ACC/2026-27/377 1 December 2026 To M/s. Sri Murugan Stationers, 45, West Masi Street, Madurai - 625 001 Kind Attention: Mr. S. Senthil, Proprietor Subject: Reminder - payment due on Invoice No. KPM/INV/2026-27/0412 Dear Sir/Madam, We thank you for your valued business and hope that our notebooks are selling well in your shop. This is a friendly reminder that the following amount, which was due for payment on 15 November 2026, remains unpaid according to our records: Invoice No. KPM/INV/2026-27/0412 dated 16 October 2026: Rs. 88,560 Less Credit Note No. KPM/CN/2026-27/019: Rs. 1,500 Balance due: Rs. 87,060 We are sure that this is only an oversight. We shall be grateful if you arrange the payment by NEFT or cheque at your earliest convenience. Our bank details are printed on the invoice, and a statement of your account is enclosed for easy reference. If you have already sent the payment, please accept our thanks and ignore this letter. If you have any query about the invoice, please let us know, and we shall clarify it at once. We look forward to continuing our pleasant business relationship with you. Yours faithfully, For KAVERI PAPER MILLS K. Suresh Accounts Manager Encl.: Statement of account as on 30 November 2026

Important vocabulary: reminder - a letter that reminds the buyer of a payment that is due or overdue; overdue - not paid by the due date; balance due / outstanding - the amount still to be paid; statement of account - a summary of invoices, credit notes and payments, showing the balance; oversight - a mistake made by not noticing something; at your earliest convenience - as soon as you can; collection series - first reminder (polite) → second reminder (firmer) → final notice or demand (warns of legal action).

Why this format is correct:

  • It is stage 1 of the collection series: polite, it assumes an oversight, and it contains no threat, no mention of legal action and no penalty.
  • It gives exact facts - invoice number, invoice date, due date, credit note and balance - and encloses a statement of account, so the buyer can check and pay at once.
  • It offers two polite ways out: "if already paid, please ignore this letter" and "if you have any query, please let us know". This keeps the customer's goodwill.
  • It is addressed to the firm (M/s.) with a Kind Attention line, so "Dear Sir/Madam," pairs with "Yours faithfully,"; the Accounts Manager signs "For KAVERI PAPER MILLS".

10.28 Bank: Stop-Payment of a Lost Cheque Formal

Question: Mrs. P. Lakshmi, 18, Bharathi Park Road, Saibaba Colony, Coimbatore - 641 011, holds Savings Bank Account No. XXXXXXXX4521 at Southern Commercial Bank, R.S. Puram Branch, Coimbatore - 641 002. She has lost Cheque No. 004517 dated 9 October 2026 for Rs. 25,000, drawn in favour of Selvam Furniture Mart, Coimbatore, before handing it over. Write her letter to the Branch Manager asking the bank to stop payment of the cheque.

Situation: Writer: Mrs. P. Lakshmi (account holder, drawer of the cheque) | Receiver: The Branch Manager, Southern Commercial Bank, R.S. Puram Branch | Relationship: customer to her bank | Purpose: to stop payment of a lost cheque before anyone can encash it | Register: Formal

Correct format: Sender's address → Date → To + Branch Manager's address → Subject → Dear Sir/Madam, → Body (account, cheque details, how it was lost, instruction, charges, confirmation) → Yours faithfully, → Name and account number

18, Bharathi Park Road, Saibaba Colony, Coimbatore - 641 011 11 October 2026 To The Branch Manager, Southern Commercial Bank, R.S. Puram Branch, Coimbatore - 641 002 Subject: Urgent - stop payment of lost Cheque No. 004517 Dear Sir/Madam, I hold Savings Bank Account No. XXXXXXXX4521 at your branch. I request you to stop payment of the following cheque, which I have lost: Cheque No.: 004517 Date: 9 October 2026 Amount: Rs. 25,000 (Rupees twenty-five thousand only) Payee: Selvam Furniture Mart, Coimbatore I had signed this cheque to pay for furniture that I had ordered from the payee. However, it was lost along with my handbag in a city bus on 10 October 2026, before I could hand it over to the payee. My account shows that the cheque has not been paid so far. Please record this stop-payment instruction immediately and do not pay the cheque if it is presented at any branch or through clearing. I agree to bear the stop-payment charges as per the bank's rules. I have informed the payee, and I shall make the payment by a fresh cheque. Please confirm in writing, or by email, that my instruction has been recorded against the cheque. Yours faithfully, P. Lakshmi SB Account No. XXXXXXXX4521

Important vocabulary: stop-payment - the account holder's instruction to the bank not to pay a particular cheque; drawer - the person who writes and signs the cheque (here, Mrs. Lakshmi); drawee - the bank on which the cheque is drawn; payee - the person or firm to whom the cheque is payable; clearing - the process by which a cheque deposited in another bank is sent to the drawee bank for payment; honour / dishonour - (of a bank) pay / refuse to pay a cheque; specimen signature - the signature kept in the bank's records to verify the customer's signature.

Why this format is correct:

  • A letter from an individual has no letterhead; it uses the formal layout: sender's address → date → "To" → The Branch Manager of the branch where the account is kept → Subject → "Dear Sir/Madam," → "Yours faithfully,".
  • It gives the four details the bank needs to identify the cheque - number, date, amount and payee - on separate lines, together with the account number (masked as XXXXXXXX4521).
  • "Urgent" in the Subject and "immediately" in the body: the instruction works only if it reaches the bank before the cheque is paid. It also asks for written confirmation.
  • The charges are accepted "as per the bank's rules" without quoting any amount. Never write a PIN, OTP, CVV or password in any bank letter, and sign exactly as in the bank's records.
  • Do not confuse: stop-payment is the drawer's instruction to the bank; dishonour is the bank's refusal to pay a cheque, returned with a return memo.

10.29 Bank: ATM Debit without Cash Formal

Question: Mr. V. Karthik, 27, Thillai Nagar Main Road, Tiruchirappalli - 620 018, holds Savings Bank Account No. XXXXXXXX4521 at Southern Commercial Bank, Thillai Nagar Branch, Tiruchirappalli - 620 018. On 3 November 2026 at 7.45 p.m., he tried to withdraw Rs. 10,000 with his debit card from another bank's ATM at the Chatram Bus Stand, Tiruchirappalli. No cash came out, but his account was debited. Write his complaint to the Branch Manager.

Situation: Writer: Mr. V. Karthik (account holder) | Receiver: The Branch Manager, Southern Commercial Bank, Thillai Nagar Branch (his own, card-issuing bank) | Relationship: customer to his bank | Purpose: to get the wrong debit reversed | Register: Formal

Correct format: Sender's address → Date → To + Branch Manager's address → Subject → Dear Sir/Madam, → Body (account, transaction details, what happened, complaint number, request) → Yours faithfully, → Name and account number → Encl.

27, Thillai Nagar Main Road, Tiruchirappalli - 620 018 4 November 2026 To The Branch Manager, Southern Commercial Bank, Thillai Nagar Branch, Tiruchirappalli - 620 018 Subject: Account debited but cash not dispensed at ATM - request for reversal Dear Sir/Madam, I hold Savings Bank Account No. XXXXXXXX4521 at your branch. I wish to report a failed ATM transaction in which my account was debited but no cash was dispensed. The details of the transaction are as follows: Date and time: 3 November 2026, 7.45 p.m. ATM: another bank's ATM at the Chatram Bus Stand, Tiruchirappalli (ATM ID: TCH0072) Card used: my debit card ending in 7788 Amount: Rs. 10,000 Transaction number (as printed on the ATM slip): 6307 The machine printed the slip but did not dispense any cash. A few minutes later, I received an SMS alert that Rs. 10,000 had been debited from my account. I reported the matter the same evening on the bank's customer care number, and my complaint number is SCB/CMP/2026/55810. I request you to verify the transaction with the ATM records and to reverse the wrong debit by crediting Rs. 10,000 back to my account at the earliest. Please also inform me in writing of the action taken. Yours faithfully, V. Karthik SB Account No. XXXXXXXX4521 Encl.: 1. Copy of the ATM transaction slip 2. Copy of the account statement showing the debit

Important vocabulary: dispense - give out (cash from a machine); failed transaction - a transaction in which the account is debited but the cash or service is not received; debit / credit - an entry that reduces / increases the balance in an account; reversal - cancelling a wrong debit by crediting the amount back; ATM ID - the identification number of the machine, printed on the slip; card-issuing bank - the bank that issued the customer's debit card (the customer's own bank); SMS alert - a text message from the bank about a transaction.

Why this format is correct:

  • The complaint goes to the customer's own (card-issuing) bank, even though another bank's ATM was used.
  • All identifying details are set out line by line - date, time, ATM location and ID, amount, transaction number, and only the last four digits of the card - so that the bank can trace the transaction quickly.
  • It mentions the complaint already registered with customer care and lists the evidence (ATM slip, statement) under "Encl.".
  • The request is specific: verify, reverse the debit, and inform in writing. Do not quote RBI time limits or compensation amounts in an exam letter unless the question gives them; such rules are revised from time to time.
  • Safety: never write the PIN, OTP, CVV or full card number; the account number is masked (XXXXXXXX4521).

10.30 Bank: Education Loan Application Formal

Question: Ms. M. Kavya, 7, Bharathi Street, Fairlands, Salem - 636 016, has been admitted to the four-year B.E. (Computer Science and Engineering) course at Thendral College of Engineering, Salem. The total cost of the course is Rs. 6,40,000, and her family can pay Rs. 90,000. Her father, Mr. R. Murugesan, holds Savings Account No. XXXXXXXX4521 at Southern Commercial Bank, Fairlands Branch, Salem - 636 016, and will be the co-borrower. Write her application to the Branch Manager for an education loan of Rs. 5,50,000.

Situation: Writer: Ms. M. Kavya (student, applicant) | Receiver: The Branch Manager, Southern Commercial Bank, Fairlands Branch | Relationship: prospective borrower to the bank where her family banks | Purpose: to apply for a loan with all the facts the bank needs to decide | Register: Formal

Correct format: Sender's address → Date → To + Branch Manager's address → Subject → Dear Sir/Madam, → Body (admission, cost, loan amount, co-borrower, repayment, request) → Yours faithfully, → Name → Encl. (numbered list of documents)

7, Bharathi Street, Fairlands, Salem - 636 016 20 July 2026 To The Branch Manager, Southern Commercial Bank, Fairlands Branch, Salem - 636 016 Subject: Application for an education loan of Rs. 5,50,000 for a B.E. course Dear Sir/Madam, I have been admitted on merit to the four-year B.E. (Computer Science and Engineering) course at Thendral College of Engineering, Salem, for the years 2026-2030. I passed the Higher Secondary examination in 2026 with 92 per cent marks. I request you to sanction an education loan to meet the cost of my studies. The cost of the course, as per the fee structure issued by the college, is: Tuition and other college fees (4 years): Rs. 4,80,000 Hostel and mess charges (4 years): Rs. 1,60,000 Total: Rs. 6,40,000 My family will meet Rs. 90,000 from its savings. I therefore request a loan of Rs. 5,50,000, to be released year by year directly to the college as the fees fall due. My father, Mr. R. Murugesan, who holds Savings Account No. XXXXXXXX4521 at your branch, will join as co-borrower. The loan will be repaid in monthly instalments after the course, as per the terms of the bank's scheme. I enclose the documents listed below. As the first-year fees are due on 20 August 2026, I shall be grateful if you process my application early. Please let me know if any other document is required. Yours faithfully, M. Kavya Encl.: 1. Admission letter from the college 2. Fee structure for 2026-2030 issued by the college 3. Higher Secondary mark sheet (copy) 4. Identity and address proof of the applicant and the co-borrower (copies) 5. Income certificate of the co-borrower 6. Passport-size photographs of the applicant and the co-borrower

Important vocabulary: education loan - a bank loan to meet the cost of higher studies; sanction - official approval of a loan; disbursement - release of the loan amount, often in stages and directly to the institution; co-borrower - a person (usually a parent) who shares the responsibility for repaying the loan; margin money - the part of the cost that the borrower pays from own funds (here, Rs. 90,000); moratorium - a period during which repayment is not required, as per the loan scheme; instalment - one of the regular part-payments of a loan (British spelling); collateral security - property or a deposit pledged as security for a loan.

Why this format is correct:

  • A formal application from an individual: sender's address, date, "To The Branch Manager" of the branch where the family banks, Subject, "Dear Sir/Madam," and "Yours faithfully,".
  • The body answers the banker's questions in order: course and college → academic record → cost break-up → own contribution and loan amount → co-borrower → repayment → documents.
  • The figures are exact and add up (Rs. 4,80,000 + Rs. 1,60,000 = Rs. 6,40,000; Rs. 6,40,000 less Rs. 90,000 = Rs. 5,50,000) and are written in the Indian lakh system with "Rs.".
  • Interest rates, subsidies and collateral limits are not quoted - they depend on the bank's current scheme and change from time to time.
  • The documents are listed as numbered enclosures, and the co-borrower's account number is masked (XXXXXXXX4521).

10.31 Resignation Letter Formal

Question: Mr. S. Vignesh, 14, Kamarajar Salai, Sivakasi - 626 123, is Accounts Executive (Employee No. KPM/E/0147) at Kaveri Paper Mills, 8, Industrial Estate, Sivakasi - 626 123. He has accepted a job in Chennai. His appointment letter requires one month's notice. Write his resignation letter to his manager, Mr. K. Suresh, Accounts Manager, stating his notice period, last working day and handover plan.

Situation: Writer: Mr. S. Vignesh, Accounts Executive (employee) | Receiver: Mr. K. Suresh, Accounts Manager (his reporting manager) | Relationship: employee to a named superior | Purpose: to resign formally, serve the notice period and leave on good terms | Register: Formal

Correct format: Sender's address → Date → To + manager's name and address → Subject → Dear Mr. Suresh, → Body (resignation and notice, reason, handover, requests, thanks) → Yours sincerely, → Name, designation, employee number → Copy to

14, Kamarajar Salai, Sivakasi - 626 123 2 November 2026 To Mr. K. Suresh, Accounts Manager, Kaveri Paper Mills, 8, Industrial Estate, Sivakasi - 626 123 Subject: Resignation from the post of Accounts Executive Dear Mr. Suresh, Please accept this letter as my resignation from the post of Accounts Executive. As required by my appointment letter, I am giving one month's notice from today, 2 November 2026. My last working day will therefore be 2 December 2026. I have accepted a position with a firm in Chennai, which will allow me to live with my elderly parents. This was not an easy decision, as I have learnt a great deal here under your guidance for more than four years. During the notice period, I shall complete the October accounts and the bank reconciliation for November. I shall hand over all pending work, files and records to the colleague you nominate, together with a written handover note listing pending payments and collections. I shall return my laptop, identity card and office keys on my last working day. I request you to accept my resignation and to arrange my relieving letter, experience certificate and full and final settlement. Thank you for your support and encouragement. I shall be glad to help in any way during the transition, and I wish Kaveri Paper Mills continued success. Yours sincerely, S. Vignesh Accounts Executive Employee No. KPM/E/0147 Copy to: The Manager - Human Resources, Kaveri Paper Mills, Sivakasi

Important vocabulary: resignation - formally giving up one's job; notice period - the time an employee must continue to work after giving notice, as fixed in the appointment letter; last working day - the final day of duty; handover - passing work, records and company property to another person; relieving letter - the employer's letter confirming that the employee has been released from duty; full and final settlement - the final payment of all dues (pending salary and other amounts) when an employee leaves; Copy to - a line showing who else receives a copy of the letter.

Why this format is correct:

  • The first paragraph does the main job at once: the resignation, the notice period and the exact last working day.
  • The reason is brief and positive, with no complaints about the employer or colleagues - a resignation letter stays in the employee's service record.
  • A clear handover plan (pending work, a written handover note, return of company property) shows responsibility; the letter also asks for the documents needed for the next job.
  • It is written to a named manager, so "Dear Mr. Suresh," pairs with "Yours sincerely,"; the name is followed by designation and employee number, and "Copy to" keeps the HR department informed.

10.32 Recommendation Letter for a Former Student Formal

Question: Dr. N. Anitha is Associate Professor and Head, Department of Commerce, Ponni Arts and Science College, Tiruchirappalli - 620 017. Her former student Mr. P. Arun (B.Com., 2022-2025) has applied for the post of Accounts Assistant at Nila Textiles Private Limited, 22, Avinashi Road, Tiruppur - 641 602. Write her letter of recommendation to the Manager - Human Resources of the company.

Situation: Writer: Dr. N. Anitha, Head of the Department of Commerce | Receiver: The Manager - Human Resources, Nila Textiles Private Limited (name not known) | Relationship: teacher recommending a former student to a possible employer | Purpose: to support the candidate for a particular post with specific evidence | Register: Formal

Correct format: Letterhead (college and department) → Ref. No. → Date → To + inside address → Subject → Dear Sir/Madam, → Body (how the writer knows him, academic record, skills, character, recommendation) → Yours faithfully, → Name and designation

PONNI ARTS AND SCIENCE COLLEGE DEPARTMENT OF COMMERCE TIRUCHIRAPPALLI - 620 017 PHONE: 0431 2XXXXXX | EMAIL: commerce.ponni@example.com Ref. No.: PASC/COM/2026-27/042 12 October 2026 To The Manager - Human Resources, Nila Textiles Private Limited, 22, Avinashi Road, Tiruppur - 641 602 Subject: Recommendation of Mr. P. Arun for the post of Accounts Assistant Dear Sir/Madam, I am pleased to recommend Mr. P. Arun for the post of Accounts Assistant in your company. I have known him for three years as his teacher and Head of Department, when he studied B.Com. in our college from 2022 to 2025. Arun was among the best students in his batch and graduated with a CGPA of 8.6 out of 10. I taught him Financial Accounting and Taxation, and he showed a clear understanding of both subjects. For his final-year project, he studied the working capital management of a textile firm in Tiruchirappalli, and his report was rated the best in the department. He is also skilled in accounting software and spreadsheets. Arun is honest, punctual and hard-working. As class representative in his final year, he dealt courteously with students and staff and handled every responsibility with care. I am confident that he will be a valuable member of your accounts team, and I recommend him without reservation. Please feel free to contact me at commerce.ponni@example.com if you need any further information about him. Yours faithfully, Dr. N. Anitha Associate Professor and Head Department of Commerce

Important vocabulary: recommendation letter - a letter by someone who knows the candidate well, supporting him or her for a particular post or course; candidate - a person applying for a job or course; batch - the group of students who study in the same year; CGPA - Cumulative Grade Point Average; courteously - politely and respectfully; without reservation - fully, without any doubt; testimonial - an older word for a written statement about a person's character and ability.

Why this format is correct:

  • The writer states her position and how long, and in what capacity, she has known the candidate (teacher and Head of Department, three years); this gives the letter weight.
  • Specific evidence - CGPA, subjects, project, skills, class representative - is more convincing than general praise such as "He is a very good student".
  • It is written for a particular post and sent to a particular employer. The reader is known only by designation, so "Dear Sir/Madam," pairs with "Yours faithfully,".
  • The college letterhead and Ref. No. make it official; it ends with a clear recommendation and an offer to give more information.

10.33 Reference Letter: To Whom It May Concern Formal

Question: Mr. S. Vignesh worked at Kaveri Paper Mills, 8, Industrial Estate, Sivakasi - 626 123, from 1 June 2022 to 2 December 2026 - first as Accounts Assistant and, from 1 April 2024, as Accounts Executive. He resigned to take up a job in Chennai (see 10.31). As Ms. T. Priya, Manager - Human Resources, issue a reference letter headed "To Whom It May Concern" confirming his period of employment, role and conduct.

Situation: Writer: Ms. T. Priya, Manager - Human Resources, Kaveri Paper Mills (employer) | Receiver: any future employer or authority (not known) | Relationship: former employer to unknown readers | Purpose: to confirm the facts of employment and the employee's conduct | Register: Formal

Correct format: Letterhead → Ref. No. → Date → TO WHOM IT MAY CONCERN (heading, in place of inside address and salutation) → Body (period and posts, duties, conduct, reason for leaving, "issued at his request") → Yours faithfully, → For firm, name, designation

KAVERI PAPER MILLS 8, INDUSTRIAL ESTATE, SIVAKASI - 626 123 PHONE: 04562 2XXXXX | EMAIL: hr.kaveri@example.com GSTIN: 33XXXXXXXXXX1Z5 Ref. No.: KPM/HR/2026-27/089 4 December 2026 TO WHOM IT MAY CONCERN This is to certify that Mr. S. Vignesh was employed with Kaveri Paper Mills, Sivakasi, from 1 June 2022 to 2 December 2026. He joined us as Accounts Assistant and was promoted to Accounts Executive on 1 April 2024, the post he held until he left us. As Accounts Executive, he maintained our books of accounts in the accounting software, handled payments to suppliers and collections from customers, prepared monthly bank reconciliation statements and assisted in preparing data for GST returns. He also trained two junior members of staff. During his service, he was honest, punctual and hard-working, and he was well liked by his colleagues and our customers. His work and conduct were found to be good throughout. He resigned of his own accord to take up a position in Chennai and was relieved from duty on 2 December 2026, after completing the handover of his work. No dues are outstanding from him. This letter is issued at his request. We wish him every success in his career. Yours faithfully, For KAVERI PAPER MILLS T. Priya Manager - Human Resources

Important vocabulary: To Whom It May Concern - a heading used when the letter is meant for any reader; it replaces the inside address and the salutation; reference letter - an employer's letter confirming facts about a person's employment and conduct; employed with - working for (an organisation); of his own accord - by his own choice; relieved from duty - formally released from the job; dues - amounts owed; experience certificate - a certificate stating the period and nature of a person's employment.

Why this format is correct:

  • "TO WHOM IT MAY CONCERN" is written in capitals in place of the inside address and the salutation, because the reader is not known; the letter can be shown to any future employer.
  • It confirms facts first - full name, period (from and to dates), posts and duties - then conduct, the reason for leaving and the words "issued at his request".
  • The letterhead, Ref. No. and date make it verifiable. It is signed by an authorised officer "For KAVERI PAPER MILLS" with designation and closes with "Yours faithfully,".
  • The language is factual and measured, not emotional; salary and personal matters are not mentioned.
  • Do not confuse: a recommendation letter (10.32) supports a person for a particular post and goes to a particular reader; a reference letter mainly confirms facts and can be shown to anyone.

10.34 Appointment Letter and Joining Report Formal

Question: Nila Textiles Private Limited, 22, Avinashi Road, Tiruppur - 641 602, has selected Mr. P. Arun, 5, Anna Street, Srirangam, Tiruchirappalli - 620 006, as Accounts Assistant after an interview on 5 November 2026. (a) As G. Ramesh, Manager - Human Resources, write the appointment letter asking him to join on 1 December 2026. (b) Write Mr. Arun's joining report to the Manager - Human Resources on the day he reports for duty, giving his local address, 12, Kumaran Road, Tiruppur - 641 601.

Situation: Writer: (a) G. Ramesh, Manager - Human Resources, Nila Textiles Private Limited; (b) Mr. P. Arun | Receiver: (a) Mr. P. Arun (selected candidate); (b) The Manager - Human Resources | Relationship: employer and newly selected employee | Purpose: (a) to appoint him and state the terms of service; (b) to report for duty and place the joining on record | Register: Formal

Correct format: (a) Letterhead → Ref. No. → Date → To + candidate's name and address → Subject → Dear Mr. Arun, → Body (appointment, numbered terms, documents, acceptance, welcome) → Yours sincerely, → For firm, name, designation → Encl. (b) Sender's address → Date → To + receiver's address → Subject → Reference → Dear Sir, → Body (report for duty, documents, contact, request) → Yours faithfully, → Name, new designation → Encl.

(a) Appointment letter - employer to candidate

NILA TEXTILES PRIVATE LIMITED 22, AVINASHI ROAD, TIRUPPUR - 641 602 PHONE: 0421 2XXXXXX | EMAIL: hr.nila@example.com GSTIN: 33XXXXXXXXXX1Z5 Ref. No.: NTPL/HR/APT/2026-27/031 16 November 2026 To Mr. P. Arun, 5, Anna Street, Srirangam, Tiruchirappalli - 620 006 Subject: Appointment as Accounts Assistant Dear Mr. Arun, With reference to your application and the interview held on 5 November 2026, we are pleased to appoint you as Accounts Assistant in our Accounts Department at Tiruppur on the following terms: 1. Date of joining: 1 December 2026, at 9.30 a.m. You will report to the Manager - Accounts. 2. Salary: Rs. 24,000 per month (gross), with other benefits as per company rules. 3. Probation: six months from the date of joining. On satisfactory completion, you will be confirmed in writing. 4. Notice period: 15 days during probation and one month after confirmation, on either side. 5. Working hours: 9.30 a.m. to 6.00 p.m., Monday to Saturday. Please bring your original certificates with one set of copies, an identity proof, two passport-size photographs and a medical fitness certificate when you report for duty. Please sign the duplicate copy of this letter in token of your acceptance and return it to us by 23 November 2026. If we do not receive your acceptance by that date, this offer will lapse. We welcome you to Nila Textiles and wish you a successful career with us. Yours sincerely, For NILA TEXTILES PRIVATE LIMITED G. Ramesh Manager - Human Resources Encl.: Duplicate copy of this letter (for signature)

(b) Joining report - employee reporting for duty

12, Kumaran Road, Tiruppur - 641 601 1 December 2026 To The Manager - Human Resources, Nila Textiles Private Limited, 22, Avinashi Road, Tiruppur - 641 602 Subject: Joining report - Accounts Assistant Reference: Your appointment letter Ref. No. NTPL/HR/APT/2026-27/031 dated 16 November 2026 Dear Sir, As directed in the letter cited above, I report for duty as Accounts Assistant in the Accounts Department today, 1 December 2026, in the forenoon. I have already returned the signed duplicate copy of the appointment letter in token of my acceptance of the terms and conditions. I now submit copies of my educational certificates and identity proof, two passport-size photographs and a medical fitness certificate. I have brought the original certificates for verification and request that they be returned to me after verification. My local address for correspondence is 12, Kumaran Road, Tiruppur - 641 601, and my email address is arun.p@example.com. Please send all official letters to this address until I inform you of any change. I request you to take my joining report on record and to issue the necessary instructions for my posting under the Manager - Accounts. I assure you that I shall perform my duties sincerely and to the best of my ability. Yours faithfully, P. Arun Accounts Assistant Encl.: 1. Copies of educational certificates 2. Copy of identity proof 3. Passport-size photographs (2) 4. Medical fitness certificate

Important vocabulary: appointment letter - the employer's letter formally appointing a selected candidate and stating the terms of service; probation - a trial period after joining, before confirmation; confirmation - making an employee permanent after satisfactory probation; on either side - applying to both the employer and the employee; lapse - come to an end because it is not accepted or used in time; joining report - the employee's written report that he or she has taken up duty; forenoon (F.N.) / afternoon (A.N.) - before / after 12 noon, used to record the exact time of joining or relief; in token of - as a sign of.

Why this format is correct:

  • (a) The appointment letter is on the company letterhead with a Ref. No. It states every key term - post, place, date and time of joining, salary, probation, notice period and working hours - as a numbered list, and asks for written acceptance by a fixed date.
  • (a) It is addressed to the named candidate, so "Dear Mr. Arun," pairs with "Yours sincerely,"; the HR manager signs "For NILA TEXTILES PRIVATE LIMITED".
  • (b) The joining report is short and factual: it quotes the reference of the appointment letter, states the exact date and time of joining ("in the forenoon") and lists the documents submitted. Offices record the date and time of joining because service is counted from them.
  • (b) It is written by an individual, so it starts with the sender's address, not a letterhead. The officer is addressed by designation: "Dear Sir," pairs with "Yours faithfully,".
  • Do not confuse: an appointment letter formally appoints a person and states the terms; a joining report confirms that the person has actually taken up duty; a relieving letter confirms release from a job (see 10.31).

11 Common Exam Mistakes

These 15 mistakes appear again and again in exam answers. In every table the Wrong column shows what NOT to write. Learn the Correct column and the reason. Layout rules are in Sections 1 and 9; register rules are in Section 12.

11.1 Wrong salutation HIGH-FREQUENCY

The salutation must suit the receiver and must match the close (see 13.4). Use one greeting word only and end with a comma.

WrongCorrectWhy
Dear Respected Sir,Respected Sir, (or Dear Sir,)"Dear" and "Respected" are never used together. Choose one.
Hello Sir, (letter to the District Collector)Sir, / Dear Sir/Madam,Spoken, informal greeting. Not used in any letter to an official.
Dear Mr. Raman Sir,Dear Mr. Raman,Title + surname is enough. Adding "Sir" after a name is spoken style.
Respected Sir, (letter to your friend Arun)Dear Arun,Friends are addressed by first name. "Respected" is only for superiors.

11.2 Wrong complimentary close HIGH-FREQUENCY

The close must match the salutation: no name → Yours faithfully; a name → Yours sincerely (see 13.5). Only the first word takes a capital letter.

WrongCorrectWhy
Your's faithfully,Yours faithfully,"Yours" is a possessive pronoun, like "ours" and "hers". It never takes an apostrophe.
Yours Faithfully,Yours faithfully,Capital Y, small f. Only the first word of the close is capitalised.
Dear Sir/Madam, ... Yours sincerely,Dear Sir/Madam, ... Yours faithfully,No name in the salutation → faithfully.
Dear Mr. Raman, ... Yours faithfully,Dear Mr. Raman, ... Yours sincerely,A name in the salutation → sincerely.

11.3 Missing subject

A formal letter without a subject line looks incomplete: the subject tells the reader the purpose at a glance. It is a short noun phrase with no full stop.

WrongCorrectWhy
(No subject line - the salutation comes straight after the receiver's address)Subject: Request for a duplicate hall ticketEvery formal and business letter needs a subject line.
Subject: I am writing to request you to kindly issue me a duplicate hall ticket as I have lost the original.Subject: Request for a duplicate hall ticketThe subject is a short heading (noun phrase), not a full sentence.
Subject: RequestSubject: Request for a duplicate hall ticketToo vague. The subject must name the exact matter.
Subject: My summer holidays (letter to your friend Priya)No subject line - begin with Dear Priya,Informal letters never have a subject line.

11.4 Repetition

Saying the same thing twice wastes the reader's time and your exam time. Make each point once, clearly.

WrongCorrectWhy
I request you to take action. Please take action immediately. Once again I request you to take action.I request you to take immediate action.One clear request is stronger than three weak ones.
Subject: Delay in delivery of chairs - and the first sentence: This letter is about the delay in the delivery of chairs.The 50 chairs we ordered on 2 September 2026 (Order No. 245) have not yet been delivered.Do not copy the subject into the first sentence. Add the facts instead.
return back, repeat again, true facts, each and every, revert backreturn, repeat, facts, every, replyThe extra word repeats the meaning of the first (redundancy).

11.5 Informal language in formal letters

Words you use with friends sound careless, even rude, in a letter to an official. Replace them with standard formal words (see 13.6).

WrongCorrectWhy
Thanks a lot for your help.Thank you for your assistance.Conversational thanks. Use "Thank you".
There are a lot of problems in our area.Our area faces several problems.Informal quantity phrase. Use many, several or a large number of.
The kids in our street have no place to play.The children in our street have no playground.Informal word for children.
I want you to fix the road soon.I request you to repair the road as soon as possible.Sounds like an order. Request politely and use the formal verb.

11.6 Excessive wording

Long, padded phrases do not make a letter more formal; they make it slow. Use the shortest clear expression.

WrongCorrectWhy
I am writing this letter to you in order to bring to your kind notice and attention the fact that the bus stop has no shelter.I wish to bring to your notice that the bus stop has no shelter.Same meaning in half the words.
Please find enclosed herewith a copy of the receipt for your kind perusal.I enclose a copy of the receipt. (or: Please find enclosed a copy of the receipt.)The extra words add nothing. "Enclosed" already means "sent with this letter".
Kindly do the needful in this matter.Please take the necessary action in this matter.Outdated, vague office phrase. Say exactly what you want.
I received your letter and I thank you for the same.Thank you for your letter.Using "the same" as a pronoun is outdated. Say "it" or name the thing.

11.7 Wrong tense HIGH-FREQUENCY

Tense errors are the commonest grammar errors in letters. Check every verb that says how long a problem has lasted.

WrongCorrectWhy
The street lights are not working since three weeks.The street lights have not been working for three weeks.An action that began in the past and is still going on takes the present perfect continuous. Use "for" + a period, "since" + a starting point.
I have received your letter yesterday.I received your letter yesterday.A finished past time (yesterday, last week) takes the simple past.
I did not received the parcel.I did not receive the parcel.After did / did not, use the base form of the verb.
I am having a savings account in your branch.I have a savings account in your branch."Have" meaning "own" is not used in the continuous form.

11.8 Wrong punctuation

Letter punctuation follows fixed rules: a comma after the salutation and the close; no full stop after the subject or the pin code; no exclamation marks in formal letters.

WrongCorrectWhy
Dear Sir/Madam.Dear Sir/Madam,The salutation ends with a comma.
Yours faithfully.Yours faithfully,The close ends with a comma. The name follows on the next line.
Subject: Request for a transfer certificate.Subject: Request for a transfer certificateThe subject is a heading, not a sentence. No full stop.
Please act immediately!!!I request you to take immediate action.No exclamation marks in formal letters. Firmness comes from clear words.

11.9 Incorrect address format

Each part of an address goes on its own line, ending with a comma; the last (pin-code) line has no punctuation. In the table, " / " marks a new line.

WrongCorrectWhy
12, Gandhi Road, K.K. Nagar, Madurai - 625 020 (all on one line)12, Gandhi Road, / K.K. Nagar, / Madurai - 625 020One part of the address per line.
12, Gandhi Road. / K.K. Nagar. / Madurai - 625 020.12, Gandhi Road, / K.K. Nagar, / Madurai - 625 020Commas at line ends, not full stops. Nothing after the pin code.
12, Gandhi Road, / K.K. Nagar, / Madurai.12, Gandhi Road, / K.K. Nagar, / Madurai - 625 020The pin code is part of every postal address. Style: City - 625 020.
To / Commissioner Sir, / Madurai.To / The Commissioner, / Madurai City Municipal Corporation, / Madurai - 625 001"The" + designation, then the office and the pin code. "Sir" belongs in the salutation, not in the address.

EXAM TIP: If the question gives no name or address, write "Examination Hall" as your address and sign as "ABC" or "XYZ". Never reveal your real identity. If the question gives a name and address, use exactly those.

11.10 Incorrect date format HIGH-FREQUENCY

Write the date in one clear style in every letter: 12 October 2026 (day, month in full, year). All-numeric dates are ambiguous.

WrongCorrectWhy
12/10/202612 October 2026Ambiguous: 12 October (Indian and British reading) or 10 December (American reading)?
12-10-2612 October 2026Numbers only and a two-digit year. Unclear.
12 Oct. 202612 October 2026Write the month in full in a letter.
12 october 202612 October 2026The name of a month always begins with a capital letter.

DO NOT CONFUSE: "12th October, 2026" is an acceptable traditional Indian style and "October 12, 2026" is the American style - neither is wrong. This book uses "12 October 2026". Whichever style you choose, use it consistently.

11.11 Using contractions

Contractions (short forms with an apostrophe) belong to speech and informal letters. In formal letters, write the full form.

WrongCorrectWhy
I'm writing to apply for the post of Junior Assistant.I am writing to apply for the post of Junior Assistant.Formal letters use full forms.
We haven't received the goods.We have not received the goods.Full form: have not.
I can't attend the interview on 20 October 2026.I am unable to attend the interview on 20 October 2026."Cannot" is written as one word; "I am unable to" is more formal still.
It's been two weeks since I applied.It has been two weeks since I applied.The short form stands for "it is" or "it has". Do not confuse it with "its" (belonging to it).

11.12 Using slang

Slang, SMS language and emoticons have no place in a formal letter. They make the writer look careless.

WrongCorrectWhy
The service in your branch is pathetic.The service in your branch has been unsatisfactory.Rude, slangy judgement. State the problem politely and factually.
Pls send the docs ASAP.Please send the documents as soon as possible.SMS abbreviations are never used in letters.
Thx for ur quick reply :)Thank you for your prompt reply.SMS spellings and smileys are not letter language.
The food in the hostel mess is awesome, but the rooms are gross.The food in the hostel mess is good, but the rooms are unclean.Slang words for "very good" and "very bad". Use plain standard words.

11.13 Mixing formal and informal tone

Choose ONE register (Section 12) and keep it from the salutation to the close. A formal opening with a chatty ending, or the reverse, confuses the reader.

WrongCorrectWhy
(Leave letter to the Principal) Respected Sir, / Hope you're doing great! I need three days' leave.Respected Sir, / I request you to grant me leave for three days, from 13 to 15 October 2026, to attend my sister's wedding.A chatty opener does not suit a letter to a superior.
I request you to sanction the loan. Thanks a ton, bye!I request you to sanction the loan. Thank you for your time.Formal start, slangy end. Keep one tone throughout.
Dear Priya, / I wish to inform you that I have been admitted to a medical college in Chennai. / Yours faithfully,Dear Priya, / I am so happy to tell you that I have got a seat in a medical college in Chennai! / Your loving friend,A letter to a friend should sound warm, not like an official notice.
Dear Mr. Raman, (your former teacher) / Hey, what's up?Dear Mr. Raman, / I hope you are keeping well.Semi-formal letters are friendly but respectful, never slangy.

11.14 Writing unnecessary information

Include only the facts the reader needs in order to act. Every extra sentence costs you time and hides your main point.

WrongCorrectWhy
(Complaint about a faulty mixer grinder) I am a B.Com. graduate and I love shopping online during Deepavali.The mixer grinder (Order No. 4587, delivered on 3 October 2026) arrived with a cracked jar.Give the order number, the date and the defect - not your life story.
(Leave letter) My cousin's wedding is at Thanjavur; the reception will be grand, with a famous music troupe and two thousand guests.I have to attend my cousin's wedding at Thanjavur on 20 October 2026.One line of reason is enough for a leave letter.
(Job application) My hobbies are watching films and chatting with friends.I hold a certificate in computer applications and can type in English and Tamil.Mention only what makes you suitable for the post.
(Letter to your bank) My neighbour also had trouble with another bank last year.(Leave it out.)Other people's unrelated problems do not belong in your letter.

11.15 Poor paragraphing

One idea per paragraph. The body of a formal letter normally has three parts: opening (why), details (what) and closing (what next) - see 13.1.

WrongCorrectWhy
The whole body written as one long blockThree paragraphs: why - what - what nextA single block hides the purpose and looks unplanned.
Every sentence written as a separate paragraphRelated sentences grouped into 3-4 paragraphsToo many tiny paragraphs break the flow.
Some paragraphs indented, others at the margin; no blank lines between themEvery line at the left margin, one blank line between paragraphs (full block)Mixing layouts looks careless.
The letter ends after the facts, with no requestA closing paragraph with the action wanted and a polite line (Thank you for your time.)Without a closing request, the reader does not know what you want.

11.16 Spot all the errors

Question: You are S. Arun of 25, Anna Street, Fairlands, Salem - 636 016. Write a letter to the Commissioner, Salem City Municipal Corporation, complaining that the street lights in your street have not been working for three weeks.

A student wrote the answer below. It contains 10 deliberate errors from 11.1-11.15. Find them all before you read the corrected version.

25, Anna Street, Fairlands, Salem - 636 016 5/10/26 To The Commissioner, Salem. Dear Respected Sir, I am writing this letter to you in order to humbly bring to your kind notice and attention the fact that the street lights in Anna Street are not working since three weeks. The whole street is dark after 7 p.m. and it's super scary for women and children. We can't let our children go out after dark. Last week an elderly resident fell in the dark near the water tank. I've lived in this street for twenty years and my daughter's wedding was held in the temple at the end of the street. I request you to arrange for the repair of the street lights as soon as possible. Thank you for your attention to this matter. Your's Faithfully, S. Arun

Corrected version:

25, Anna Street, Fairlands, Salem - 636 016 5 October 2026 To The Commissioner, Salem City Municipal Corporation, Salem - 636 001 Subject: Complaint regarding non-functioning street lights in Anna Street, Fairlands Dear Sir/Madam, I wish to bring to your notice that the street lights in Anna Street have not been working for the past three weeks. The whole street is dark after 7 p.m. and it is unsafe for women and children. We cannot let our children go out after dark. Last week an elderly resident fell in the dark near the water tank. I request you to arrange for the repair of the street lights as soon as possible. Thank you for your attention to this matter. Yours faithfully, S. Arun

The 10 errors and their fixes:

  1. Date (11.10): written only in numbers, with a two-digit year - ambiguous. Fix: 5 October 2026
  2. Receiver's address (11.9): incomplete - no office name, no pin code, and a full stop at the end. Fix: The Commissioner, / Salem City Municipal Corporation, / Salem - 636 001
  3. Subject line (11.3): missing. Fix: Subject: Complaint regarding non-functioning street lights in Anna Street, Fairlands
  4. Salutation (11.1): "Dear" and "Respected" used together. Fix: Dear Sir/Madam, (the receiver is an official who is not named)
  5. Excessive wording (11.6): a padded 21-word lead-in. Fix: I wish to bring to your notice that ... (8 words)
  6. Wrong tense (11.7): present continuous with "since" + a period. Fix: have not been working for the past three weeks
  7. Contractions (11.11): three short forms in a formal letter. Fix: it is, cannot (the third goes out with error 9)
  8. Slang (11.12): a slang phrase for "very frightening"; it also mixes the tone (11.13). Fix: unsafe
  9. Unnecessary information (11.14): years of residence and a family wedding have nothing to do with street lights. Fix: delete the sentence
  10. Complimentary close (11.2): an apostrophe in "Yours" and a capital F. Fix: Yours faithfully,

EXAM TIP: Keep the last two minutes of every letter answer for a quick check: address lines and pin code, date in words, "To", subject, the salutation-close pair, three paragraphs, tenses, no contractions or slang, and the name below the close.

12 Formal vs Semi-formal vs Informal

Register = the level of formality of a letter. Decide it FIRST. The salutation, the close, the layout and the vocabulary all follow from it.

12.1 The high-yield comparison table HIGH-FREQUENCY

FeatureFormalSemi-formalInformal
A. The eight must-know features
ReceiverOfficials, offices, organisations, companies, banks, editors, unknown persons; superiors on official mattersPeople you know but are not close to: a teacher, neighbour, landlord or a friend's parentFamily members, relatives and close friends
ToneImpersonal, polite, objective, to the pointPolite and respectful, but personalWarm, friendly, conversational
SalutationDear Sir/Madam, / Sir, / Madam, (no name); Respected Sir, / Respected Madam, (superiors); Dear Mr. Raman, (a named person)Dear Mr./Mrs./Ms./Dr. + surname, e.g. Dear Mr. Raman,Dear Priya, / Dear Appa, / My dear Kavin,
LanguageStandard formal English; formal words (request, inform, receive); no slang, SMS language or exclamation marksSimple, courteous standard English; no slangEveryday conversational English; personal news and feelings
ClosingYours faithfully, (with Sir/Madam); Yours sincerely, (with a name)Yours sincerely,Your loving friend, / Your loving son, / Your loving daughter, / Yours affectionately, / With love,
ContractionsNever: I am, do not, cannotAvoid them (safer in exams)Allowed: I'm, can't, don't
PurposeOfficial or business matters: applications, complaints, requests, orders, inquiriesPersonal matters with people you know formally: thanks, apology, request, invitation, congratulations, condolenceSharing news, feelings, invitations, advice and good wishes
ExampleComplaint to the Commissioner about irregular garbage collection; leave letter to the PrincipalLetter thanking your former teacher Mr. Raman for a recommendation letter; request to your landlord for repairsLetter to your friend Priya about your holiday; letter to Appa asking for money for a school trip
B. Layout and extras
Subject lineCompulsory (before the salutation in our layout)OptionalNever
Receiver's addressYes, after "To": designation, office, pin codeYes: the receiver's name and addressNo
LengthShort and to the pointShort to mediumFlexible; can be longer
ParagraphsUsually three: why - what - what next (13.1)Two to four short paragraphsFree flow: greeting, news, good wishes
Sign-off nameFull name with initial (R. Karthik); designation for office letters; "ABC" / "XYZ" in exams if no name is givenFull name (R. Karthik)First name only (Kavin)
Postscript (P.S.)Avoid - put every point in the bodyRare; better avoidedAllowed for an afterthought (P.S. Say hello to Amma.)

DO NOT CONFUSE: A formal letter to a named person and a semi-formal letter both use "Dear Mr. Raman, ... Yours sincerely,". The difference is the PURPOSE: an official or business matter = formal; a personal matter with someone you know formally = semi-formal.

12.2 Decide the register in 10 seconds

Read the question and find the row that fits. The pair to use is given with each register.

Question to askIf yes → register
Am I writing to an office, an official, a company, a bank or an editor - or to someone whose name I do not know?Formal Dear Sir/Madam, (or Sir,) → Yours faithfully,
Is it an official or business matter, but the question names the receiver (e.g. Mr. Raman, the Sales Manager)?Formal (named) Dear Mr. Raman, → Yours sincerely,
Am I a student or an employee asking my Principal or Head of Office for leave or permission?Formal Respected Sir, / Respected Madam, → Yours faithfully, (students may also use Yours obediently,)
Is it an official matter (loan, admission, complaint) even though the receiver is my relative or friend?Formal - the purpose decides. Write to the office, not to the person: Dear Sir/Madam, → Yours faithfully,
Am I writing about a personal matter to someone I know but am not close to (teacher, neighbour, landlord, a friend's parent)?Semi-formal Dear Mr. Raman, → Yours sincerely,
Am I writing to a family member or a close friend about personal news?Informal Dear Priya, / Dear Appa, → Your loving friend, / Your loving son,
Is one government officer writing personally to another to draw attention or speed up a matter?Demi-official (D.O.) letter: Dear Shri Kumar, (Tamil Nadu: Dear Thiru Kumar,) → Yours sincerely, (see 13.8)
Is it a government communication between Ministries or Departments, or an order, circular or notice?O.M. / Office Order / Circular / Notice: no salutation and no close (see Section 6)

12.3 Must-remember points

MUST REMEMBER: The salutation decides the close. No name (Sir/Madam) → Yours faithfully. A name (Dear Mr. Raman) → Yours sincerely. Family and friends → Your loving friend / Your loving son / Yours affectionately / With love.

MUST REMEMBER: Formal = receiver's address AND subject line, both compulsory. Semi-formal = receiver's name and address; subject optional. Informal = neither - only your short address and the date before "Dear Priya,".

MUST REMEMBER: Purpose and relationship decide the register, not the topic. Once chosen, keep ONE register from salutation to close: no contractions, slang or exclamation marks in a formal letter; no official phrases in a letter to a friend.

13 Letter-Writing Tricks

Short memory aids for the points examiners test most. Learn the trick, then check it against the table below it.

13.1 Letter structure: Why - What - What next

Every letter has a top (addresses, date, subject, salutation), a body and a bottom (close, name, Encl.). The top and bottom follow fixed patterns (13.2). The body is where most answers go wrong, so build it in three parts.

MEMORY TRICK: WHY - WHAT - WHAT NEXT. Paragraph 1 says WHY you are writing. The middle paragraph(s) give WHAT happened (the facts). The last paragraph says WHAT NEXT (the action you want). If a sentence answers none of the three, cut it.

PartQuestion it answersUseful startersExample (street-light complaint)
OpeningWHY am I writing?I wish to bring to your notice that ... / I am writing to apply for ... / With reference to your letter No. ... dated ...I wish to bring to your notice that the street lights in Anna Street have not been working for three weeks.
Main (one or two paragraphs)WHAT are the facts?Dates, places, numbers, names; one idea per paragraphThe street is dark after 7 p.m. Last week an elderly resident fell in the dark near the water tank.
ClosingWHAT NEXT - what do I want?I request you to ... / I would be grateful if you could ... / I look forward to your reply. / Thank you for your time.I request you to arrange for the repair of the street lights as soon as possible.

The same trick works for informal letters: WHY = greeting and reason for writing (a reply, some news); WHAT = your news and feelings; WHAT NEXT = plans, good wishes and regards to the family.

EXAM TIP: Leave one blank line between paragraphs and do not indent (full block). Three clear paragraphs show planning before the examiner reads a word.

13.2 Formal letter sequence HIGH-FREQUENCY

MEMORY TRICK: S-D-R-S-S-B-C-S = "Sunday Dinner Remains Special - Sambar, Biryani, Curd, Sweet". Eight parts, top to bottom.

LetterTrick wordPart of the letterModel ( / = new line)
SSundaySender's address (no name)12, Gandhi Road, / K.K. Nagar, / Madurai - 625 020
DDinnerDate12 October 2026
RRemainsReceiver's addressTo / The Commissioner, / Madurai City Municipal Corporation, / Madurai - 625 001
SSpecialSubjectSubject: Complaint regarding irregular garbage collection in K.K. Nagar
SSambarSalutationDear Sir/Madam,
BBiryaniBodyWhy - What - What next (13.1)
CCurdComplimentary closeYours faithfully,
SSweetSignature (name)R. Karthik

Extras: a Reference line goes after the subject, only when you reply to earlier correspondence. Encl.: and Copy to: go below the name, only when needed.

MEMORY TRICK: Special comes before Sambar: the Subject comes before the Salutation in our layout. And your NAME is the last dish (Sweet), never the first - only your address goes at the top.

DO NOT CONFUSE: Two accepted variants exist. (1) In the traditional British / commerce-textbook layout, the subject comes AFTER the salutation. (2) The "From-To" format taught in many Tamil Nadu schools: "From" + your name and address, "To" + the receiver's address, salutation, subject, body, "Thanking you," then "Yours faithfully," and your name, with "Place:" and "Date:" at the bottom-left; school exams may also ask for the address on the envelope. Both are accepted. This book's model letters use the full-block S-D-R-S-S-B-C-S order; if a question asks for a particular format, follow the question.

13.3 Business transaction sequence

MEMORY TRICK: "Intelligent Queens Order Costly Diamonds; Delivery Includes Proper Receipts" = Inquiry, Quotation, Order, Confirmation, Dispatch, Delivery, Invoice, Payment, Receipt.

StepTrick wordLetter or documentFrom → ToWhat it does
1IntelligentInquiryBuyer → SellerAsks for prices, terms, a catalogue or samples
2QueensQuotationSeller → BuyerStates prices, terms and conditions; valid for a stated period (firm offer)
3OrderOrder letter / Purchase Order (PO)Buyer → SellerPlaces the order; a PO is a formal numbered document
4CostlyConfirmation (acceptance) of orderSeller → BuyerAccepts the order and confirms the delivery date; once accepted, the PO is binding
5DiamondsDispatch adviceSeller → BuyerSays the goods have been sent; gives the LR / RR / B/L / AWB number
6DeliveryDeliverySeller (through the transporter) → BuyerGoods arrive with a delivery challan; the buyer records them in a Goods Received Note (GRN)
7IncludesInvoice (tax invoice)Seller → BuyerThe bill: quantity, price, taxes, total and due date
8ProperPaymentBuyer → SellerPays by cheque or bank transfer, with a covering letter
9ReceiptsReceiptSeller → BuyerAcknowledges the payment

EXAM TIP: In real trade the invoice often travels with the goods or the dispatch advice; the trick gives the logical order. A proforma invoice is different: a preliminary bill sent BEFORE supply, for advance payment or import formalities.

EXAM TIP: Two side roads. Goods faulty → Complaint → Claim → Adjustment (13.7). Payment late → first reminder (polite) → second reminder (firmer) → final notice (warns of legal action).

13.4 Salutations

MEMORY TRICK: Distance decides the salutation. FAR (an office, no name) → Sir/Madam. MIDDLE (you know the name but are not close) → Mr./Mrs./Dr. + surname. NEAR (friends, family) → first name or relation (Appa, Amma). ABOVE you (Principal, boss) → Respected Sir/Madam.

ReceiverSalutationCloseRegister
Unknown person, company or organisationDear Sir/Madam,Yours faithfully,Formal
Official by designation (Collector, Commissioner, Branch Manager)Sir, / Madam, / Dear Sir/Madam,Yours faithfully,Formal
Editor of a newspaperSir, / Dear Sir/Madam,Yours faithfully,Formal
Principal or Head of Office (your superior)Respected Sir, / Respected Madam,Yours faithfully, (students may also use Yours obediently,)Formal
Named official or business contactDear Mr. Raman,Yours sincerely,Formal
Teacher, neighbour, landlord, a friend's parentDear Mr. Raman, / Dear Mrs. Raman,Yours sincerely,Semi-formal
FriendDear Priya, / My dear Kavin,Your loving friend, / With love,Informal
ParentsDear Appa, / Dear Amma,Your loving son, / Your loving daughter,Informal
Younger relativeMy dear Kavin,Yours affectionately,Informal
Government officer to officer (D.O. letter)Dear Shri Kumar, (Tamil Nadu: Dear Thiru Kumar,)Yours sincerely,Demi-official
O.M., Office Order, Order, Notification, government Circular, Notice, internal MemoNoneNoneOfficial forms

DO NOT CONFUSE: "Dear" OR "Respected" - never both together. Put a comma after every salutation; the colon (Dear Sir:) is American business style, not used in Indian exams. "Sir" goes in the salutation, never in the receiver's address.

13.5 Complimentary closes HIGH-FREQUENCY

MEMORY TRICK: Faceless (Sir/Madam) → Faithfully; Surname (Dear Mr. Raman) → Sincerely; Family → Lovingly / Affectionately. F goes with F, S goes with S. "Lovingly" stands for the loving closes: Your loving son, / Your loving daughter, / Your loving friend, / With love,

DO NOT CONFUSE: Faceless and Family both begin with F, but only Faceless gets Faithfully. A letter to Appa or Priya never ends with "Yours faithfully,".

CloseGoes withUsed for
Yours faithfully,Dear Sir/Madam, / Sir, / Madam, / Respected Sir, / Respected Madam,Officials, organisations, editors, unknown persons, superiors
Yours sincerely,Dear Mr. Raman, / Dear Shri Kumar,Named persons in formal letters, all semi-formal letters, D.O. letters
Yours obediently,Respected Sir, / Respected Madam,Traditional: school students writing to the Principal
Your loving son, / Your loving daughter,Dear Appa, / Dear Amma,Parents
Your loving friend, / With love,Dear Priya,Friends
Yours affectionately,My dear Kavin,Family, especially elders writing to younger relatives
No closeNo salutationO.M., Office Order, Order, Notification, government Circular, Notice, internal Memo

MUST REMEMBER: Format of the close: capital first letter only, no apostrophe, a comma at the end, and the name on the next line (Yours faithfully, / R. Karthik). Variants you may see but should not use in model answers: "Sincerely yours," and "Sincerely," (American business), "Yours truly," (formal, also seen in letters to the editor).

EXAM TIP: "Thanking you," on a line of its own before the close is accepted Indian usage. The better modern style is a sentence inside the last paragraph: "Thank you for your time." Avoid "Thanking you in anticipation" - it is dated and presumes the answer.

13.6 Formal vocabulary

MEMORY TRICK: Upgrade the verb. In a formal letter, swap the everyday word for its formal partner: get → receive, ask → request, tell → inform. Keep the sentence short - only the word changes.

Informal wordFormal wordExample
getreceive / obtainWe received your quotation dated 2 October 2026.
askrequest / inquireI request you to issue a duplicate hall ticket.
tellinformI wish to inform you that the goods have been dispatched.
needrequireWe require 200 reams of A4 paper.
helpassistKindly assist us in tracing the missing parcel.
buypurchaseWe wish to purchase ten desktop computers.
giveprovidePlease provide details of your delivery terms.
startcommenceThe work will commence on 1 November 2026.
endconcludeThe training programme will conclude on 30 October 2026.
sorryregretWe regret the inconvenience caused to you.
checkverifyPlease verify the entries in my passbook.
fixrectify / repairPlease rectify the error in my electricity bill.
sendforward / dispatchI am forwarding my application through proper channel.
aboutregarding / concerningSubject: Complaint regarding delay in delivery
enoughsufficientThe present stock is not sufficient to meet the demand.
tryendeavour / attemptWe shall endeavour to deliver the goods by Friday.
answerreply / respondI look forward to your reply.
find outascertainPlease ascertain the cause of the delay.
put offpostponeThe meeting has been postponed to 20 October 2026.
keepretainPlease retain a copy for your records.
mistakeerrorWe regret the error in our invoice.
money backrefundPlease refund the amount of Rs. 2,500.
jobpost / positionI wish to apply for the post of Junior Assistant.
a lot ofmany / a large number ofA large number of residents have complained.

13.7 Inquiry vs quotation vs order vs complaint vs claim vs adjustment HIGH-FREQUENCY

MEMORY TRICK: ASK → PRICE → BUY → GRUMBLE → GET PAID → PUT RIGHT. The two P-words, PRICE and PUT RIGHT, are the seller's letters (P = the Provider). The other four come from the buyer.

LetterWho writesTrick wordMeaning
Inquiry (enquiry)BuyerASKAsks for information: prices, terms, a catalogue or samples. "Inquiry letter" and "enquiry letter" are both accepted.
QuotationSellerPRICEThe seller's statement of prices, terms and conditions, sent in reply to an inquiry (before the order). It has a validity period (firm offer).
OrderBuyerBUYA letter placing an order. A Purchase Order (PO) is a formal numbered document issued by the buyer; it is binding once the seller accepts it.
ComplaintBuyerGRUMBLEReports a problem: defect, delay, shortage or wrong goods.
ClaimBuyerGET PAIDDemands a specific remedy or compensation: refund, replacement or price reduction.
AdjustmentSellerPUT RIGHTThe seller's reply that settles a complaint or claim: full grant, partial grant, or a polite refusal with reasons.

DO NOT CONFUSE: A COMPLAINT only reports the problem; a CLAIM asks for a specific remedy. An ADJUSTMENT letter is written by the SELLER, never by the buyer. A QUOTATION is a firm price; an ESTIMATE is an approximate cost; a TENDER is a formal (often sealed) offer made in response to an invitation, usually from public bodies. More pairs: see Section 15.

EXAM TIP: Spelling: in the British tradition "enquiry" means asking for information and "inquiry" means a formal investigation; American English uses "inquiry" for both. Either spelling is accepted for the letter - use one consistently.

13.8 Government forms tricks

Full formats are in Section 6. These tricks fix the salutation, the close and the key phrase of each form.

FormMemory trickSalutation → CloseKey features and use
Official letter"I am Directed" = OfficialSir, / Madam, → Yours faithfully,From a Government office to other governments, bodies, officials or the public; begins "I am directed to ..."; signed with designation
Demi-official (D.O.) letterD.O. = Dear Officer → sincerelyDear Shri Kumar, (Tamil Nadu: Dear Thiru Kumar,) → Yours sincerely,Officer to officer; personal, friendly tone; first person singular; to draw personal attention, expedite a matter or exchange views; D.O. No. at the top
