Answer: contractions - Formal letters avoid contractions, slang, exclamation marks and SMS language; contractions are allowed only in informal letters.

FB11. "Two copies of the audited accounts are sent ________ for your verification." (One word meaning "with this letter".) (PYQ-style)

Answer

Answer: herewith - It means "with this letter"; never combine it with "enclosed" ("Please find enclosed herewith" is redundant, so write "I enclose").

FB12. The buyer's own document recording that the goods have arrived and have been checked is the ________. (PYQ-style)

Answer

Answer: Goods Received Note (GRN) - The buyer prepares it on receipt of the goods, while the delivery challan travels with the goods from the seller.

FB13. A D.O. letter from a Central Government officer to Shri R. K. Verma begins "Dear ________ Verma," and ends "Yours sincerely,". (PYQ-style)

Answer

Answer: Shri - Because a D.O. letter is personal in tone, it names the officer with the honorific "Shri" (Tamil Nadu Government usage: "Thiru").

FB14. The line "Copy forwarded to the Tahsildar, Vellore, for information and necessary action." at the foot of a government letter is called an ________. (PYQ-style)

Answer

Answer: endorsement - In government correspondence, an endorsement forwards copies of a communication to others (or returns a paper to the sender).

FB15. A school student writing to the Principal with "Respected Sir," may close with "Yours faithfully," or with the traditional "________". (PYQ-style)

Answer

Answer: Yours obediently, - This traditional close is accepted for a student writing to the Principal; "Yours faithfully," is equally correct.

FB16. "Any breach of these conditions, or of any part ________, will lead to cancellation of the licence." (One word meaning "of it" or "of them".) (PYQ-style)

Answer

Answer: thereof - It means "of it / of them", referring to the thing or things just mentioned (here, the conditions).

FB17. The informal "Can you tell me the fee?" becomes formal as "I would be ________ if you could inform me of the fee." (PYQ-style)

Answer

Answer: grateful (also "obliged") - Formal requests are softened with "I would be grateful if you could ..." instead of a direct question.

FB18. In Tamil Nadu, the Secretariat departments issue their decisions as a "G.O.", which stands for ________. (PYQ-style)

Answer

Answer: Government Order - G.O.s are issued by the Secretariat departments, while Heads of Departments and District officers issue orders in the "Proceedings" format.

FB19. Kavin's letter to his father begins "Dear Appa," and ends "Your loving ________," followed by his first name. (PYQ-style)

Answer

Answer: son - Informal letters to family members end with a close such as "Your loving son," and only the writer's first name.

FB20. When a cheque is dishonoured, the payee may send a legal demand notice under Section ________ of the Negotiable Instruments Act, 1881. (PYQ-style)

Answer

Answer: 138 - The notice is sent within 30 days of receiving the bank's return memo, and the drawer then has 15 days to pay.

FB21. Instead of the outdated "Kindly revert back", a modern formal letter says "Please ________ by 20 October 2026." (PYQ-style)

Answer

Answer: reply (or "respond") - "Revert back" is outdated and redundant; "reply" or "respond" is the correct formal word.

FB22. An employee's application to the Director is forwarded through the immediate superior officer. This is called sending it "through ________". (PYQ-style)

Answer

Answer: proper channel - The application goes through the immediate superior, and a "Through: ..." line is written above or below the addressee.

FB23. "We have received your cheque and will deposit the same tomorrow" is better written as "We have received your cheque and will deposit ________ tomorrow." (PYQ-style)

Answer

Answer: it - "The same" used as a pronoun is outdated; modern formal English uses "it".

FB24. Orders issued by a District Collector in Tamil Nadu in the format "Present: Thiru ..., Rc. No. ..., Dated ..., Sub: ..., Ref: ..., ORDER: ..." are called ________. (PYQ-style)

Answer

Answer: Proceedings - Heads of Departments and District officers in Tamil Nadu issue orders in the "Proceedings" format, while Secretariat departments issue G.O.s.

FB25. The prefix written before the name of a firm, as in "________ Kaveri Traders, Erode", is short for "Messrs". (PYQ-style)

Answer

Answer: M/s. - "M/s." (Messrs) is written before the name of a firm; individuals get Mr./Mrs. or, in Tamil Nadu Government usage, Thiru/Tmt./Selvi.

16.3 Match the Following (20)

Match List I with List II and choose the correct code from options (a) to (d).

M1. Match List I with List II: (PYQ-style)

List I (Letter)List II (Purpose)
A. Inquiry letter1. Seller states prices, terms and a validity period
B. Quotation2. Seller's reply that settles a complaint or claim
C. Complaint letter3. Prospective buyer asks for prices, terms or samples
D. Adjustment letter4. Buyer reports a defect, delay, shortage or wrong goods
  1. A-3, B-2, C-4, D-1
  2. A-4, B-1, C-3, D-2
  3. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2
  4. A-1, B-3, C-2, D-4
Answer

Answer: (c) - The buyer asks (inquiry), the seller answers with prices (quotation), the buyer reports a problem (complaint) and the seller settles it (adjustment).

M2. Match List I with List II: (PYQ-style)

List I (Letter)List II (Purpose)
A. Order letter1. Asks a customer to pay an overdue amount
B. Claim letter2. Places an order, stating quantity, price and delivery terms
C. Collection letter3. Gives the same information to many customers at once
D. Circular letter4. Demands a specific remedy such as a refund, replacement or price reduction
  1. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3
  2. A-4, B-2, C-1, D-3
  3. A-2, B-4, C-3, D-1
  4. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2
Answer

Answer: (a) - An order letter places the order, a claim letter demands a specific remedy, a collection letter asks for an overdue payment, and a circular sends one message to many readers.

M3. Match List I with List II: (PYQ-style)

List I (Salutation)List II (Complimentary close)
A. "Dear Sir/Madam," (to the Manager of a bank)1. Your loving son,
B. "Dear Mr. Raman," (formal letter to a named person)2. Yours obediently,
C. "Dear Appa," (son to father)3. Yours sincerely,
D. "Respected Madam," (school student to the Principal)4. Yours faithfully,
  1. A-3, B-4, C-1, D-2
  2. A-4, B-3, C-2, D-1
  3. A-2, B-3, C-1, D-4
  4. A-4, B-3, C-1, D-2
Answer

Answer: (d) - "Dear Sir/Madam," can take only "Yours faithfully,", so the student's "Respected Madam," takes the traditional "Yours obediently,"; the named person gets "Yours sincerely," and the son writes "Your loving son,".

M4. Match List I with List II: (PYQ-style)

List I (Communication)List II (Complimentary close)
A. Letter to the Editor, The Hindu, Chennai, beginning "Sir,"1. Your loving friend,
B. D.O. letter beginning "Dear Thiru Kumar,"2. None: no salutation and no complimentary close
C. Office Order3. Yours faithfully,
D. Letter to a friend beginning "Dear Priya,"4. Yours sincerely,
  1. A-4, B-3, C-2, D-1
  2. A-3, B-4, C-2, D-1
  3. A-3, B-2, C-4, D-1
  4. A-1, B-4, C-3, D-2
Answer

Answer: (b) - A letter to the editor takes "Yours faithfully,", a D.O. letter "Yours sincerely,", a letter to a friend an informal close, and an Office Order has neither salutation nor close.

M5. Match List I with List II: (PYQ-style)

List I (Government form)List II (Feature)
A. Office Memorandum1. First person, personal tone; "Dear Shri ...," and "Yours sincerely,"
B. Demi-official letter2. Third person ("The undersigned is directed to ..."); no salutation or close
C. Notification3. Note at the foot forwarding copies: "Copy forwarded to ..."
D. Endorsement4. Published in the Official Gazette: rules, appointments, promotions
  1. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3
  2. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3
  3. A-2, B-1, C-3, D-4
  4. A-3, B-4, C-1, D-2
Answer

Answer: (a) - O.M. = third person with no salutation or close; D.O. = first person and personal tone; notification = Official Gazette; endorsement = "Copy forwarded to ...".

M6. Match List I with List II: (PYQ-style)

List I (Government form)List II (Used for)
A. Office Order1. Financial sanctions and disciplinary matters
B. Order2. Public announcement of important policy decisions or of the appointment of committees and commissions, published in the Gazette
C. Resolution3. The same information sent to many offices or persons
D. Circular4. Internal instructions such as distribution of work, grant of leave and postings within the office
  1. A-1, B-4, C-2, D-3
  2. A-4, B-1, C-3, D-2
  3. A-4, B-1, C-2, D-3
  4. A-3, B-2, C-1, D-4
Answer

Answer: (c) - Office Order = internal instructions; Order = financial sanctions and disciplinary matters; Resolution = policy decisions and committees (in the Gazette); Circular = the same message to many.

M7. Match List I with List II: (PYQ-style)

List I (Banking term)List II (Meaning)
A. Letter of Credit (L/C)1. Bank's written undertaking to pay the seller/exporter when specified documents are presented
B. Bank reference2. Intimation, usually from a bank, that an account has been credited
C. Stop-payment letter3. Bank's statement about its customer's account and general standing, given on request
D. Credit advice4. Account holder's instruction to the bank not to pay a particular cheque
  1. A-3, B-1, C-4, D-2
  2. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3
  3. A-1, B-3, C-2, D-4
  4. A-1, B-3, C-4, D-2
Answer

Answer: (d) - L/C = bank's undertaking to pay the exporter against documents; bank reference = the bank's report on its customer; stop-payment = do not pay this cheque; credit advice = the account has been credited.

M8. Match List I with List II: (PYQ-style)

List I (Document)List II (Description)
A. Credit note1. Seller's bill for goods supplied, showing quantity, price, taxes, total and due date
B. Debit note2. Issued by the seller; reduces the amount the buyer owes
C. Proforma invoice3. Issued by the buyer when returning goods or claiming a deduction
D. Tax invoice4. Preliminary bill sent before supply, for advance payment or import formalities
  1. A-3, B-2, C-4, D-1
  2. A-2, B-3, C-4, D-1
  3. A-2, B-3, C-1, D-4
  4. A-4, B-1, C-2, D-3
Answer

Answer: (b) - The credit note (seller) reduces the buyer's dues, the debit note (buyer) goes with returns or deductions, the proforma invoice comes before supply and the tax invoice after it.

M9. Match List I with List II: (PYQ-style)

List I (Stage)List II (Document)
A. Buyer asks for prices and terms1. Goods Received Note
B. Seller replies with prices, terms and a validity period2. Quotation
C. Buyer confirms the purchase with a formal numbered document3. Inquiry letter
D. Buyer checks the goods on arrival and records their receipt4. Purchase Order
  1. A-2, B-3, C-4, D-1
  2. A-3, B-2, C-4, D-1
  3. A-3, B-2, C-1, D-4
  4. A-4, B-1, C-3, D-2
Answer

Answer: (b) - The trade cycle runs inquiry → quotation → purchase order → goods sent with a delivery challan → Goods Received Note.

M10. Match List I with List II: (PYQ-style)

List I (Mode of dispatch)List II (Transport document)
A. By road (lorry)1. RR
B. By rail2. AWB
C. By sea3. B/L
D. By air4. LR
  1. A-1, B-4, C-3, D-2
  2. A-4, B-1, C-2, D-3
  3. A-4, B-3, C-1, D-2
  4. A-4, B-1, C-3, D-2
Answer

Answer: (d) - LR = Lorry Receipt (road), RR = Railway Receipt (rail), B/L = Bill of Lading (sea) and AWB = Airway Bill (air).

M11. Match List I with List II: (PYQ-style)

List I (Stage)List II (Document)
A. Payment is overdue; first letter to the customer1. Legal demand notice under Section 138 of the Negotiable Instruments Act, 1881
B. Two reminders have been ignored2. Polite first reminder
C. The bank returns a cheque unpaid3. Return memo
D. The payee takes action on the dishonoured cheque4. Final notice warning of legal action
  1. A-2, B-4, C-3, D-1
  2. A-4, B-2, C-3, D-1
  3. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3
  4. A-3, B-1, C-2, D-4
Answer

Answer: (a) - Collection moves from a polite reminder to a final notice; on dishonour the bank gives a return memo, and the payee may send a Section 138 demand notice within 30 days of receiving it.

M12. Match List I with List II: (PYQ-style)

List I (Abbreviation)List II (Expansion)
A. CC1. Postscript: a short afterthought added below the signature
B. BCC2. Blind carbon copy: a copy sent without the main receiver knowing
C. P.S.3. Enclosure(s): papers sent with the letter
D. Encl.4. Carbon copy: a copy sent to another person, named on the letter
  1. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3
  2. A-4, B-2, C-3, D-1
  3. A-4, B-2, C-1, D-3
  4. A-1, B-3, C-4, D-2
Answer

Answer: (c) - CC = carbon copy (named on the letter), BCC = blind carbon copy (hidden from the main receiver), P.S. = postscript and Encl. = enclosure(s).

M13. Match List I with List II: (PYQ-style)

List I (Abbreviation)List II (Expansion)
A. E&OE1. Errors and Omissions Excepted
B. w.e.f.2. per procurationem: signing on behalf of another person
C. p.p.3. Letter of Credit
D. L/C4. with effect from
  1. A-1, B-2, C-4, D-3
  2. A-4, B-1, C-2, D-3
  3. A-3, B-4, C-2, D-1
  4. A-1, B-4, C-2, D-3
Answer

Answer: (d) - E&OE (printed on invoices) = Errors and Omissions Excepted; w.e.f. = with effect from; p.p. = per procurationem (on behalf of); L/C = Letter of Credit.

M14. Match List I with List II: (PYQ-style)

List I (Abbreviation)List II (Expansion)
A. PO1. Demi-official: an officer-to-officer letter in a personal tone
B. GRN2. Office Memorandum: a third-person communication between departments
C. D.O.3. Purchase Order: a formal numbered order issued by the buyer
D. O.M.4. Goods Received Note: the buyer's record of goods received
  1. A-3, B-4, C-1, D-2
  2. A-4, B-3, C-1, D-2
  3. A-3, B-4, C-2, D-1
  4. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3
Answer

Answer: (a) - PO = Purchase Order and GRN = Goods Received Note are trade documents; D.O. = Demi-official and O.M. = Office Memorandum are government forms.

M15. Match List I with List II: (PYQ-style)

List I (Phrase)List II (Function)
A. "With reference to your letter No. 45/2026 dated 12 October 2026, ..."1. Conveys unwelcome news politely
B. "I shall be grateful if you could ..."2. Links the letter to earlier correspondence
C. "I regret to inform you that ..."3. Makes a polite request
D. "I look forward to your early reply."4. Closes the letter by asking for a response
  1. A-3, B-2, C-1, D-4
  2. A-2, B-3, C-1, D-4
  3. A-2, B-1, C-3, D-4
  4. A-4, B-3, C-2, D-1
Answer

Answer: (b) - "With reference to" links, "I shall be grateful if" requests, "I regret to inform you" gives bad news politely, and "I look forward to" closes by inviting a reply.

M16. Match List I with List II: (PYQ-style)

List I (Expression)List II (Function)
A. "I am directed to ..."1. Marks a demi-official letter
B. "The undersigned is directed to ..."2. Endorsement forwarding a copy
C. "Copy forwarded to the Tahsildar, Salem, for information."3. Opening of an Office Memorandum (third person)
D. "D.O. No. 12/2026" written at the top4. Opening of an official letter from a Government office

  4. A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3
Answer

Answer: (c) - "I am directed to" opens an official letter, "The undersigned is directed to" opens an O.M., "Copy forwarded to" is an endorsement, and a D.O. No. at the top marks a D.O. letter.

M17. Match List I with List II: (PYQ-style)

A. "Please do the needful."1. "Please reply."
B. "Kindly revert back."2. "I enclose ..."
C. "Please find enclosed herewith ..."3. "Please take the necessary action."
D. "Kindly be informed that ..."4. "This is to inform you that ..."
  1. A-3, B-1, C-2, D-4

  3. A-3, B-2, C-1, D-4
  4. A-4, B-1, C-2, D-3
Answer

Answer: (a) - "Do the needful" becomes "take the necessary action", "revert back" becomes "reply", "enclosed herewith" becomes "I enclose", and "Kindly be informed" becomes "This is to inform you".

M18. Match List I with List II: (PYQ-style)

List I (Letter)List II (Purpose)
A. Letter of condolence1. Expresses views on a public issue, for publication
B. Letter of acknowledgement2. Expresses sympathy on a death
C. Letter to the editor3. Expresses regret for a mistake and offers to set it right
D. Letter of apology4. Confirms that a letter, goods or a payment has been received
  1. A-4, B-2, C-1, D-3

  3. A-3, B-4, C-1, D-2
  4. A-2, B-4, C-1, D-3
Answer

Answer: (d) - Condolence = sympathy on a death; acknowledgement = confirms receipt; letter to the editor = views on a public issue for publication; apology = regret plus a remedy.

M19. Match List I with List II: (PYQ-style)

A. Ex-works1. Seller pays cost, insurance and freight up to the destination port

C. FOB3. Buyer collects the goods from the seller's premises

  4. A-2, B-1, C-3, D-4
Answer

Answer: (c) - Ex-works = the buyer collects at the seller's premises; FOR = Free on Rail; FOB = the seller's cost and risk end on loading on board the ship; CIF = the seller pays cost, insurance and freight to the destination port.

M20. Match List I with List II: (PYQ-style)

Answer: (b) - In Tamil Nadu Government usage Selvi = Miss and Tmt. (Thirumathi) = Mrs.; "Hon'ble" is used for Ministers and judges, and "M/s." (Messrs) for firms.

16.4 Identify the Letter Type (20)

ID1. Identify the type of letter: "Dear Kavin, It was wonderful to get your letter after so long. Amma and I can't wait to see you in Chennai this summer!" (PYQ-style)

  1. Semi-formal letter
  2. Personal (informal) letter
  3. Formal letter
  4. Demi-official letter
Answer

Answer: (b) - A first-name salutation, family news, a contraction (can't) and an exclamation mark show a personal letter to a friend or relative.

ID2. A tenant writes to the house owner: "Dear Mr. Raman, I am writing to inform you that the water tank on the terrace has been leaking since last week. I would be grateful if you could arrange for a plumber." The letter ends "Yours sincerely, Arun Kumar". Identify the type of letter. (PYQ-style)

  1. Informal letter
  2. Official letter
  3. Office Memorandum
  4. Semi-formal letter
Answer

Answer: (d) - A known person addressed politely as "Dear Mr. + Surname," with "Yours sincerely," is a semi-formal letter.

ID3. A resident writes to the Commissioner, Madurai City Municipal Corporation: "Dear Sir/Madam, I wish to bring to your notice that garbage has not been collected in our street for the past ten days." Identify the type of letter. (PYQ-style)

  1. Formal letter (complaint to a public authority)
  2. Semi-formal letter
  3. Demi-official letter
  4. Office Memorandum
Answer

Answer: (a) - A citizen writing to an official about a public problem uses the formal register: "Dear Sir/Madam," with "Yours faithfully,".

ID4. Identify the letter type: "We are a stationery shop in Salem and wish to stock your notebooks. Please send us your latest price list, trade discount and terms of delivery." (PYQ-style)

  1. Order letter
  2. Quotation
  3. Inquiry letter
  4. Sales letter
Answer

Answer: (c) - A prospective buyer asks for information (prices, discount, terms) before buying - an inquiry (enquiry) letter.

ID5. Identify the letter type: "Thank you for your inquiry of 5 October 2026. We are pleased to quote Rs. 45 per notebook (200 pages), inclusive of GST, with delivery within 10 days. This offer is valid for 30 days." (PYQ-style)

  1. Inquiry letter
  2. Order letter
  3. Adjustment letter
  4. Quotation
Answer

Answer: (d) - The seller's reply to an inquiry, stating prices and terms with a validity period (firm offer), is a quotation.

ID6. Identify the letter type: "Please supply the following as per your quotation No. KPM/Q/214 dated 8 October 2026: 500 notebooks (200 pages) at Rs. 45 each. Payment will be made within 30 days of delivery." (PYQ-style)

  1. Order letter
  2. Quotation
  3. Inquiry letter
  4. Credit letter
Answer

Answer: (a) - The buyer places an order, quoting the seller's quotation, the quantity, price and payment terms.

ID7. Identify the MOST suitable letter type: "Of the 50 cartons of floor tiles received on 14 October 2026, six were broken in transit. We therefore claim replacement of the six cartons or a credit of Rs. 4,800." (PYQ-style)

  1. Complaint letter
  2. Claim letter
  3. Adjustment letter
  4. Collection letter
Answer

Answer: (b) - The buyer demands a specific remedy (replacement or credit); a complaint letter only reports the problem.

ID8. Identify the letter type: "We are sorry that six cartons of tiles in our consignment of 12 October 2026 reached you broken. Replacements have been dispatched today, free of cost." (PYQ-style)

  1. Claim letter
  2. Sales letter
  3. Adjustment letter
  4. Complaint letter
Answer

Answer: (c) - It is the seller's reply that settles the buyer's claim (here a full grant) - an adjustment letter.

ID9. Identify the letter type: "This is our second reminder. Our invoice No. 318 for Rs. 36,500, due on 30 August 2026, is still unpaid. Please send your payment within seven days." (PYQ-style)

  1. Collection letter
  2. Order letter
  3. Credit letter
  4. Circular letter
Answer

Answer: (a) - The seller asks for an overdue payment; this is the firmer second reminder of the collection series (first reminder → second reminder → final notice).

ID10. A firm posts the same letter to 400 customers and suppliers: "We are pleased to inform you that we have opened a new branch at Gandhipuram, Coimbatore, from 1 November 2026." Identify the letter type. (PYQ-style)

  1. Sales letter
  2. Inquiry letter
  3. Office Order
  4. Circular letter
Answer

Answer: (d) - The same information (a new branch) sent to many persons at once is a (business) circular letter.

ID11. Identify the letter type: "Tired of power cuts? Our new home inverter keeps your fans and lights running for up to four hours. Order before 30 November 2026 and get free installation." (PYQ-style)

  1. Circular letter
  2. Quotation
  3. Sales letter
  4. Order letter
Answer

Answer: (c) - It catches Attention, builds Interest and Desire, and urges Action (AIDA) to persuade the reader to buy - a sales letter.

ID12. Identify the letter type: "I request you to stop payment of cheque No. 452178 dated 10 October 2026 for Rs. 25,000, drawn in favour of Sri Murugan Stationers, as the cheque has been lost." (PYQ-style)

  1. Request for a Letter of Credit
  2. Stop-payment letter
  3. Cheque dishonour notice
  4. Bank reference letter
Answer

Answer: (b) - The account holder instructs the bank not to pay a cheque, giving its number, date, amount and payee.

ID13. An importer writes to its bank: "Please open an irrevocable Letter of Credit for USD 40,000 in favour of Island Spice Exports, Colombo, valid for 90 days, payable against the bill of lading, invoice and certificate of origin." Identify the letter type. (PYQ-style)

  1. Stop-payment letter
  2. Bank reference letter
  3. Proforma invoice
  4. Request (application) for a Letter of Credit
Answer

Answer: (d) - The importer (applicant) asks its bank to issue an L/C - the bank's undertaking to pay the exporter (beneficiary) when the stated documents are presented.

ID14. Identify the government communication: "The undersigned is directed to say that all Ministries/Departments should send their budget estimates for 2027-28 to this Ministry by 15 November 2026." It has no salutation and no complimentary close. (PYQ-style)

  1. Office Memorandum
  2. Demi-official letter
  3. Office Order
  4. Notification
Answer

Answer: (a) - Third person ("The undersigned is directed ..."), no salutation or close, addressed to Ministries/Departments - an Office Memorandum (O.M.).

ID15. Identify the communication: "D.O. No. 45/Edn/2026 … Dear Thiru Kumar, I would like to draw your personal attention to the delay in the supply of free textbooks to schools in your district." (PYQ-style)

  1. Official letter
  2. Office Memorandum
  3. Demi-official (D.O.) letter
  4. Circular
Answer

Answer: (c) - Officer to officer, first person singular, seeking personal attention, "Dear Thiru Kumar," (closed with "Yours sincerely,") and a D.O. No. - a demi-official letter.

ID16. Identify the communication: "Thiru R. Suresh, Assistant, is posted to Section C with immediate effect. Selvi M. Divya, Assistant, will attend to the work of Section A in addition to her own duties." (PYQ-style)

  1. Notification
  2. Office Order
  3. Office Memorandum
  4. Circular letter
Answer

Answer: (b) - Internal administrative instructions (postings and distribution of work within the office) are issued as an Office Order.

ID17. A communication to be published in the Tamil Nadu Government Gazette reads: "Thiru S. Arun, Deputy Collector, is appointed as District Revenue Officer, Salem." Identify it. (PYQ-style)

  1. Notification
  2. Office Order
  3. Resolution
  4. Endorsement
Answer

Answer: (a) - Rules, orders, appointments and promotions published in the Official Gazette are Notifications; a Resolution announces policy decisions or the appointment of committees.

ID18. At the foot of a letter from the District Collector, Erode, this line appears: "Copy forwarded to the Tahsildar, Erode, for information and necessary action." Identify this form of communication. (PYQ-style)

  1. Office Order
  2. Notification
  3. Demi-official letter
  4. Endorsement
Answer

Answer: (d) - A note at the foot of a communication forwarding copies to others "for information / necessary action" is an endorsement.

ID19. Identify the MOST suitable letter type: "Through: The Superintendent, Accounts Section. Sir, I, K. Anitha, Junior Assistant, respectfully submit that my annual increment due in April 2026 has not been sanctioned so far." (PYQ-style)

  1. Petition
  2. Office Memorandum
  3. Representation (through proper channel)
  4. Letter to the editor
Answer

Answer: (c) - An employee's formal request to a higher authority about a service grievance, sent through the immediate superior, is a representation; a petition is usually a collective request, often signed by many people.

ID20. A letter to the District Collector, signed by 85 residents, begins: "We, the undersigned residents of Gandhi Nagar, Vellore, humbly request that a bus stop be provided near our colony." Identify the letter type. (PYQ-style)

  1. Representation by an employee
  2. Petition
  3. Circular letter
  4. Letter to the editor
Answer

Answer: (b) - A formal request to an authority signed by many people ("We, the undersigned ...") is a petition.

16.5 Error Detection (15)

Each snippet contains exactly ONE error. In the snippets, " / " marks a new line.

ED1. Find the error and correct it. (Letter to the Branch Manager of a bank; the manager's name is not known.) "Dear Sir/Madam, … Yours sincerely, / R. Karthik" (PYQ-style)

Answer

Answer: Correct form: "Dear Sir/Madam, … Yours faithfully, / R. Karthik". Rule: "Dear Sir/Madam," (reader not named) pairs with "Yours faithfully,"; "Yours sincerely," goes with a named reader ("Dear Mr. Raman,").

ED2. Find the error and correct it. "Your's faithfully, / S. Meena" (PYQ-style)

Answer

Answer: Correct form: "Yours faithfully,". Rule: "Yours" is a possessive pronoun (like hers, ours, its) and never takes an apostrophe.

ED3. Find the error and correct it. "Yours Faithfully, / V. Lakshmi" (PYQ-style)

Answer

Answer: Correct form: "Yours faithfully,". Rule: only the first word of the complimentary close takes a capital letter (Yours faithfully, / Yours sincerely,), and a comma follows it.

ED4. Find the error and correct it. "Dear Mr. Raman / I am writing to thank you for the guidance you gave my son during his college admission." (PYQ-style)

Answer

Answer: Correct form: "Dear Mr. Raman,". Rule: a comma follows every salutation (and every complimentary close).

ED5. Find the error and correct it. (Date line of a formal letter) "12/10/2026" (PYQ-style)

Answer

Answer: Correct form: "12 October 2026". Rule: all-numeric dates are ambiguous (12 October in India and Britain, but December 10 in the USA); write the month in words. "12th October, 2026" is an acceptable traditional Indian style.

ED6. Find the error and correct it. (Letter to the Controller of Examinations) "I'm writing to request a duplicate mark sheet." (PYQ-style)

Answer

Answer: Correct form: "I am writing to request a duplicate mark sheet." Rule: no contractions (I'm, don't, can't) in formal letters; they are allowed only in informal letters.

ED7. Find the error and correct it. (Letter to a bank) "I request you to do the needful." (PYQ-style)

Answer

Answer: Correct form: "I request you to take the necessary action." (Better still, name the action: "I request you to issue a duplicate passbook.") Rule: "do the needful" is outdated; state clearly what you want done.

ED8. Find the error and correct it. "Please revert back to me by 20 October 2026." (PYQ-style)

Answer

Answer: Correct form: "Please reply by 20 October 2026." Rule: "revert back" is outdated and redundant; use "reply" or "respond".

ED9. Find the error and correct it. "I would like to meet you to discuss about the delay in the supply of books." (PYQ-style)

Answer

Answer: Correct form: "I would like to meet you to discuss the delay in the supply of books." Rule: "discuss" takes a direct object without "about" (but "a discussion about" is correct).

ED10. Find the error and correct it. "Subject: I am writing this letter to request you to repair the street lights in K.K. Nagar." (PYQ-style)

Answer

Answer: Correct form: "Subject: Request for repair of street lights in K.K. Nagar". Rule: the subject line is a short noun phrase with no full stop, not a full sentence.

ED11. Find the error and correct it. (Opening lines of a full-block formal letter) "R. Karthik, / 12, Gandhi Road, / K.K. Nagar, / Madurai - 625 020" (PYQ-style)

Answer

Answer: Correct form: "12, Gandhi Road, / K.K. Nagar, / Madurai - 625 020". Rule: only the sender's address goes at the top; the name is written below the complimentary close ("Yours faithfully, / R. Karthik"). (The From-To school format, where the name follows "From", is an accepted variant.)

ED12. Find the error and correct it. (End of a formal letter) "Encl.: 1. Copy of the receipt / Yours faithfully, / R. Karthik" (PYQ-style)

Answer

Answer: Correct form: "Yours faithfully, / R. Karthik / Encl.: 1. Copy of the receipt". Rule: the enclosure line comes after the name (signature block), at the left margin; "Copy to" (if any) comes after "Encl.".

ED13. Find the error and correct it. (Letter to a company) "Thanks a lot for your prompt reply to my inquiry." (PYQ-style)

Answer

Answer: Correct form: "Thank you for your prompt reply to my inquiry." Rule: "Thanks a lot" is conversational; formal letters use "Thank you" or "I am grateful".

ED14. Find the error and correct it. (Inside address) "To / M/s. Priya Raman, / Branch Manager, / Southern Commercial Bank, / Coimbatore - 641 002" (PYQ-style)

Answer

Answer: Correct form: "Ms. Priya Raman,". Rule: "M/s." (Messrs) is used only before the name of a firm, e.g. "M/s. Kaveri Traders"; before a person's name use Mr./Ms./Mrs./Dr. (Tamil Nadu Government usage: Thiru/Tmt./Selvi).

ED15. Find the error and correct it. "Please find enclosed herewith a copy of my transfer certificate." (PYQ-style)

Answer

Answer: Correct form: "Please find enclosed a copy of my transfer certificate." or "I enclose a copy of my transfer certificate." Rule: "enclosed" already means "sent with this letter", so "herewith" is redundant.

16.6 Arrange the Parts of a Letter (10)

EXAM TIP: Fix the two ends first - the sender's address (or letterhead / File No.) opens the letter and the name (or Encl.) closes it - then eliminate options. Inside a part, " / " marks a new line.

AR1. Arrange the parts of a formal letter of complaint (body omitted) in the correct order. (PYQ-style)

  • P: Dear Sir/Madam,
  • Q: 12 October 2026
  • R: Subject: Complaint regarding irregular garbage collection in K.K. Nagar
  • S: 12, Gandhi Road, / K.K. Nagar, / Madurai - 625 020
  • T: Yours faithfully, / R. Karthik
  • U: To / The Commissioner, / Madurai City Municipal Corporation, / Madurai - 625 001
  1. Q S U R P T
  2. S Q R U P T
  3. S Q U R P T
  4. S U Q P R T
Answer

Answer: (c) - Sender's address → date → "To" + receiver → subject → salutation → (body) → close and name.

AR2. Arrange the parts of an informal letter in the correct order. (PYQ-style)

  • P: Your loving friend, / Kavya
  • Q: Dear Priya,
  • R: 5, Park Street, / Salem - 636 007
  • S: 3 October 2026
  • T: I was so happy to read about your prize in the essay competition! … (body)
  1. R S Q T P
  2. S R Q T P
  3. R Q S T P
  4. Q R S T P
Answer

Answer: (a) - Informal layout: short address → date → salutation → body → close + first name. There is no subject line and no receiver's address.

AR3. Arrange the parts of a semi-formal letter to a landlord (body omitted) in the correct order. (PYQ-style)

  • P: Yours sincerely, / Arun Kumar
  • Q: Dear Mr. Raman,
  • R: 14 October 2026
  • S: 8, Lake View Road, / Tiruchirappalli - 620 017
  • T: Mr. S. Raman, / 22, Temple Street, / Tiruchirappalli - 620 002
  • U: Subject: Repair of the leaking water tank
  1. S R U T Q P
  2. R S T U Q P
  3. S T R U Q P
  4. S R T U Q P
Answer

Answer: (d) - Sender's address → date → receiver's name and address → subject (optional in semi-formal letters) → "Dear Mr. Raman," → (body) → "Yours sincerely," + full name.

AR4. Arrange the parts of a business order letter (body omitted) in the correct order. (PYQ-style)

  • P: Ref. No. SMS/PUR/112
  • Q: SRI MURUGAN STATIONERS (letterhead with address, phone, email and GSTIN)
  • R: Subject: Order for 500 notebooks
  • S: Dear Sir/Madam,
  • T: The Sales Manager, / Kaveri Paper Mills, / Sivakasi - 626 123
  • U: 15 October 2026
  • V: Yours faithfully, / For SRI MURUGAN STATIONERS / S. Senthil / Proprietor
  1. Q T P U R S V
  2. Q P U T R S V
  3. P Q U T R S V
  4. Q P U R T S V
Answer

Answer: (b) - Letterhead → Ref. No. → date → inside address → subject → salutation → (body) → close, "For FIRM NAME", name and designation.

AR5. Arrange the parts of a letter to the editor in the correct order. (PYQ-style)

  • P: To / The Editor, / The Hindu, / Chennai
  • Q: Subject: Poor condition of roads in Velachery
  • R: Sir,
  • S: 10, Bharathi Street, / Velachery, / Chennai - 600 042
  • T: Yours faithfully, / V. Lakshmi
  • U: 20 October 2026
  • V: Through the columns of your esteemed newspaper, I wish to draw the attention of the authorities to … (body)
  1. S U P Q R V T
  2. U S P Q R V T
  3. S U Q P R V T
  4. S P U Q R V T
Answer

Answer: (a) - Sender's address → date → "To The Editor" → subject → "Sir," → body → "Yours faithfully," + name.

AR6. Arrange the parts of a letter to a bank (body omitted) in the correct order. (PYQ-style)

  • P: To / The Branch Manager, / Southern Commercial Bank, / Anna Nagar Branch, / Chennai - 600 040
  • Q: Subject: Change of address in Savings Bank Account No. XXXXXXXX4521
  • R: Dear Sir/Madam,
  • S: 18, Nehru Street, / Anna Nagar, / Chennai - 600 040
  • T: 16 October 2026
  • U: Yours faithfully, / M. Divya
  • V: Encl.: 1. Self-attested copy of Aadhaar card
  1. S T P Q R V U
  2. T S P Q R U V
  3. S T P Q R U V
  4. S T Q P R U V
Answer

Answer: (c) - Formal layout: sender's address → date → receiver → subject → salutation → (body) → close + name → Encl. (after the name; only a "Copy to" line, if any, follows it). Note the masked account number.

AR7. Arrange the parts of an Office Memorandum in the correct order. (PYQ-style)

  • P: OFFICE MEMORANDUM
  • Q: No. 12/4/2026-Admn.
  • R: The undersigned is directed to say that … (body)
  • S: Government of India / Ministry of Rural Development
  • T: (R. Vignesh) / Under Secretary to the Government of India
  • U: Subject: Submission of monthly progress reports
  • V: New Delhi, 20 October 2026
  1. Q S V U R P T
  2. Q S V P U R T
  3. Q S V P R U T
  4. T Q S V P U R
Answer

Answer: (b) - File No. → Government/Ministry → place and date → title → subject → third-person body → signature with name and designation. An O.M. has no salutation and no complimentary close.

AR8. Arrange the parts of a demi-official (D.O.) letter in the correct order. (PYQ-style)

  • P: Yours sincerely,
  • Q: Dear Thiru Kumar,
  • R: D.O. No. 45/Edn/2026
  • S: I am writing to draw your personal attention to the delay in … (body)
  • T: 22 October 2026
  • U: (S. Arun)
  1. Q R T S P U
  2. R T S Q P U
  3. R T Q S U P
  4. R T Q S P U
Answer

Answer: (d) - D.O. No. at the top → date → "Dear Thiru Kumar," → first-person body → "Yours sincerely," → name (signature).

AR9. The sender's address and the date of a job application are already written. Arrange the remaining parts in the correct order. (PYQ-style)

  • P: Yours faithfully, / S. Vignesh
  • Q: Reference: Your advertisement in The Hindu dated 18 October 2026
  • R: To / The Manager (HR), / Kaveri Paper Mills, / Sivakasi - 626 123
  • S: Encl.: 1. Resume 2. Copies of certificates
  • T: Dear Sir/Madam,
  • U: Subject: Application for the post of Junior Accountant
  • V: In response to your advertisement, I wish to apply for the post of Junior Accountant. I hold a B.Com. degree … (body)
  1. R U Q T V P S
  2. R U T Q V P S
  3. U R Q T V P S
  4. R U Q T V S P
Answer

Answer: (a) - Receiver → subject → reference (the advertisement) → salutation → body → close + name → Encl.

AR10. Arrange the letters and documents of a business transaction in the order in which they are normally exchanged. (PYQ-style)

  • P: Order letter - the buyer places the order
  • Q: Quotation - the seller states prices and terms
  • R: Inquiry - the buyer asks for prices and terms
  • S: Payment - the buyer remits the amount due
  • T: Invoice - the seller dispatches the goods and bills the buyer
  • U: Receipt - the seller acknowledges the payment
  1. R P Q T S U
  2. R Q P T S U
  3. Q R P T S U
  4. R Q P T U S
Answer

Answer: (b) - Inquiry → Quotation → Order → Invoice (with the goods) → Payment → Receipt. A complaint and an adjustment come in between only if something goes wrong (see Section 5).

16.7 Short-Answer Questions (10)

SA1. Distinguish a demi-official (D.O.) letter from an official letter. (PYQ-style)

Answer

An official letter goes from a Government office to other governments, bodies, officials or the public; it is impersonal, begins "I am directed to ...", uses "Sir/Madam" with "Yours faithfully" and is signed with the designation. A D.O. letter goes from one officer to another by name; it is personal and friendly, written in the first person singular to draw personal attention or expedite a matter, uses "Dear Shri X," (Tamil Nadu: "Dear Thiru X,") with "Yours sincerely" and carries a D.O. No. at the top.

SA2. What is a subject line and where is it placed? (PYQ-style)

Answer

It is a short noun phrase that tells the reader at a glance what the letter is about, e.g. "Subject: Request for a duplicate mark sheet". It starts with "Subject:" (or "Sub:") and has no full stop. In our full-block layout it comes after the receiver's address and before the salutation; the traditional British layout places it after the salutation (accepted variant). Informal letters have no subject line.

SA3. Why should all-numeric dates such as 12/10/2026 be avoided in letters? (PYQ-style)

Answer

They are ambiguous: in India and Britain 12/10/2026 means 12 October 2026, but in the USA it means December 10, 2026. Writing the month in words ("12 October 2026") removes all doubt, which matters in official, legal and banking letters. "12th October, 2026" is an acceptable traditional Indian style; "October 12, 2026" is the American style.

SA4. What is meant by "through proper channel"? (PYQ-style)

Answer

An employee's application or representation to a higher authority is not sent directly; it is sent through the employee's immediate superior, who forwards it. A "Through: ..." line naming the immediate superior is written above or below the addressee, e.g. "Through: The Superintendent, Accounts Section".

SA5. When do you use "Yours faithfully" and when "Yours sincerely"? (PYQ-style)

Answer

"Yours faithfully," follows "Dear Sir/Madam,", "Sir,", "Madam," or "Respected Sir," - letters to officials, organisations, editors and unknown persons. "Yours sincerely," follows a named salutation such as "Dear Mr. Raman," - formal letters to a named person, all semi-formal letters and D.O. letters. Both take a capital Y, a small f/s, no apostrophe and a comma after.

SA6. Distinguish a complaint letter, a claim letter and an adjustment letter. (PYQ-style)

Answer

A complaint letter is written by the buyer to report a problem (defect, delay, shortage, wrong goods). A claim letter is written by the buyer to demand a specific remedy or compensation (refund, replacement, price reduction). An adjustment letter is the seller's reply that settles the complaint or claim - full grant, partial grant, or polite refusal with reasons.

SA7. What is an Office Memorandum (O.M.)? State its main features. (PYQ-style)

Answer

An O.M. is a communication between Ministries/Departments (also used with attached offices and employees). It is written in the third person ("The undersigned is directed to ..."), has no salutation and no complimentary close, and is signed with the officer's name and designation.

SA8. What is a Letter of Credit (L/C)? Name its parties. (PYQ-style)

Answer

A Letter of Credit is a bank's written undertaking, issued at the request of the buyer/importer (applicant), to pay the seller/exporter (beneficiary) when specified documents are presented; it is used mainly in foreign trade. Parties: applicant, issuing bank, beneficiary and advising / confirming bank.

SA9. What does "Encl." mean, and where is it written? (PYQ-style)

Answer

"Encl." (enclosure) lists the documents sent with the letter, e.g. "Encl.: 1. Copy of the bill". It is written at the left margin after the name (signature block) and before "Copy to". In the body, write "Please find enclosed ..." or "I enclose ..."; avoid "enclosed herewith".

SA10. What should you write as the address and the name when an exam question does not give them? (PYQ-style)

Answer

Write "Examination Hall" as the address and sign as "ABC" or "XYZ"; never reveal your real identity in an exam answer. If the question gives a name and address, use exactly those.

16.8 Letter-Writing Questions (10)

EXAM TIP: Each answer below is an outline, not a full letter (for complete model letters see Section 10). Before you write, fix four things: the layout, the subject line, the salutation-close pair and a three-paragraph plan.

LW1. You are R. Karthik of 12, Gandhi Road, K.K. Nagar, Madurai - 625 020. Write a letter to the Commissioner, Madurai City Municipal Corporation, complaining about irregular garbage collection and blocked drains in your street. (About 150 words) (PYQ-style)

Answer

Model answer outline Formal

Format checklist: Sender's address (12, Gandhi Road, K.K. Nagar, Madurai - 625 020; no name at the top) → Date (e.g. 12 October 2026) → To / The Commissioner, / Madurai City Municipal Corporation, / Madurai - 625 001 → Subject → Salutation → Body (3 paragraphs) → Close → R. Karthik

Subject line: Subject: Complaint regarding irregular garbage collection and blocked drains in K.K. Nagar

Salutation and close: "Dear Sir/Madam," … "Yours faithfully,"

Paragraph plan:

  • Opening: State the purpose - to bring to the Commissioner's notice the irregular garbage collection and blocked drains in Gandhi Road, K.K. Nagar.
  • Main: Give facts - garbage not cleared for about ten days; drains overflowing; foul smell and mosquitoes; risk of dengue and other diseases; earlier complaints to the ward office not acted upon.
  • Closing: Request immediate clearance, desilting of the drains and regular collection; thank the Commissioner in the last sentence.

Useful phrases:

  • I wish to bring to your notice the ...
  • The residents of our street are facing great hardship because ...
  • Despite repeated complaints to the ward office, no action has been taken.
  • This poses a serious threat to public health.
  • I request you to take immediate action to ...
  • Thank you for your time and attention.

LW2. You are V. Lakshmi of 10, Bharathi Street, Thanjavur - 613 001. Write a letter to the Editor, The Hindu, Chennai, about the increasing use of single-use plastic bags in your town and its effects. (Not exceeding 200 words) (PYQ-style)

Answer

Model answer outline Formal

Format checklist: Sender's address → Date → To / The Editor, / The Hindu, / Chennai → Subject → Salutation → Body (3-4 paragraphs) → Close → V. Lakshmi

Subject line: Subject: Increasing use of plastic bags in Thanjavur

Salutation and close: "Sir," (or "Dear Sir/Madam,") … "Yours faithfully,"

Paragraph plan:

  • Opening: Through the columns of the newspaper, draw the attention of the public and the authorities to the problem.
  • Main: Describe the problem - plastic bags in markets and shops; blocked drains and waterlogging; cattle eating plastic; burning of plastic waste; causes such as convenience and weak enforcement.
  • Closing: Suggest remedies - strict enforcement, cloth and jute bags, awareness drives in schools; hope that the letter will create awareness.

Useful phrases:

  • Through the columns of your esteemed newspaper, I wish to draw the attention of ...
  • It is distressing to note that ...
  • This has led to ...
  • The authorities concerned should ...
  • Citizens, too, must ...
  • I hope this letter will create awareness among the public.

LW3. You are M. Divya of 18, Nehru Street, Anna Nagar, Chennai - 600 040, and hold a savings bank account with Southern Commercial Bank, Anna Nagar Branch, Chennai - 600 040. A cheque you issued to Sri Murugan Stationers has been lost. Write a letter to the Branch Manager requesting stop payment. (About 120 words) (PYQ-style)

Answer

Model answer outline Formal

Format checklist: Sender's address → Date → To / The Branch Manager, / Southern Commercial Bank, / Anna Nagar Branch, / Chennai - 600 040 → Subject → Salutation → Body (3 short paragraphs) → Close → M. Divya / SB Account No. XXXXXXXX4521

Subject line: Subject: Request to stop payment of cheque No. 452178

Salutation and close: "Dear Sir/Madam," … "Yours faithfully,"

Paragraph plan:

  • Opening: State that you hold Savings Bank Account No. XXXXXXXX4521 in the branch and request stop payment of a cheque.
  • Main: Give the cheque details - number, date, amount and payee (e.g. No. 452178 dated 10 October 2026 for Rs. 25,000 in favour of Sri Murugan Stationers); it was lost and has not been presented.
  • Closing: Request that the instruction be recorded at once and confirmed; authorise the bank to debit the stop-payment charges, if any.

Useful phrases:

  • I request you to stop payment of the following cheque issued by me.
  • The cheque has been lost and has not been presented for payment so far.
  • Please record this instruction immediately and confirm it in writing.
  • You may debit the charges, if any, to my account.
  • I shall be grateful for your prompt action.

MUST REMEMBER: Never write a PIN, OTP, CVV or password in a bank letter; mask the account number.

LW4. You are S. Senthil, Proprietor, Sri Murugan Stationers, 45, East Masi Street, Madurai - 625 001. Write a letter to Kaveri Paper Mills, Sivakasi - 626 123, placing an order for notebooks and A4 paper with reference to their quotation No. KPM/Q/214 dated 8 October 2026. (About 150 words) (PYQ-style)

Answer

Model answer outline Formal

Format checklist: Letterhead (SRI MURUGAN STATIONERS, address, phone, email, GSTIN) → Ref. No. → Date → Inside address (The Sales Manager, / Kaveri Paper Mills, / Sivakasi - 626 123) → Subject → Salutation → Body (with a list of items) → Close → For SRI MURUGAN STATIONERS → S. Senthil → Proprietor

Subject line: Subject: Order for notebooks and A4 paper - your quotation No. KPM/Q/214

Salutation and close: "Dear Sir/Madam," … "Yours faithfully,"

Paragraph plan:

  • Opening: Refer to the quotation (number and date) and place the order.
  • Main: List the items - description, quantity, rate and amount (e.g. 500 notebooks of 200 pages at Rs. 45 = Rs. 22,500; 50 reams of A4 paper at Rs. 260 = Rs. 13,000); state the delivery date, mode of transport, packing and payment terms (e.g. within 30 days of the invoice).
  • Closing: Ask for an acknowledgement of the order and an invoice showing the GSTIN; express hope of prompt delivery.

Useful phrases:

  • With reference to your quotation No. ... dated ..., we are pleased to place an order for the following items.
  • Please supply the goods as per the specifications given below.
  • The goods should reach us not later than ...
  • Please pack the goods carefully to avoid damage in transit.
  • Payment will be made within 30 days of receipt of your invoice.
  • Kindly acknowledge receipt of this order.

LW5. You have seen an advertisement in The Hindu for the post of Junior Accountant in Kaveri Paper Mills, Sivakasi - 626 123. Write a letter of application. Use "Examination Hall" as your address and sign as ABC. (About 200 words) (PYQ-style)

Answer

Model answer outline Formal

Format checklist: Examination Hall → Date → To / The Manager (HR), / Kaveri Paper Mills, / Sivakasi - 626 123 → Subject → Reference (the advertisement) → Salutation → Body (3 paragraphs) → Close → ABC → Encl.: 1. Resume 2. Copies of certificates

Subject line: Subject: Application for the post of Junior Accountant

Salutation and close: "Dear Sir/Madam," … "Yours faithfully,"

Paragraph plan:

  • Opening: Name the post and where you saw it advertised (The Hindu, with the date); the same advertisement is cited in the Reference line above the salutation.
  • Main: Give your qualifications (e.g. B.Com.), skills (accounting software, GST returns, spreadsheets), experience, and why you suit the post.
  • Closing: Mention the enclosed resume and certificates, your availability for an interview, and thank the reader.

Useful phrases:

  • In response to your advertisement in The Hindu dated ..., I wish to apply for the post of ...
  • I hold a B.Com. degree from ...
  • I have two years' experience as ...
  • I am confident that my skills will be useful to your organisation.
  • I enclose my resume and copies of my certificates.
  • I shall be glad to attend an interview at your convenience.

LW6. You are S. Kavya of Class XII-A, Sri Vidya Higher Secondary School, Vellore, living at 14, Kamarajar Street, Sathuvachari, Vellore - 632 009. Write a letter to your Principal requesting leave for three days to attend your sister's wedding. (About 100 words) (PYQ-style)

Answer

Model answer outline Formal

Format checklist: Sender's address → Date → To / The Principal, / Sri Vidya Higher Secondary School, / Vellore → Subject → Salutation → Body (2-3 short paragraphs) → Close → S. Kavya / Class XII-A

Subject line: Subject: Request for leave from 15 to 17 October 2026

Salutation and close: "Respected Sir," or "Respected Madam," … "Yours faithfully," (school students may also use the traditional "Yours obediently,")

Paragraph plan:

  • Opening: Introduce yourself (name, class, section) and request leave for three days, giving the exact dates.
  • Main: Give the reason - your elder sister's wedding at Thanjavur; you must travel with your family.
  • Closing: Promise to complete the lessons and assignments you miss; request the Principal to grant leave; thank.

Useful phrases:

  • I am S. Kavya, a student of Class XII-A.
  • I request you to grant me leave for three days, from ... to ...
  • ... as I have to attend my sister's wedding at ...
  • I shall complete the lessons and assignments that I miss.
  • I shall be grateful if you grant me leave for these days.
  • Thank you for your kind consideration.

LW7. Write a letter to your friend Priya inviting her to the house-warming ceremony of your family's new house in Coimbatore. Use "Examination Hall" as your address and sign as ABC. (About 120 words) (PYQ-style)

Answer

Model answer outline Informal

Format checklist: Examination Hall (short sender's address) → Date → Salutation → Body (3 short paragraphs) → Close → ABC. No receiver's address.

Subject line: None - an informal letter has no subject line.

Salutation and close: "Dear Priya," … "Your loving friend," (or "With love,")

Paragraph plan:

  • Opening: Greet her warmly; ask about her and her family.
  • Main: Share the news - your family's new house; the date, time and place of the function (e.g. 8 November 2026, 7 a.m., at the new house in Coimbatore); what is planned; how to reach.
  • Closing: Insist that she come (a day early if possible); send regards to her parents; say you look forward to meeting her.

Useful phrases:

  • How are you and everyone at home?
  • I'm delighted to tell you that ...
  • We'd love to have you with us on ...
  • Please do come a day early so that we can spend some time together.
  • Convey my regards to your parents.
  • Looking forward to seeing you soon.

LW8. You are Arun Kumar, a tenant living at 8, Lake View Road, Tiruchirappalli - 620 017. Write a letter to your landlord, Mr. S. Raman, 22, Temple Street, Tiruchirappalli - 620 002, requesting him to repair the leaking water tank of the house. (About 150 words) (PYQ-style)

Answer

Model answer outline Semi-formal

Format checklist: Sender's address → Date → Mr. S. Raman, / 22, Temple Street, / Tiruchirappalli - 620 002 → Subject (optional) → Salutation → Body (3 paragraphs) → Close → Arun Kumar (full name)

Subject line: Subject: Repair of the leaking water tank (optional in a semi-formal letter)

Salutation and close: "Dear Mr. Raman," … "Yours sincerely,"

Paragraph plan:

  • Opening: Greet him politely and state the purpose - the water tank of the house is leaking.
  • Main: Give details - since when (e.g. 1 October 2026); water wastage, damp walls and damage to the ceiling; a plumber's opinion that the tank needs repair.
  • Closing: Request repairs within a week; offer to arrange a plumber if he approves; thank him for his cooperation.

Useful phrases:

  • I am writing to inform you that ...
  • The problem has become worse over the past week.
  • I would be grateful if you could arrange for the repairs as soon as possible.
  • If you wish, I can arrange for a plumber with your approval.
  • Thank you for your understanding.
  • I look forward to hearing from you.

LW9. You are R. Meena, Customer Care Manager, Kaveri Home Appliances, 12, Avinashi Road, Coimbatore - 641 018. A customer, Ms. Divya Krishnan, has complained that the washing machine she bought was delivered ten days late with a damaged panel. Write a letter of apology offering a suitable remedy. (About 150 words) (PYQ-style)

Answer

Model answer outline Formal

Format checklist: Letterhead (KAVERI HOME APPLIANCES, address, phone, email, GSTIN) → Ref. No. → Date → Inside address (Ms. Divya Krishnan and her address) → Subject → Salutation → Body (3 paragraphs) → Close → R. Meena → Customer Care Manager

Subject line: Subject: Apology for the delayed delivery and damaged panel of your washing machine

Salutation and close: "Dear Ms. Krishnan," … "Yours sincerely," (the customer is named)

Paragraph plan:

  • Opening: Thank her for her letter and apologise sincerely for the delay and the damage.
  • Main: Give a brief reason without excuses (e.g. a transport delay at the warehouse) and the remedy - a technician will replace the damaged panel free of cost within 48 hours, or the machine will be replaced if she prefers; add a goodwill gesture (e.g. one free service visit). This is an adjustment letter (full grant).
  • Closing: Mention the steps taken to prevent a repeat; give a contact person and phone number; thank her for her patience.

Useful phrases:

  • Thank you for bringing this matter to our notice.
  • We sincerely apologise for the inconvenience caused to you.
  • We have arranged for ... free of cost.
  • We have taken steps to ensure that this does not happen again.
  • Please contact me at ... if you need any further help.
  • We value your trust in our products.

LW10. You are K. Anitha, Junior Assistant, Accounts Section, Office of the District Collector, Salem - 636 001. Your annual increment due on 1 April 2026 has not been sanctioned. Write a representation to the District Revenue Officer, Salem, through proper channel. (About 150 words) (PYQ-style)

Answer

Model answer outline Formal

Format checklist: Sender's address (Accounts Section, / Office of the District Collector, / Salem - 636 001) → Date → To / The District Revenue Officer, / Office of the District Collector, / Salem - 636 001 → Through: The Superintendent, Accounts Section (below or above the addressee) → Subject → Salutation → Body (3 paragraphs) → Close → K. Anitha → Junior Assistant

Subject line: Subject: Representation regarding non-sanction of annual increment due on 1 April 2026

Salutation and close: "Sir," (or "Respected Sir," / "Respected Madam,") … "Yours faithfully,"

Paragraph plan:

  • Opening: Introduce yourself (name, designation, section, date of joining) and state that you are submitting a representation about your increment.
  • Main: Give the facts - increment due on 1 April 2026; satisfactory service; no disciplinary proceedings pending; earlier requests (with dates) not answered; financial hardship.
  • Closing: Request sanction of the increment with arrears; thank the officer; keep a respectful tone throughout.

Useful phrases:

  • I respectfully submit the following for your kind consideration.
  • My annual increment, due on 1 April 2026, has not been sanctioned so far.
  • My service has been satisfactory, and no disciplinary proceedings are pending against me.
  • I request you to sanction the increment with arrears.
  • I shall be grateful for your kind consideration.

17 Final Revision Sheet

Last-minute revision: five tables, ten golden rules and one box. Every entry follows the house conventions used throughout this book. For explanations and model letters, go back to the section named in each introduction line.

17.1 50 Most Important Letter-Writing Terms

Parts of a letter (1-16), registers and letter types (17-19), government correspondence (20-32), business and banking (33-50). Details: Sections 1, 4, 6 and 7.

No.TermOne-line meaning
1LetterheadThe printed heading of a firm or office: name, address, phone and email (and GSTIN for a firm).
2Sender's addressThe writer's address at the top-left; the name is NOT written here but below the close.
3Date lineThe date of writing, in the style "12 October 2026".
4Ref. No.The sender's file or letter number, written above the date in business letters and used to trace correspondence.
5Inside addressThe receiver's name or designation and full address, written below "To".
6Attention line"Kind Attention: ..." - directs a letter addressed to a firm to a particular person or department.
7Subject line"Subject:" + a short noun phrase stating the purpose; before the salutation; no full stop ("Sub:" is accepted).
8Reference line"Reference: Your letter No. ... dated ..." - used only when referring to earlier correspondence.
9SalutationThe greeting before the body, e.g. "Dear Sir/Madam,"; always followed by a comma.
10Complimentary closeThe polite phrase before the signature, e.g. "Yours faithfully,".
11Signature blockSignature, name and, in official or business letters, designation.
12Enclosure (Encl.)A document sent with the letter, listed below the signature.
13Copy to / CCOther persons who receive a copy of the letter (CC = carbon copy or courtesy copy).
14Postscript (P.S.)A short note added below the signature; avoid it in formal letters.
15Full-block formatA layout in which every line starts at the left margin; used for all model letters in this book.
16RegisterThe level of formality of a letter: formal, semi-formal or informal.
17Formal letterA letter to an official, an organisation or an unknown person, in a fixed format and formal language.
18Semi-formal letterA letter to a known person (not close) on a personal or partly official matter: "Dear Mr. Raman," → "Yours sincerely,".
19Informal letterA personal letter to family or friends; no subject line and no receiver's address; contractions allowed.
20Official letterFrom a Government office to other governments, bodies, officials or the public; begins "I am directed to ..."; signed with designation.
21Demi-official (D.O.) letterAn officer-to-officer letter in a personal tone and the first person; D.O. No. at the top; "Dear Shri Kumar," → "Yours sincerely,".
22Office Memorandum (O.M.)Communication between Ministries/Departments; third person ("The undersigned is directed to ..."); no salutation, no close.
23Office OrderInternal administrative instructions: distribution of work, grant of leave, postings within the office.
24OrderUsed for financial sanctions, disciplinary matters, etc.
25NotificationPublication of rules, orders, appointments, promotions, etc. in the Official Gazette.
26ResolutionPublic announcement of important policy decisions or of the appointment of committees or commissions; published in the Gazette.
27CircularThe same information sent to many offices or persons.
28NoticeAn announcement displayed or published for the information of a group or the public; no salutation, no close.
29EndorsementGovernment: a note at the foot forwarding copies or returning a paper to the sender. Banking: a signature on the back of a cheque to transfer it.
30Government Order (G.O.)An order issued by a Secretariat department of the Tamil Nadu Government.
31ProceedingsThe format used by Heads of Departments and District officers in Tamil Nadu: Present, Rc. No., Dated, Sub, Ref, ORDER.
32Through proper channelAn employee's application to a higher authority, sent through the immediate superior (a "Through: ..." line).
33Inquiry / enquiry letterA letter asking for information such as prices, terms or availability; both spellings are accepted.
34QuotationThe seller's statement of prices, terms and conditions in reply to an inquiry; valid for a stated period (firm offer).
35TenderA formal (often sealed) offer in response to an invitation, usually from public bodies.
36Purchase Order (PO)A formal numbered document issued by the buyer; binding once accepted by the seller.
37Proforma invoiceA preliminary bill sent before supply, for advance payment or import formalities.
38Invoice (tax invoice)The seller's bill for goods supplied: quantity, price, taxes, total and due date.
39Complaint letterThe buyer reports a problem: defect, delay, shortage or wrong goods.
40Claim letterThe buyer demands a specific remedy: refund, replacement or price reduction.
41Adjustment letterThe seller's reply that settles a complaint or claim: full grant, partial grant or polite refusal with reasons.
42Credit noteIssued by the seller; reduces the amount the buyer owes (goods returned, overcharge, allowance).
43Debit noteIssued by the buyer when returning goods or claiming a deduction; a seller may also send one to charge an undercharged amount.
44Letter of Credit (L/C)A bank's written undertaking, issued at the buyer's request, to pay the seller when specified documents are presented.
45Bank referenceA bank's statement about its customer's account and general standing, given on request.
46Credit referenceInformation about creditworthiness from trade referees or credit information companies (e.g. CIBIL).
47Collection seriesFirst reminder (polite) → second reminder (firmer) → final notice (warns of legal action).
48Stop-paymentThe account holder's instruction to the bank not to pay a cheque; give the cheque number, date, amount and payee.
49E&OEErrors and Omissions Excepted - printed on invoices and similar documents so that errors can be corrected later.
50M/s.Messrs - written before the name of a firm, e.g. "M/s. Kaveri Traders".

17.2 50 Most Useful Formal Phrases

Modern, correct phrases only: openings (1-15), requests (16-25), enclosures and references (26-30), complaints (31-35), business replies (36-43), government (44-45) and closings (46-50). Details: Section 8.

No.PhraseUse
1I am writing to inform you that ...Opening: giving information
2I am writing to request ...Opening: making a request
3I am writing to bring to your notice ...Opening: reporting a problem (complaint)
4I wish to draw your attention to ...Pointing out an issue to an authority

6With reference to your letter No. ... dated ...,Replying to earlier correspondence
7Thank you for your letter dated ...Opening a reply politely
8In response to your advertisement in The Hindu dated ..., I wish to apply for the post of ...Job application

13We acknowledge receipt of your letter dated ...Confirming that a letter has arrived

16I would be grateful if you could ...Polite request

20I request you to look into the matter and take suitable action.Complaint: asking for action

41This quotation is valid for 30 days from its date.Validity of a quotation (firm offer)

48Please do not hesitate to contact me if you need any further information.Offering further help
49I hope you will consider my request favourably.Closing a request
50I would be grateful for an early reply.Asking for a quick reply

17.3 25 Most Important Letter Types

Register, salutation-close pair and a key opening phrase for each type. Details: Sections 2-6 and 12.

No.Letter typeRegisterSalutation → CloseKey opening phrase
1Letter to a friendInformal"Dear Priya," → "Your loving friend,""I was delighted to receive your letter."
2Letter to a parentInformal"Dear Appa," → "Your loving son," / "Your loving daughter,""I am doing well here and hope everyone at home is fine."
3Letter to a teacher or neighbour (known person)Semi-formal"Dear Mr. Raman," → "Yours sincerely,""I am writing to thank you for ..."

23Circular (government)FormalNo salutation / no close"All Heads of Departments are requested to ..."
24NotificationFormalNo salutation / no close"In exercise of the powers conferred by section ... of the ... Act, ..."
25NoticeFormalNo salutation / no close"This is to inform all ... that ..."

17.4 20 Most Confusing Terms

The key difference in one cell. Full comparison tables: Section 15.

No.PairKey difference
1Yours faithfully vs Yours sincerelyFaithfully: after "Dear Sir/Madam,", "Sir," or "Respected Sir," (officials, organisations, editors, unknown persons). Sincerely: after "Dear Mr. Raman," (named person), in all semi-formal letters and in D.O. letters.
2Enquiry vs InquiryBritish tradition: enquiry = asking for information; inquiry = formal investigation. American English uses "inquiry" for both; "inquiry letter" and "enquiry letter" are both accepted.
3Quotation vs EstimateQuotation: firm prices, terms and conditions with a validity period. Estimate: approximate cost only.
4Quotation vs TenderQuotation: the seller's reply to a buyer's inquiry. Tender: a formal (often sealed) offer in response to an invitation, usually from public bodies.
5Order letter vs Purchase OrderOrder letter: a letter placing an order. Purchase Order: a formal numbered document issued by the buyer; binding once accepted by the seller.
6Proforma invoice vs InvoiceProforma invoice: a preliminary bill sent before supply (for advance payment or import formalities). Invoice: the bill for goods supplied - quantity, price, taxes, total and due date.
7Complaint letter vs Claim letterComplaint: reports a problem (defect, delay, shortage, wrong goods). Claim: demands a specific remedy (refund, replacement, price reduction).
8Claim letter vs Adjustment letterClaim: written by the buyer. Adjustment: the seller's reply that settles it (full grant, partial grant or polite refusal with reasons).
9Credit note vs Debit noteCredit note: issued by the seller; reduces what the buyer owes. Debit note: issued by the buyer when returning goods or claiming a deduction (a seller may also send one for an undercharge). Side note: under GST law both are issued by the supplier.
10Credit letter vs Letter of CreditCredit letter: a business letter about credit (opening a credit account, status inquiry, granting or refusing credit). Letter of Credit: a bank's undertaking to pay the seller against specified documents.
11Credit advice vs Debit adviceIntimation, usually from a bank, that an account has been credited (credit advice) or debited (debit advice).
12Bank reference vs Credit referenceBank reference: a bank's statement on its customer's account and general standing. Credit reference: creditworthiness information from trade referees or credit information companies (e.g. CIBIL).
13Stop-payment vs Cheque dishonourStop-payment: the account holder tells the bank not to pay a cheque. Dishonour: the bank returns the cheque unpaid with a return memo; the payee may then send a legal demand notice under Section 138 of the Negotiable Instruments Act, 1881, within 30 days of receiving the memo.
14FOB vs CIFFOB: the seller's cost and risk end when the goods are loaded on board the ship at the port of shipment. CIF: the seller pays cost, insurance and freight up to the destination port.
15Official letter vs D.O. letterOfficial: "Sir/Madam," → "Yours faithfully,"; begins "I am directed to ..."; signed with designation. D.O.: "Dear Shri Kumar," → "Yours sincerely,"; personal tone, first person; D.O. No. at the top.
16Office Memorandum vs Office OrderO.M.: communication between Ministries/Departments, in the third person. Office Order: internal instructions within an office (distribution of work, leave, postings).
17Office Order vs OrderOffice Order: internal administrative matters. Order: financial sanctions, disciplinary matters, etc.
18Notification vs ResolutionNotification: rules, orders, appointments and promotions published in the Gazette. Resolution: important policy decisions or the appointment of committees or commissions (also published in the Gazette).
19Circular vs NoticeCircular: the same information sent to many offices or persons. Notice: displayed or published for the information of a group or the public. Neither a government circular nor a notice has a salutation or a close.
20Endorsement (government) vs Endorsement (banking)Government: a note at the foot forwarding copies ("Copy forwarded to ... for information / necessary action") or returning a paper to the sender. Banking: a signature on the back of a cheque or other negotiable instrument to transfer it.

17.5 10 Letter Formats to Memorise

Read each line from left to right; every model letter in this book is in full-block format with one blank line between blocks. Details: Sections 6 and 9.

No.LetterOrder of parts
1Formal letterSender's address → Date → To → Receiver's designation and address → Subject → Reference (only if needed) → "Dear Sir/Madam," → Opening paragraph → Main paragraph(s) → Closing paragraph → "Yours faithfully," → Name → Encl. / Copy to (if any)
2Semi-formal letterSender's address → Date → Receiver's name and address → Subject (optional) → "Dear Mr./Mrs. Surname," → Body → "Yours sincerely," → Full name
3Informal letterSender's address (short) → Date → Salutation ("Dear Priya,") → Body → Close ("Your loving friend,") → First name (no subject line, no receiver's address)
4Business letterLetterhead (firm name in capitals, address, phone, email, GSTIN) → Ref. No. → Date → Inside address → Kind Attention (optional) → Subject → Salutation → Body → Complimentary close → "For FIRM NAME" (optional) → Name → Designation → Encl. → CC
5Letter to the EditorSender's address → Date → To → The Editor, newspaper, city → Subject → "Dear Sir/Madam," (or "Sir,") → Body (issue, facts, suggestions) → "Yours faithfully," → Name
6Job applicationSender's address → Date → To → Employer's designation and address → Subject: Application for the post of ... → "Dear Sir/Madam," → Body (post and advertisement, qualifications and experience, request for an interview) → "Yours faithfully," → Name → Encl.: CV and copies of certificates
7Bank letterSender's address → Date → To → The Branch Manager, bank and branch address → Subject (with the masked account number) → "Dear Sir/Madam," → Body (account details, request, reason) → "Yours faithfully," → Name (signature as in bank records) → Encl. (if any)
8D.O. letterSender's name, designation and office with D.O. No. (at the top) → Date → "Dear Shri Kumar," (Tamil Nadu: "Dear Thiru Kumar,") → Body (first person, personal tone) → "Yours sincerely," → Signature and name → Addressee's name and address (bottom-left)
9Office Memorandum (no salutation, no close)File No. → Government of India, Ministry / Department → Place and date → OFFICE MEMORANDUM → Subject → Body in the third person ("The undersigned is directed to ...") → Signature, name and designation → To: addressee(s) (bottom-left) → Copy to
10Leave letterSender's address → Date → To → The Principal / Head of Office, with address → Subject: Request for leave → "Respected Sir," (or "Dear Sir/Madam,") → Body (dates, reason, request) → "Yours faithfully," (school students may also use "Yours obediently,") → Name (with class or designation)

17.6 10 Golden Rules of Formal Letter Writing

  1. Exam identity rule. If the question gives no name or address, write "Examination Hall" as the address and sign as "ABC" (or "XYZ"); if the question gives them, use exactly those. Never reveal your real identity.
  2. Pair the salutation and the close. "Dear Sir/Madam," / "Sir," / "Respected Sir," → "Yours faithfully,"; "Dear Mr. Raman," and D.O. letters → "Yours sincerely,"; family and friends → "Your loving ...". Write "Yours" with a capital Y and no apostrophe, and put a comma after both the salutation and the close.
  3. Write a precise subject line before the salutation. "Subject:" + a short noun phrase with no full stop, e.g. "Subject: Complaint regarding irregular garbage collection in K.K. Nagar".
  4. Build a three-part body. Opening (why you are writing) → main (facts and details) → closing (the action you want).
  5. No contractions or slang. Also no exclamation marks, SMS language or emoji in a formal letter; use modern phrases, not outdated ones.
  6. Write the date as "12 October 2026". Avoid all-numeric dates such as 12/10/2026, which are ambiguous.
  7. One purpose per letter. Do not mix a complaint with an unrelated request; write a separate letter for each.
  8. Polite but firm tone. State facts, not anger; even a final reminder or a strong complaint stays courteous.
  9. Check names, designations and addresses. Use the receiver's exact designation, office, city and pin code ("Madurai - 625 001"), and spell every name correctly.
  10. End with a clear request or action. Say exactly what you want done and, where relevant, by when.

17.7 Final MUST REMEMBER Box

MUST REMEMBER: "Dear Sir/Madam," → "Yours faithfully," but "Dear Mr. Raman," → "Yours sincerely,"; the subject line comes before the salutation and has no full stop; O.M., Office Order, Notification, government Circular and Notice have no salutation and no close; the credit note is issued by the seller and reduces what the buyer owes; date every letter "12 October 2026"; no name in the question → "Examination Hall" and "ABC".

Letter Writing Master Notes — TNPSC General English. Model letters use the full-block exam format; where textbooks differ, the accepted variant is noted.
